edX help

This guide holds edX’s own help, gathered on 12 October 2026 from its two help centres: the one for learners and the one for organisations that buy edX for their staff. The organisations’ categories begin with “edX for Business”, so a learner can skip them. Every article came from edX’s own listing, with its title and text, so nothing depended on a page drawn in the browser. Udemy has its own guide in this collection; Coursera is not here, because its help is drawn in the browser and cannot be gathered.

The help text belongs to its publisher and is gathered here unchanged for accessible reading. This guide’s own arrangement and wording are available under CC BY-SA 4.0.

About Audit and Verified Courses

Does upgrading to the verified track extend my access to the course?

Upgrading to the verified track will give you access to all materials, including graded assignments, until the course end date.

Upgrading will extend your access to the course content but it will not change the course end date.

You’ll still need to pass the course before it ends in order to earn a certificate. After the course end date, as a verified learner, you will have access to archived course content and materials, as long it’s hosted on edX.org.

How can I get access after a course expires?

When you enroll in a course in the free audit track, you will have access for a limited time. You’ll find a message on the right side on the course homepage within the course displaying the date your access expires.

After the expiration date, you would need to pay and upgrade to the verified certificate track to regain access in this session, or enroll in a future session when the course is offered again.

Upgrading to the verified track will give you access to all materials, including graded assignments, until the course end date. Upgrading will not change or extend the course end date. You’ll still need to pass the course before it ends in order to earn a certificate.

After the course end date, as a verified learner, you will have access to archived course content and materials, as long it exists on edX.

How long can I access a course in the audit track before it expires?

In the free audit track, you can access the course content for a limited time only.

This access counts from the date and time you first enroll, whether or not you’re actively viewing the course. While you have audit access, a banner on the right side of the course homepage will display the date your access will expire.

If your access to the audit track has expired and the course is still open for verified enrollment, you may Upgrade to Verified from your edX Dashboard to regain access.

If the upgrade option no longer appears or if you prefer not to upgrade, you may instead enroll again in a future session. Most edX courses repeat in new sessions.

Please review What are the differences between audit (free) and verified (paid) courses? to learn more about the differences between audit (free) and verified (paid) content.

Some courses such as Boot Camps and Executive Education courses do not offer an audit track.

What are the differences between audit (free) and verified (paid) courses?

You’ll have a different experience in your course depending on whether you’ve enrolled in the free audit track or paid verified track.

Free audit track
  • Start learning for free and upgrade later if the upgrade deadline hasn’t passed
  • Access course readings and video lectures for a limited time
  • Complete any ungraded, practice assignments if the course features any
  • Participate in the the course discussion forums
  • View progress of any ungraded practice assignments As a free audit learner, you will have temporary access to course materials except graded assignments, and you will not earn a certificate at the end of the course.

The audit access counts from the date and time that you first enrolled in the course, or if you enrolled before the course started, it counts from the date and time that the course opened.

Once the audit access duration has passed, you’ll lose access to the course and you will no longer be able to click View Course on your dashboard. If the upgrade deadline has not passed, you can extend access by upgrading to the verified track.

Paid verified track
  • Qualify to receive a verified certificate if you earn a passing score before the course ends
  • Access graded assignments and exams
  • View the course materials even after the course is archived
  • Includes readings, videos, discussions, practice problems and progress just like the free audit track
  • Easily share your certificate to your CV or resume, or post it directly on LinkedIn
  • Support our mission of affordable education to everyone globally

What is the audit track?

Most edX courses have the option to audit for free. As a free audit learner, you will have temporary access to all course materials except graded assignments, and you will not earn a certificate the end of the course.

Some edX courses, such as Boot Camps and Executive Education courses do not offer an audit track.

How to enroll in the audit track

If a course is available in the audit track, the course enrollment page will mention a FREE option.

Click the Enroll button to enroll.

Note: If you’re interested in auditing one of our XSeries, Professional Certificate, MicroMasters, or MicroBachelors programs, please read this article for steps to enroll.

After you click Enroll you’ll have the option to Upgrade Now or Continue. If you select Continue you can still upgrade later if the upgrade deadline hasn’t passed.

Enjoy the audit track

  • Start learning for free and upgrade later if the upgrade deadline hasn’t passed
  • Access course readings and video lectures for a limited time
  • Complete any ungraded, practice assignments if the course features any
  • Participate in the the course discussion forums
  • View progress of any ungraded practice assignments

Upgrade to the verified track to earn a certificate

Certificates are not available in the free audit track. If the upgrade deadline has not passed, you may upgrade to the verified track. You can read more about the features of the verified track here.

What is the verified track? What does verified mean?

If a course is active, you can enroll in the verified track to pursue a verified certificate of achievement.

Benefits of the verified track

  • Qualify to receive a verified certificate if you earn a passing score before the course ends
  • Access all graded assignments and exams
  • View the course materials even after the course is archived
  • Includes readings, videos, discussions, practice problems and progress just like the free audit track
  • Easily share your certificate to your CV or resume, or post it directly on LinkedIn

How to enroll in the verified track

From the course introduction page, click the Enroll button to enroll. If the course is active and the upgrade deadline has not passed, you’ll have the option to Upgrade Now or Continue.

If you click Upgrade Now you’ll be directed to the payment page. Enter your credit card information or click the PayPal button in the upper right to pay from your PayPal account. All fees are charged in US Dollars.

How do I receive the certificate?

In order to receive a verified certificate after upgrading you’ll need to earn a passing total score before the course ends, and pass proctoring if your course contains proctored exams.

Why can’t I access the assignments and exams?

If you’re enrolled as a free audit learner, you won’t have access to graded assignments and exams. You can expect to see a message encouraging you to upgrade to the verified track on any page that features a graded assignment.

About Dates and Schedules

Does my course have due dates?

edX courses are either self-paced or instructor-paced. Your experience with due dates and their flexibility will differ depending on the course’s pacing type. All course sessions have end dates. Read more about where to find your course’s due dates.

Instructor-paced

Instructor-paced courses have a fixed schedule. Course materials may be published periodically, and there may be fixed due dates. After an assignment’s due date passes, you will no longer be able to complete the assignment. If you join the course late, you cannot make up any due dates you missed, but you may still be able to earn a passing score. Check your course’s syllabus, calendar, or welcome module for information about the course schedule and grading policy.

Self-paced

Self-paced courses may feature suggested due dates. These due dates are personalized suggestions based on the date you first enrolled to help you plan and pace your studies. You can submit assignments or exams any time up until the final end date of the course. The final end date is fixed and not shiftable or flexible. Read more about the suggested schedule.

Note: the suggested schedule does not apply to the final course end date or open response assessments such as peer-reviewed essay assignments. These due dates are fixed by your instructor and they can’t be shifted or extended.

Related

  • What is self-paced or instructor-paced? Which is my course?
  • Where can I view my due dates?
  • How does the suggested schedule work?
  • Why doesn’t the Submit button work?

How can I prepare before my course begins? Is there suggested reading material?

Each course is different - some have prerequisites, and some don’t. Take a look at your specific course’s recommended prerequisites on the course enrollment page before you enroll. If you do not meet a particular prerequisite, you may still take the course. Are there prerequisites for my course?

All required academic materials will be provided during the course, within your browser. Some courses may list additional resources on their course description pages. For supplemental reading material during a course, post a question in the course discussion forum to ask your classmates and course staff for suggestions.

And, of course, be sure to check out DemoX to get a better sense of the edX platform and how courses work.

How does the suggested schedule work?

If you enroll in a self-paced course long before its end date, the course will feature suggested deadlines on assignments to help you plan and stay on-track. You can view this by clicking the Dates tab.

The final end date is not part of the suggested schedule and cannot be shifted.

The suggested schedule in the paid verified track

If you check the Dates tab in a self-paced course, you may notice a message ‘We’ve built a suggested schedule to help you stay on track. But don’t worry—it’s flexible so you can learn at your own pace.’

If you’re enrolled in the verified certificate track and you miss a suggested deadline, you can shift your due dates.

To do so, click the Shift due dates button available on the Dates page, course homepage, or at the top of any assignment page.

There will also be a link to Shift due dates next to the Submit button on the assignment page.

You’ll find a message prompting you to shift your due dates only after you miss a suggested deadline. It’s not possible to shift the dates before missing a deadline.

The suggested schedule in the free audit track

In most courses, graded assignments aren’t available in the free audit track. Graded assignments on the Dates tab and on the Progress page will show as locked to audit track learners.

​

If you’re enrolled in the free audit track, after missing any deadline on the suggested schedule, you may be required to upgrade to the verified track in order to shift the suggested schedule.

How long do I have to finish? When does the course end?

Every course on edX.org, including those which are self-paced will have an end date. To earn a certificate on the verified track you will need to have a passing score by the end date.

You can find the course end date:

  • On the course enrollment page, if the course is ending this year
  • On your dashboard listed below the course title
  • On the course homepage under Important Dates
  • Under the course Dates tab Read more: When does my course start and end?

What time is the class?

edX courses are not “live”, so there is no set time when you will need to be viewing the course.

All lectures are pre-recorded and available 24 hours a day so that you can review the lectures anytime during the week, whenever is most convenient for you. Some instructor-paced courses may publish the the course materials periodically throughout the course duration, or set fixed due dates for assignments for exams. You can read more about how edX courses are paced in this article.

To get a better sense of how our courses and platform work, take a quick tour of the Demo course.

When does my course start and end?

Your course’s start and end date are posted in several places, both before and after you’ve enrolled.

  • The course Enrollment page (before you enroll)
  • Your dashboard (after you’ve enrolled)
  • The course homepage (after you’ve enrolled)
  • The courses Dates tab (after you’ve enrolled) Date

Where to find it

What does it mean?

Start Date

  • The course enrollment page
  • Your dashboard You can’t view the course until the start date has passed.

End Date

  • The course enrollment page
  • Your dashboard
  • The course homepage
  • The course Dates tab Read more about archived courses.

The Course Enrollment Page

If you search for a course in the course catalog then click the course thumbnail, you will view the course enrollment page.

The start and end date will show above the Enroll button. The end date may not show if the course is not ending any time soon.

If the course is self-paced and the course has already begun, the start date will show today’s date. This means you can start today!

Your Dashboard

Find your dashboard at home.edx.org.

If the course hasn’t started yet, its start date will be listed below the course title.

After the course has started, its end date will be listed below the course title.

The Course Homepage

When you click the course title from your dashboard, you will find your course homepage. You can view upcoming deadlines and the final course end date on the right hand side details under Important Dates. When the end date is approaching soon, it will be posted there. The course end date will be labelled Course ends.

The Dates tab

The end date (in addition to any due dates on assignments) will show on the Dates tab. You can read more about due dates in this article.

To navigate to the Dates tab, from any page within the course, click the Dates tab in the course’s navigation menu.

When does the course start?

For information about how to start a course, see How do I start my course?

After you have enrolled in a course, the course is posted on your edX dashboard. The course start date is located below the course title.

Until the start time has passed, you will be unable to access the course. Once this datetime has passed, the Begin Course button will activate.

Depending on your timezone, the start time may be later than you expect. If the course is unavailable on the start date try again later that day.

When will this course be offered again?

Courses are scheduled by the individual course teams at our partner universities and organizations. Most edX courses repeat usually at least once a year. Courses which are part of programs are expected to repeat more often. Please check our course catalog frequently for new course sessions.

Also, for updates on new and repeat course runs, keep watching for our newsletter updates via email. You can also keep up-to-date by “liking” edX on Facebook or following us on Twitter.

Where can I view my due dates?

On a browser, you can find the end date and any assignment due dates on the course homepage and on the Dates tab. On the mobile app, you can check the Dates tab.

On the course homepage, you can find the end date under Important Dates labelled Course ends.

The Dates tab will list all assignment due dates and the end date.

Self-paced courses

In a self-paced course, all due dates except the final end date and open response assessments are flexible up until the end date. Read more about how self-paced courses and the suggested schedule works.

Instructor-paced courses

In instructor-paced courses, all due dates are fixed and can’t be extended. If an assignment hasn’t been released yet, the Dates page indicates this with ‘Not yet released.’ The Dates page also lists the date certificates will be awarded (Certificates Available) to learners who earned a passing score, after the course end date.

Why is the submit button not active?

The submit button for an assignment will activate once you enter an answer for the assignment, unless the due date has passed. Does my course have due dates?

If your course is self-paced and the end date has not passed, then you may be able to Shift your deadlines using the Shift due dates button available on the Dates tab or course homepage.

About Identity Verification

Can I complete ID verification without a webcam?

If you do not have a webcam but change your name in your Account settings and are prompted to complete ID verification, you can verify your identity using a mobile or tablet device.

To do so, please visit your Account settings page in a mobile browser on your mobile device.

Please note that we recommend using Chrome or Firefox, not Safari.

Next, follow these steps in your mobile browser.

If you do not own a desktop, mobile, or tablet device with a camera, you can certainly use someone else’s compatible device to verify. We recommend that you ask if your local library or internet cafe has available webcams, or borrow a computer or mobile device from a friend.

Can I send or email my photos for ID verification?

We use a secure verification software service for ID verification to ensure your privacy and security. Our support team cannot see the photos you submit, and we cannot manually override the process. So, we cannot accept any images that you email us.

However you can upload an image your photo identification card through the photo verification page:

account.edx.org

Upload your Photo ID Card Image

If you are having trouble getting a clear image of your photo identification card you may instead upload an image of your identification card that you have taken separately in the review step.

After you have taken photos of both your face and your photo identification card you will be on the Review Your Photos page. Click on the question “Is your ID image not clear and too blurry?” This will expand that section and expose the upload option.

Then click the Browse button or Choose File button to select a file from your device. This will enable you to upload an image of your photo identification card that you have scanned or photographed using another method. We do not recommend using the Safari browser to upload photos.

We can only accept jpg, jpeg, or png files that are under 10 MB. Please do not upload .pdf files.

How do I verify my identity after changing the name on my account?

If you would like to change the name on your account after earning an edX certificate, you may be required to verify your identity.

To verify your identity, please follow these steps:

  1. First, make the name change you wish to make on your Account settings page by clicking ‘Edit’ next to your name. After making your update click ‘Save’

  2. If you are making a significant name change, you will see a message stating ‘This name change requires identity verification’. Please click ‘Continue’:

  3. Next, you will be asked to enter your name exactly as it appears on your photo identification card. The name on your ID must exactly match the name you enter during this step. After entering your full name, please click ‘Continue’:

  4. Next, you will follow the instructions through ID verification. First, you will take a photo of yourself.

  5. Next, you will take a photo of your ID card (like a passport, driver’s license, or school or work ID).

  6. Finally, you will be asked to once again confirm your name exactly matches your ID card. Please click ‘Next’:

  7. Finally, you will see a page with both of your photos, as well as your name. After confirming your information, click ‘Submit’.

You can then check to see that your photos are pending review on your Account settings page:

Reviews generally take 5-7 business days. Once your photos are approved, your name change will show on your Account settings page.

If your first attempt to verify your identity fails, please review these tips:

  • Make sure your face is well lit and fills the picture window in the first image. Optimal camera positioning varies with each computer, but generally the best position for a headshot is approximately 12-18 inches (30-45 centimeters) from the camera, with your head centered relative to the computer screen.
  • Make sure your photo identification card fills the window and your name is visible in the second photo. Generally, the best position for a photo of an ID card is 8-12 inches (20-30 centimeters) from the camera, with the ID card centered relative to the camera.
  • We accept most official identification cards that have your name and photo on one side like Passports, driver’s licenses, work or school IDs
  • The name on your ID must exactly match the name you enter during the first step of ID verification, including being in the same language/alphabet.
  • Do not submit blurry photos. If the image you submit is rejected as unreadable, try moving the camera slightly relative to the room’s main light source, which should be in front of your face or ID card. If your computer does not have a camera, you can also use a mobile device that has a camera by opening your Account settings page on the mobile browser, and changing your name there. You also may borrow a friend’s computer to verify then use yours to take the courses. Chrome on iOS does not support camera access. Please use a different browser to complete ID verification.

I am having technical difficulties with verification

Photo verification can be completed on most computers or mobile devices with an up to date browser. The edX support team is not able to review verification photos by email. These must be submitted through the verification web page which is secured and sends these images directly to our verification service in an encrypted form.

If you use Single Sign On (SSO) through your company or university to log into edX, please try logging fully out of edX.org and singing back in though SSO.

If you do not use SSO to log in through your organization, please try the following:

  • Switch browsers to a current version of Chrome or Firefox

  • At the top of your browser window, a message may ask you to share the camera with edx.org. To see and respond to this message every time you take or retake a photograph for the verification process, select Share Selected Device. To share the webcam throughout the process select Always Share.

  • Try an alternate internet connection. Some secure network connections, such as the internet connection in a place of employment, block users from uploading their photos successfully.

  • If you continue to have trouble, we recommend using a different computer or device to submit your photos, then return to your computer to take the course.

Is my verification information secure?

We take security and privacy very seriously at edX. Here’s a quick overview of how your verification photos are managed:

  • We use a third-party partner to perform verification.
  • Images are always transmitted and stored in an encrypted format.
  • edX does not have the ability to view the images.
  • Once your verification is complete, the partner’s staff’s access to images is disabled and images are automatically destroyed.

Why was my ID verification denied?

If your photos were denied, you’ll receive an email notification with the reason for denial, and a notification will also display at the top of your Account Settings page. You can submit your photos again on the edX Identity Verification page.

Common reasons for denial:

  • Photo ID Not Provided
  • User Photo Not Provided
  • Text Not Clear
  • Name Mismatch
Photo ID Not Provided

This means that you did not submit an image of your photo identification card for the second photo. When you submit your photos, the second photo should be a picture of your photo ID card. We accept most identification cards with your photo and name on it.

We recommend that you try again one more time, ensuring that for the second photo, you take a still, clear picture of your photo ID with the name on your ID fully visible.

User Photo Not Provided

This means that you did not submit an image of your face for the first photo. Please note that our verification software will not accept professional headshot images, but rather the user photo must be a ‘selfie’ that you take yourself.

When you try again, in the first photo, make sure that you take a clear, centered picture of yourself with your device’s camera. Optimal camera positioning varies with each computer, but generally the best position for a headshot is approximately 12-18 inches (30-45 centimeters) from the camera, with your head centered relative to the computer screen.

Text Not Clear

This means that the name on the image of your photo ID card was too blurry or not readable in the second photo. When you try again, for the second photo, hold your ID card as still as possible and make sure that your full name and photo are visible in the image. Generally, the best position for a photo of an ID card is 8-12 inches (20-30 centimeters) from the camera, with the ID card centered relative to the camera.

Name mismatch

This means that the name on your edX account does not exactly match the name on the photo ID you provided. This includes being in the same language or alphabet.

You may update the name on your edX account before trying again in your Account Settings. If you prefer to use a different name other than the name on your photo ID card, you can change your name back after your verification photos have been approved.

If you have an accessibility limitation that prevents positioning yourself or your ID card, please contact our Learner Support team by clicking Help at the bottom of this page.

About MicroBachelors

Does enrolling in MicroBachelors mean I’m enrolled in a university?

Enrolling in a MicroBachelors course or program does not mean you are enrolled at the institution teaching the program.

However, if you purchase all courses in a MicroBachelors program and successfully earn a program certificate you are eligible to redeem this certificate for credit at no additional cost through our credit partner institution, Thomas Edison State University, which is the school awarding credit for MicroBachelors programs.

Although you can earn credit at TESU you are not enrolled at their institution unless you choose to apply and are admitted through their admissions process. Please see more information on “How does credit work for MicroBachelors programs?” to learn more about earning credit.

Is there an application or prerequisites for MicroBachelors?

Like most edX courses and programs, there are no firm prerequisites required to enroll in MicroBachelors™ courses. Additionally, MicroBachelors™ programs do not require a formal application and do not have a decision process for admissions.

A GED or High School equivalency is not required to redeem your MicroBachelors program certificate for credit at Thomas Edison State University. However, a GED or high school equivalency is required if you apply to enroll in a degree program with Thomas Edison State University.

About MicroMasters

Are there prerequisites for MicroMasters® programs?

Some MicroMasters® programs have suggested prerequisites. To find out about prerequisites for a specific MicroMasters® program, please review the program’s introduction page.

Regarding prerequisites for a single course, you can read more here.

For most programs, the prerequisites are suggested and not checked or required. If you do not meet the prerequisites you are welcome to join the courses and try your best.

For more information, see the edX MicroMasters® page.

Can anyone enroll in a MicroMasters® program? Are there any eligibility requirements?

MicroMasters® programs are open to everyone and have no requirements for eligibility.

Courses in MicroMasters® programs are graduate-level courses, and some courses might have prerequisite knowledge or courses. If a course has prerequisites, the prerequisites are listed on the enrollment page for that course. Read more: Are there prerequisites for my course?

Can the MicroMasters® credential be transferred to any school for credit?

Typically, only the institution that offers the MicroMasters® program grants credit. However, a few schools do offer credit for MicroMasters® programs from other universities. The universities that offer credit for each MicroMasters® program are listed on the right side of the MicroMasters® program detail page.

If you’re viewing the program introduction page for an edX MicroMasters® program, expand the Certificate & Credit Pathways section to read specific details about which institutions accept the credential for credit.

If you’re not sure whether your institution will accept the credit from a MicroMasters credential, we recommend that you contact the institution directly to confirm.

Does earning a MicroMasters® credential guarantee admission to a university master’s program?

Earning a MicroMasters® credential does not guarantee admission to a full master’s program.

However, if you apply and are accepted into the on-campus or online program that is associated with your MicroMasters® program, the MicroMasters® credential will count toward the degree.

If you’re viewing the program introduction page for an edX MicroMasters® program, expand the Certificate & Credit Pathways section to read specific details about which institutions accept the credential for credit.

For more information, see the edX MicroMasters® page.

How many credits toward a degree does the MicroMasters® offer?

The number of credits varies across MicroMasters® programs. Each university determines the number of credits that will count toward the Master’s degree.

If you’re viewing the program introduction page for an edX MicroMasters® program, expand the Certificate & Credit Pathways section to read specific details. An explanation here will list how many credits this program may award.

For more information, see the edX MicroMasters® page.

How much does a MicroMasters® cost?

To receive a MicroMasters® certificate, you must earn a verified certificate in each of the courses in the program. The fees for verified certificates vary across courses and MicroMasters® programs.

For information about the cost of a specific MicroMasters® program, view the program page for that program, or view the pages for the individual courses in the MicroMasters® program.

For more information, see the edX MicroMasters® page.

My course has a proctored exam. How should I prepare?

Before you take a proctored exam, you must install the 3rd party software that allows monitoring of your computer during the exam. You will also need a functioning webcam, so that you can take photos of yourself and a photo ID, and scan the room where you will take the exam.

When you take the exam, you must follow a set of defined rules, and must not engage in any behavior that the proctoring review considers suspicious. Be sure to review the exam information within your course, as some courses have different allowances or restrictions for proctored exams.

Read the following articles for more detailed information:

Preparing to Take a Proctored Exam

Proctored Exam Rules and Requirements

Software Secure exams

Setting up the Proctoring Software

Proctortrack exams

Taking a Proctortrack Onboarding Exam

What are MicroMasters® programs?

MicroMasters® programs are series of graduate level courses that you take to earn credentials in a specific career field. MicroMasters® credentials bridge the gap between education and the workplace and are created by esteemed edX university partners, valued by top companies including Walmart and GE, and linked to specific career outcomes.

MicroMasters® credentials are also a valuable addition to academic portfolios as the credential offers a pathway to credit. MicroMasters® programs have no admissions requirements, and are open to anyone.

Once you’ve successfully earned a MicroMasters® certificate on edX, you can apply to a full Masters program that recognizes the MicroMasters® credential. If you’re accepted, you can apply your MicroMasters credit toward a portion of the degree, resulting in an accelerated program.

For more information, read the edX MicroMasters page.

Will the MicroMasters® credential help me get a job or interview with the company that recognizes the program?

While a MicroMasters® credential will give you the tools to excel in the industry and area of focus of the program, it does not guarantee you an interview or job. You can share your credential on your LinkedIn page and resume to demonstrate your accomplishments and highlight your knowledge.

For more information, see the edX MicroMasters® page.

Accessibility

Are courses accessible to learners with disabilities?

edX strives to create an innovative online-learning platform that promotes accessibility for all learners, regardless of any physical limitation that they might have. edX is dedicated to creating a platform that is not only itself accessible, but also enables course content creators to create accessible content. You may read our accessibility policy here.

Request an exception accommodation for proctored exams

I have a disability that requires use of special software, special hardware, a second monitor, or other tools. What can I do to get a security exception accommodation for the proctoring process?

We recommend that you request an accessibility accommodation at least a week before the scheduled exam. Accommodations are granted by the partner institution, not by edX. Since timing is important, we suggest contacting the course instructor first, by whatever method they have documented in the course.

You can also send your accommodation request to accessibility@edx.org and we will forward it to the course team for review. Requests through accessibility@edx.org may introduce a small additional delay.

Request exception for Room Scan requirement due to mobility constraints

I am unable to move my webcam to scan the room due to a mobility constraint. What can I do to proceed with my proctored exam?

If your mobility constraint is due to a disability, you can request an accessibility accommodation exception from the course team for the Room/Desk Scan requirement. Contact the course team or accessibility@edx.org at least a week before the exam to request an accommodation exception, in order to allow sufficient time to process your request.

Accessing Course Content

Can I access edX courses on my smartphone or tablet?

Yes, you can take edX courses using your smartphone or tablet.

The free edX mobile app has Android and iOS versions. The Android app is available in the Google Play Store, and the iOS app is available in the Apple App Store.

Most components of your courses will function in the app or a mobile browser, but some might not, depending on the content of the particular course. At times you might need to use a computer with an up-to-date version of Firefox or Chrome to complete tasks.

For more information about using the edX mobile app, see What can I do on the edX app?

Can I bookmark a place in a course?

  • Add a Bookmark
  • Remove a Bookmark
  • View your Bookmarks Yes! You can set a bookmark on any page in the course so that you can easily come back to it later.

Note: You can create up to 100 bookmarks for any one course.

Add a Bookmark

You can add a bookmark by selecting Bookmark this page below the page’s title.

After you add a bookmark, Bookmark this page changes to Bookmarked, and a bookmark indicator appears on that page’s tab in the navigation bar above.

Remove a Bookmark

To remove a bookmark, navigate to the bookmarked page and click Bookmarked.

View Your Bookmarks

You can find your bookmarks on the course homepage. Click the Bookmarks link in the Course Tools menu on the right side of the homepage. Bookmarks are listed in the order they were created, and include a location to the course material and the date that you added the bookmark.

Can I download my course or textbook?

edX courses are fully online and can be accessed through the edX platform. It isn’t possible to download a course, but in many courses, you’ll find links to download lecture videos underneath the video player window.

Most edX courses do not have a “textbook” and instead have any required reading within the course itself.

Courses share reading materials in different ways; your course team should provide you with information. Some courses provide links to reading materials under Course Handouts.

Can I view my course offline?

To complete assignments and view the majority of our course content, you’d an active internet connection.

Many courses do offer downloadable videos and transcripts which you can view offline. In the lower left corner of the video window, in a course and there you will find the download options, if they are available.

Also, our edX mobile apps available through the Google Play Store or Apple Appstore can download videos for offline viewing.

Do edX course videos have transcripts or captions?

Yes! edX course videos have interactive transcripts as well as closed captions. In some courses, the transcripts can also be downloaded.

Turn on Interactive Transcripts

You can turn on interactive transcripts for a video by clicking the double quotation (“) button in the lower right corner of the video player. Interactive transcripts appear to the right of the screen, and keep pace with the video as it plays.

Turn on Closed Captions

You can turn on closed captions by clicking the closed caption (CC) button. Closed captions appear along the bottom of the video.

Choose Your Preferred Language

Some videos may offer captions and interactive transcripts in multiple languages. In this case, you can select your language preference by clicking the closed caption (CC) button and then select your preferred language. Auto-generated captions will be indicated atop the interactive transcript and offer the opportunity to provide feedback on quality. If you have urgent transcription concerns, click the Help button in the lower right hand corner of this page to contact Learner Support.

Download Transcript Files

Many courses offer downloadable transcripts in SubRip (.srt) or text (.txt) file formats. You can access the transcripts by clicking the download options below the video window under Transcripts.

SubRip (.srt) files can be opened by most word processor programs and browsers. Please note that .srt files include time information. If you prefer to download a file without the time information, choose the .txt format. This format does not include time data, and therefore might be easier to read.

To download a video transcript, below the video player, select the download option for the transcript file format that you want. In the dialog that opens, you can choose to open or save the transcript file.

For more information about course videos and the edX video player controls, see Watching Videos on the edX Video Player.

How do I get help with my course?

You can get help from course moderators and peers in the discussion forum within your course. That’s the best place to ask questions about assignments, grades, and other course content.

To get to your course discussion forum click the link at the top of the page within your course:

Your course’s discussion forum is monitored by the course instructors, who have designed the course lessons and are experts in the course material.

Common questions or issues that can be answered by the course team on the discussion forum include the following:

  • I need help with non-edX software needed for my course, such as Microsoft Excel or Jupyter.
  • I need help finding a specific lesson or handout.
  • I believe I’ve found a mistake or wrong answer in the course content. You can also find information about a specific course or topic by searching our course catalog and reading the course information page.

Note: edX is not able to provide direct contact information for professors, teaching assistants (TAs), or other course staff.

How do I start my course?

After you enroll in a course from the edX course catalog, the course will be listed on your dashboard.

If the course hasn’t started yet, its start date will be listed below the course title and you won’t be able to view the course yet.

If the course has started, click Begin Course to view the Course Homepage. You may then click Start Course in the upper right of the course homepage, or scroll down to browse the course outline.

How many assignments are in a course? Are there exams?

The types and number of assignments vary by course. If you enroll in the course in the free audit track, you can preview the course and its syllabus to confirm the required assignments.

Most courses do not have just a single quiz or exam to pass, but instead have many assignments throughout the course. If you finish the course in the verified track with at least the minimum passing score, you will earn a certificate.

The minimum passing score also varies by course. You can see the minimum score for a particular course on the progress page.

To get a better sense of how our courses and platform work, please check out our Demo course.

Return to where you were working in a course

You can return to the unit that you completed most recently in any edX course, or in a specific edX course.

Return to the Most Recently Completed Unit in Any Course

To return to the unit you completed most recently in any of your edX courses, select the arrow next to your username on any edX page, and then select Resume your last course.

The course that you were most recently working in, opens to the unit that you most recently completed.

Return to the Most Recently Completed Unit in a Specific Course

To return to the unit you completed most recently in a specific edX course, you have the following options:

  • On your edX dashboard, locate your course, and then select Resume Course next to the social media sharing options.
  • Open the course, and select Resume Course on the Course page. When you select Resume Course, the course opens to the unit that you most recently completed.

What if the Resume Course button doesn’t work?

If the Resume Course button takes you to the wrong place or takes you to an error page, try visiting your course’s homepage.

To navigate to the course homepage from your dashboard, click the title of the course.

To navigate to the course homepage from any page within the course, click the Course tab in the course’s navigation menu.

From the course homepage, scroll down to the course outline. Click the Expand all button to display every section in the course. You can usually find the place where you last left off by selecting a section that’s missing a green checkmark.

Watching videos on the edX video player

Related article: Why can’t I play the videos in my course?

All edX courses include videos, and edX has its own video player. Most of the controls on the player will be familiar to you if you have watched online videos before. However, the edX video player has some extra features you may not have seen.

The following image shows the video player in the middle of a video, followed by an explanation of each option or control on the video player.

Note: Not all videos will have all the controls shown. Only the controls that apply to a specific video will be included in the video player.

  • Play/pause: You can play the video by selecting this control. Select this control again to pause the video.
  • Time: The first number indicates the length of time the video has already played. The second number indicates the total length of the video.
  • Playback bar: You can go to a different point in the video by selecting and dragging this control or by using the left and right arrow keys.
  • Speed: If you want the video to play faster or slower, you can select different speeds using this control.
  • Volume: You can use this control to change the volume of the video.
  • HD: You can play the video in high-definition visual resolution by selecting this option if HD quality is available for the video. This option works best if you have a fast Internet connection. Select this control again to play the video in standard resolution. (If this option is not available, the HD button will not be available.)
  • Full screen: You can expand the video to fill your browser window by selecting this control. To return to default mode, press ESC on your keyboard or select this control again.
  • Show or hide closed captioning: You can show an overlaid transcript of the audio portion of the file by selecting this control. If you show the captions, you can move them to different areas on the video screen by dragging and dropping them. To hide the captions, select this control again.
  • Note: In some cases, two sets of captions can appear when you select CC. This situation can occur if YouTube is the host service for the video and your YouTube account settings for playback are set to always show captions. As a result, YouTube and your course might both provide captions for the video. To correct this problem, select CC again or change your YouTube account setting.
  • Show transcript: You can show a complete, scrolling transcript of the audio portion of the file to the right of the video by selecting this control. Many course teams set video transcripts to play by default when your video starts. Select this control again to hide the transcript.
  • Language menu: If translations are available, you can select a different language for the closed captions and the transcript using this control.
  • Download video: You can download the video to watch later with this option (if available for the video).
  • Download transcript: You can download the video’s transcript as a SubRip (.srt) file or as a text (.txt) file. You can open files of either type in a text editor such as Notepad.
  • Handouts: You can download any handouts that the course team has associated with the video.
  • License or Copyright: If the course team reserves rights or specifies a Creative Commons license for the video that is different from the course-wide license, that information appears below the video player. For Creative Commons licenses, you can select the license to open a web site with more information about your rights.
  • Transcript: You can scroll through the transcript and select any line to go to that point in the video. For a demonstration of the edX video player, see Videos on edX in the edX DemoX course.

What’s the Course Homepage?

The course homepage is the course’s main page for:

  • The course outline: You can navigate to any available page in the course from the course homepage.
  • Announcements and Welcome Messages
  • Course Handouts
  • Important Dates: Deadlines coming up soon To navigate to the course homepage from your dashboard, click the title of the course.

To navigate to the course homepage from any page within the course, click the Course tab in the course’s navigation menu.

The course outline

Click Expand All to view every available page in the course, or click the title of a specific module to expand or collapse that section’s content. Click the links listed below a section title to view the lessons in that subsection.

Important Dates

On the course homepage, you can view Important Dates on the right hand side. This will display any upcoming due dates or the verification upgrade deadline. The course’s end date is labelled Course ends.

Where are the course handouts?

In many courses, additional documents are available in the Course Handouts section. This is on the far right side of the course homepage, below Course Tools and Important Dates.

Certificate Payments & Refunds

Can I get a receipt for my enrollment fee?

When you purchase an edX course an entry is made in your Order History. You can view the payment confirmation in your account settings.

To View Your Order Details

  • Select the arrow next to your username in the upper right of edx.org.
  • Select Order History.
  • Select View Order Details to view the confirmation page of an order. If you have paid by PayPal, you can also find a detailed invoice in your PayPal account.

We are not able to create custom invoices or receipts with additional information under any circumstances.

Does edX offer financial assistance for verified certificates?

edX offers financial assistance to learners who demonstrate that paying the verified certificate fee would cause economic hardship.

Financial assistance currently provides a 80% reduction in the verified track fee. You can find detailed steps on applying for Financial Assistance in this article: How do I apply for financial assistance?

Financial assistance is not currently available for Professional Education courses and select MicroMasters courses. While Boot Camps also do not offer traditional edX Financial Assistance, you can learn more about payment options for Boot Camps here.

How do I enter my coupon code?

If you received a coupon code for a course, you may enter this on the payment page after selecting Upgrade to Verified from your edX dashboard or selecting the verified track when enrolling.

To apply the coupon, paste your code into the Add coupon code field and then click the Apply button before placing the order.

After clicking Apply the discount will appear and the total will be reduced. Make sure this reduced total shows before you complete the order with the Place Order button. A message “Coupon code ‘[your code]’ added to basket.” will also display at the top of the page.

If you did not add your code before clicking Place Order you may unenroll for a refund, then re-enroll and pay again with the coupon applied.

How do I take a course for free?

If a course is available in the audit track, on the course introduction page will mention a FREE option. Click the Enroll button to enroll.

After you click Enroll you’ll have the option to Upgrade Now or Continue. If you select Continue you can enroll now for free and optionally upgrade later if the upgrade deadline hasn’t passed.

If you’re interested in auditing one of our programs, please read this article for steps to enroll.

You can read more about the free audit track here: What does audit mean? What is the audit track?

Some edX courses, such as Boot Camps and Executive Education courses do not offer an audit track.

Certificates

Can I earn a certificate after the end?

No, it’s not possible to earn a certificate in a course session that has ended. To earn a certificate you will have to upgrade to the verified track by the course upgrade deadline, and reach a passing score before the course end date. After the course ends and becomes archived, progress scores no longer update.

Read more:

  • When does my course start and end?
  • How long do I have to finish? When does the course end?
  • What does archived mean?

Can I get a paper copy of my certificate?

edX can not send individual printed copies of certificates. However, you can save the certificate by downloading it as a PDF file using the Chrome or Safari browsers.

Saving your certificate to PDF in Chrome

  1. Choose File > Print.

  2. Choose Adobe PDF as the printer in the Print dialog box. To customize the Adobe PDF printer setting, click the Properties (or Preferences) button.

  3. Click Print. Type a name for your file, and click Save.

Saving your certificate to PDF in Safari

  1. Choose File > Export to PDF

  2. Type the name for your file and optionally select the location of the file under Where.

  3. Click Save.

Can I get a transcript?

edX cannot provide academic transcripts. However, if you’ve earned certificate in any courses that are part of a program credential, you can find and share those grades from your official Learner Records in your account profile.

The program record will show your full name, edX username and connected email address as well as show each course within the program, the number of times you attempted the course and your highest final grade.

Outside of programs, verified certificates can be used as proof of course completion.

We recommend printing your progress page if you wish to have a record of your course grade.

Can I get an IBM Badge?

Learners who complete select IBM courses and earn a verified certificate are eligible for an additional digital badge from IBM and Credly. To claim this click the Claim your badge button in the course. This button will only appear when you have successfully completed the course with a passing score and earned a certificate.

(By clicking the button you also consent to sharing your name and email with Credly)

Badge FAQs:

  • What is a digital badge? A digital badge is a portable online data object which represents an outcome or achievement. They are secure, web-enabled credentials that contain granular, verified information employers can use to evaluate an individual’s potential.

  • How does my credential get displayed as a digital badge? edX and IBM have partnered with Credly to leverage their platform to translate your learning outcomes or experiences into the digital badges. This allows the badge earner (you) to accept, manage and share your digital badge and the credentials it represents as a digital asset.

  • What is Credly? Credly is a digital badging platform (formerly known as Credly’s Acclaim platform). Its focal point is an enterprise-class badging platform built in compliance with the IMS Global Learning Consortium Open Badge Specifications to support the specialized needs of credential issuers, employers, and professionals. The Credly web platform provides a secure means of storage, transport and credential publishing that adds a layer of protection against those who may falsely claim high-stakes credentials.

  • What is the relationship between edX, IBM and Credly? edX and IBM have entered into a vendor relationship with Credly to implement a program for web-enabled credentials displayed as digital badges. Credly is the web site from which you claim (accept), store and transmit open badges related to your credentials and where those to whom you send your credentials verify the metadata contained within. edX is the issuer of badges for IBM courses on edx.org, as authorized by IBM.

My name on my certificate is not appearing how I wanted. Can I change it?

The full name in your edX account is the name that is used for certificates. At any time, you can change your full name in your account settings. After you change your name in your account, any certificates you have earned will automatically update to reflect the change*.

For step by step instructions for updating your name in your edX account, please review How do I change my name on my account?

*Unfortunately we are no longer able to update older PDF certificates earned before October 2015.

What is a verified certificate?

A verified certificate shows that you have successfully passed an edX course in the verified track. edX certificates feature the logos of both edX and the partnering institution. Each certificate has a unique and authenticating URL and ID. Our certificates are both shareable and printable. Our certificates are issued electronically, and we do not send paper certificates by mail.

You can find more information on upgrading to the verified track here.

Read more

We encourage you to read our Learner Stories blog for more details on how our users have changed their careers and enriched their education with edX verified certificates of achievement.

What types of certificates does edX offer?

edX currently offers two types of certificates:

  • Certificates for single courses
  • Program certificates for programs (series of courses) Note: Not all courses offer certificates, and archived courses do not award certificates.

Course Certificates

Course certificates are available for a fee that varies by course. You earn a certificate by passing the course with the required grade in the paid track.

For more information about verified certificates, see the edX Verified Certificates page.

Program Certificates

You earn program certificates when you have earned a verified certificate for each course in the program. edX offers program certificates for MicroMasters™, MicroBachelors™, Professional Certificate, and XSeries programs. For example, here is an XSeries program certificate.

For more information about programs, see the topics in the Programs section.

For more information about receiving edX certificates, see How are edX certificates delivered?.

Where can I find my program certificate?

MicroMasters™, MicroBachelors™, Professional Certificates, and XSeries program certificates are issued within a few weeks after you have received all of the verified certificates for the individual courses in the program.

When your certificate is ready, a link appears on the Program Details Page. To access the Program Details page, go to your edX dashboard, click the Programs tab at the top, then click the particular program. A link to view the certificate appears in the upper right.

Also see What does a program certificate look like?

For information about receiving individual course certificates, check out How are edX certificates delivered?

Learner records are also updated shortly after all the course certificates and the program certificate are issued. For more information on program records see the article Viewing and sharing your program record

Will my certificate include a grade and/or number of credit hours?

edX certificates do not show your final grade. Earning an edX certificate indicates that you completed the course with a passing grade. Your final course grade appears with other course information on your dashboard once the course has ended, and a complete record of your scores on all course assignments and exams appears on the course Progress page. If you need a record of your performance in a course, use your web browser to print one of these pages.

In some Spanish language courses, certificates have been set up to show the “Hours of Effort”, but it depends on the course team. For an approximation of the hours of effort a course represents, please refer to the course introduction page.

Checking Your Course Progress

How can my course be marked as completed?

If you are registered in the verified track and earn a certificate, this certificate is your proof of completion. You may share this with others to show that you completed the course.

Once you begin an edX course, on your dashboard the View Course button will change to Resume Course. This button is there for your convenience to return to the course and it doesn’t indicate completion or non-completion.

To view your grade towards earning the certificate in the verified track you can view your course grading on the Progress tab within your course.

Course Completion Chart

This radius chart available on the New Progress page experience represents how much of the course content has a green checkmark. That’s not just graded assignments, but also readings, videos, or practice assignments.

This Completion chart doesn’t affect your certificate eligibility - check that you’ve passed the course to assure you’re all set. Why doesn’t the Course Completion chart show 100%?

How do I know if I passed?

  • New Progress page experience
  • Legacy Progress page experience You can view your current grade in a course by clicking the Progress tab in the course.

As you work through the graded assignments in a course, your scores will appear on the Progress tab of your course.

Progress scores stop updating after the course end date has passed.

New Progress page

Most courses display your Progress using this new Progress page experience. Here, your total score is indicated on the Grades bar by Your current grade. It’s also indicated on the Grade summary table across from Your current weighted grade summary.

In order to pass the course and earn the Certificate, Your current grade must meet or exceed the Passing grade indicated on the Grades bar.

Legacy Progress page

In some select courses, the Progress page still displays the legacy progress page experience. Your course team decides which one is displayed.

The Progress tab contains a chart of all graded assignments in the course and below that, a list of all assignments and their scores.

To pass and earn a certificate in the verified track you will need a total score meeting or exceeding the “Pass” line between the gray and white sections on the graph by the course end date.

You can confirm which bar corresponds to which assignment by hovering over the bar or clicking its label on the bottom. A tooltip will display the name of the assignment.

Note: Only graded assignments appear in the graph and count towards your final score. Ungraded “practice” assignments are not shown in the graph.

The “average” columns show your cumulative average for all assignments of that type through your course. This will rise as you complete more assignments of that type.

For details on individual problem scores within an assignment, review View Your Grading Details.

Notes about grading

All questions regarding grading and course content are best addressed in your course discussion forum. It is there that course staff can directly read and respond to your query.

Note: Most edX courses post their grading policy in the course syllabus. Both the syllabus and the discussion forum are available once your class has begun.

In some high stakes courses the staff may hide the progress tab during the final exam. These will be restored shortly after the course end date.

How much work will I have to do to pass my course?

To be considered as passing in the verified track and eligible for a certificate, learners will need to complete the graded assignments within the course and achieve the minimum passing score in the total column on the progress page.

The projected hours of study required for each course are included on the course description page. This time is just an estimate from the course staff. Some students may need less time, or more time to be successful. For further details please check your syllabus or course discussion forum when your course begins.

If I fail a course, can I retake it?

If you did not pass the course with the grade required to earn a verified certificate, you can enroll in a future session if it’s offered again. Most edX courses repeat in new sessions. Please note you will need to pay the verified certificate fee again.

Note: Un-enrolling from and re-enrolling in the same course session won’t reset your course progress.

Each course team sets a fixed number of attempts on each graded assignment. Once you’ve used up all available attempts on an assignment, it isn’t possible to retake the assignment. It’s also not possible to reset your Progress score. You’ll need to wait for a future session to retake the course.

View your grade details

  • New progress page experience
  • Legacy progress page experience

New Progress page experience

Most courses display your Progress using this new Progress page experience. You can view your grades on individual graded assignments under the Detailed grades table on your Progress page.

The Detailed Grades table outlines each graded assignment chronologically by course section with the learner’s corresponding score. The title of each graded assignment links to the page where you can find that assignment.

Legacy Progress page experience

In some select courses, the Progress page may still show the legacy Progress experience.

You can view your scores on individual problems, as well as your current overall course grade, by selecting the Progress page and scrolling down to the outline below the Progress chart.

Below the grading chart is a list of course sections in the order that they occur in the course. Each section will contain one of the following grading details:

  • Problem Scores: The points that you earned out of the possible points for graded assignments. These count towards your final score for the certificate. In addition, this graded subsection may not be available to audit track learners.
  • Practice Scores: The points that you earned for ungraded assignments. These assignments do not count towards earning the certificate, and they may award zero points.
  • No problem scores in this section: Sections that do not have any assignments, graded or ungraded.

What if I have a question about course grading?

You can see your current grade in a course by clicking the Progress tab in the course. How do I know if I passed?

All questions regarding grading and course content are best addressed in your course discussion forum. It is there that course staff can directly read and respond to your query.

Note: Most edX courses post their grading policy in the course syllabus. Both the syllabus and the discussion forum are available once your class has begun.

Why are the averages on my progress page low?

The Progress page shows your grade on various assignments, which may be grouped by assignment type. The average is the percentage of points you have earned so far for a particular assignment type out of the total possible points for all assignments of that type.

As you complete more of the course and earn more points on that assignment type, the average will increase.

For example, if there are 4 assignments labelled ‘Check for Understanding’ in the course, and you’ve only completed one of them, that’s 1/4 of the ‘Check for Understanding’ assignments. After you’ve completed all 4 of them, your score for that assignment type will be as high as it can be.

For details on individual problem scores within an assignment, see View Your Grading Details.

Why does my proctored or timed exam score show zero?

There are a few reasons why a proctored or timed exam you’ve completed may be scored as zero:

  • Exam scores are hidden
  • The proctored exam did not pass all proctoring requirements (Read more here)
  • The course is archived

Scores are hidden

In some special, advanced-level courses (like MicroMasters courses), the course team may configure the exam’s score to be hidden until the due date has passed or always hidden.

Your certificate will still be available based on the true score that you earned on the exam.

The proctored exam attempt didn’t pass proctoring rules and requirements

Proctored exams are reviewed by a secure third party to ensure the learner followed the exam’s rules. If the exam was flagged for any reason, you should receive an email notification. You can contact edX Support for assistance appealing this.

The course is archived

In archived courses, the progress score for all assignments and exams won’t update. Archived courses aren’t active and it’s not possible to earn a certificate in archived courses. You can read more about archived courses here.

Why doesn’t Course Completion show 100%?

  • Did I miss anything in the course?
  • Did I miss any assignments?

Course Completion Chart

The Course Completion radius chart available Progress page represents how much of the course content has a green checkmark. That’s not just graded assignments, but also readings, videos, or practice assignments.

Course Completion is not required to earn a certificate. Certificates are awarded based on your Grades in the course. The Completion Chart status only shows to you and doesn’t display on the verified certificate you’ll earn or elsewhere.

Did I miss anything in the course?

You can check the Course tab and click the Expand all button to display every section in the course. In most cases, you might have not watched an entire video, or skipped some optional content.

If you’re a perfectionist, try reviewing any unchecked sections to ensure:

  • you’ve watched the entire video
  • you’ve submitted answers to all graded assessments - if you’re enrolled in the free audit track, upgrade to the verified track for access
  • you’ve received a grade on any open response assessment

Did I miss any assignments?

You can check the Dates tab to confirm that you’ve completed all the graded assignments that count towards the certificate. Any section that features graded work will show Completed if you’ve submitted all graded work and reviewed all content in that section.

If you’re enrolled in the free audit track, assignments may be locked: Read more here.

If you’re enrolled in the paid verified track, any graded assignment you haven’t submitted - or any open response assessment where you don’t have a grade yet - may show Due next or Past Due.

If your course is instructor-paced, these assignments may show Not yet released.

Why doesn’t the green progress checkmark appear?

Sometimes the green progress checkmark on a page or section may not appear when expected, but don’t worry!

If you’re working towards earning the certificate in the verified track, you’ll qualify for the certificate if you earn a passing score. If a green check mark is missing, it won’t prevent you from passing or receiving the certificate.

These checkmarks are there to help you visualize your progress in the course, and we’re always working on improving this feature. If you’re a perfectionist, try checking that:

  • you’ve watched the entire video
  • you’ve submitted answers to all graded assessments - upgrade to the verified track for access
  • you’ve received a grade on any open response assessment

Completing Assignments

Help with software and external resource sites

Many course teams add external resources from outside sites and software providers like DataCamp, Codio, Vocareum, Matlab and others to enhance their course experience.

The edX platform is built so that course teams can easily add on additional learning materials like these. However, since they are not part of the edX platform itself, the edX support team may have limited ability to support these assignments.

If you have any questions regarding these sites or other external software, please post in the course’s discussion forum where your course staff can reply. Since these were embedded and configured by your course staff, they will best be able to assist.

I can’t make a deadline; can I get an extension?

  • Self-paced courses
  • Instructor paced courses
  • What if I can’t pass before the end? Note: It is not possible to shift the final course end date or due dates on open response assessments as both of those are fixed by the instructor.

Self-paced courses

If your course is an active self-paced course, the end date has not passed, and you have missed the suggested due date on an assignment, you can shift your due dates into the future yourself. You can read more about self-paced courses in this article.

If you have missed a suggested due date, you can find the Shift due dates button on the course homepage or on the Dates tab.

It is not possible to shift a suggested due date before it has passed. You will only see the Shift due dates button if you have missed a suggested due date, not before.

If your course has ended and is archived, the suggested schedule cannot be shifted.

Instructor-paced courses

If your course is an instructor-paced course, the due dates are fixed. Your course staff may be able to make an exception to provide a short extension for assignments. Please reach out to your course team directly with any contact information they provide within the course, or post on the course discussion forum.

It is certainly possible to pass an edX course if you miss some assignments; however, coursework is progressive, so you should review and study what you may have missed.

Some courses automatically drop one or more of your lowest graded assignments. Check your course syllabus for important dates and more information.

I can’t pass the course before it ends

Depending on your course’s grading policy, you may still be able to earn a passing score if you miss a couple of assignments. However if you still have a significant portion of the course to complete, we encourage you to enroll in the course again in a future session. Most edX courses repeat, so you’ll have a chance to try again.

Why does this assignment I completed show as Due?

A graded assignment in your course may show as Due or not be marked as Completed if you are missing a green check mark in that section, but this does not affect your grading or certificate status. Sometimes advanced assignment types might not receive the green check mark, or there will be a delay before they display as completed (for example, proctored exams).

Your scores and your eligibility for the certificate are determined by the points you earn on graded assessments as reflected on your Progress page. You can qualify for the certificate as long as you earn a passing score before the course ends.

A missing check mark will not negatively affect your Progress score or prevent you from earning the verified certificate.

Why doesn’t the Submit button work?

If you are trying to submit an assignment but the Submit button remains inactive, first check the Dates tab in your course.

If it is a self-paced course and you have missed the suggested due date on an assignment, you will not be able to submit the assignment until you shift your due dates. You can read more about self-paced courses in this article.

If it is an instructor-paced course and you have missed the due date on an assignment, you will no longer be able to submit and the deadline remains fixed. You can read more about instructor-paced courses in this article.

If the course has ended and is now archived, the submit button may no longer work. You can read more about archived courses here.

Completing Essay Assignments

Assess peer responses

We invite you to answer a survey on improvements we may make to open response assessment grading in future.

In the peer assessment step of an open response assessment, you evaluate responses that were submitted by other learners in your course. The course team sets the requirement for how many peer assessments each learner is expected to complete.

How to Assess Peer Responses

At the top of the peer assessment step, there are counts of how many responses you are expected to assess and how many you have already assessed. For example, if you are required to perform 3 peer assessments and are about to start your first peer assessment, the count appears as ‘0 of 3 completed’ on the right side of the Grade your peers page.

Note: If there are no submitted responses available for grading, a status message indicates that no peer responses are currently available for you to assess, and that you should check back later.

On the Grade your peers page, the assignment prompt will be displayed and your peer’s response will be displayed below Peer response.

You assess other learners’ responses by selecting options in the rubric. Additionally, this step has a field below the rubric where you can provide comments about the learner’s response.

Note: In addition to a field for overall comments on a learner’s response, some peer assessments include Comments fields for individual criteria that allow you to enter up to 1000 characters. In some assessments, you must enter comments before you can submit the assessment.

After you have selected options in the rubric and provided comments about the response, select Submit peer grade. Then a modal will ask you, ‘Ready to submit this peer grade?’ You can select Submit peer grade to submit or Go back to keep working.

After you submit a peer assessment, if you’re required to grade more responses, a message will read, ‘Peer Assessment Successfully Submitted. Continue to submit peer assessments until you have completed the required number.’ Select Grade next peer to review the next peer’s response.

When you have completed the required number of peer assessments, the next step in the assignment becomes available.

Assess your own response

On this step, you’ll review your own submission based on criteria provided by the course team.

Respond to each of the criteria in the rubric, and then select Submit self grade. If the Submit self grade button doesn’t respond, try scrolling back up to check for an error message, ‘Rubric selection is required.’

Once all required fields have been completed, you can submit. Then a modal will ask you, ‘Ready to submit this peer grade?’ You can select Submit peer grade to submit or Go back to keep working.

When you have completed assessing your own response, the next step in the assignment becomes available. If there are no further steps, you can receive your score.

Can I preview or practice an open response assessment?

You can enroll in the DemoX course to preview and practice a peer-reviewed open response assessment in the Optional: Example Problem Types module and Open Response Assessment section.

The first assignment shows the original user interface, and the second one shows our newest user interface for the open response assessment.

How are open response assessments graded?

You may have to wait for your grade

When you submit a response to an ORA, your submission may be reviewed and graded by:

  • yourself. This is called the assess your response step. You’ll receive your self-assessment score as soon as you assess yourself.
  • your peers in the course. This is called the assess peers step. You will be required to review your peers’ submissions, and you might have to wait a while for a grade from your peers.
  • your instructors. This is called the staff grade step. You might have to wait a while for a grade from course staff. Note: Course staff can grade your ORA if you’re waiting on peer assessments. To request that staff manually grade your ORA, you should reach out to course staff through the course discussion forum.

About the rubric

Grading for every type of assessment step in an ORA assignment is done by comparing each response against the same set of guidelines, called a rubric. Every open response assessment has a rubric provided by the course team.

A rubric consists of several criteria and a set of options for each criterion.

Criteria

Each criterion describes characteristics that a response should have.

For example, “Determine if there is a unifying theme or main idea” or “Assess the content of the submission” are possible criteria for assessing a response.

Options

Each criterion has options which describe how well each response satisfies the criterion. The options are usually a range of ratings, with details to help you decide the rating, and an associated point value.

For example, this table shows the options for the criterion “Determine if there is a unifying theme or main idea.”

Option

Description

Points

Poor

Difficult to understand the main idea. Too brief or too repetitive to establish or maintain a focus.

0

Fair

Presents a unifying theme or main idea, but includes minor tangents. Stays somewhat focused on topic and task.

3

Good
