Presents a unifying theme or main idea without going off on tangents. Stays completely focused on topic and task.

5

When you assess a response, you evaluate the response, and for each criterion, select the option that best describes how well the response met that criterion.

How are peer assessment scores calculated?

Peer assessments are scored by criteria. The score for each criterion is the median (not the average) of the scores that each peer assessor gave that criterion. For example, if the Ideas criterion in a peer assessment receives 10 from one learner, 9 from a second learner, and 5 from a third learner, the score for that criterion is 9 (the median), not 8 (the average).

Your final score for a peer assessment is the sum of the median scores for all of the criteria.

For example, your response might receive the following scores from peer assessors.

Criterion Name

Peer 1

Peer 2

Peer 3

Median

Ideas (out of 10)

10

7

8

8

Content (out of 10)

7

9

8

8

Grammar (out of 5)

4

4

5

4

FINAL SCORE (out of 25)

20

To calculate the final score, the system adds the median score for each criterion:

Ideas median (8 out of 10) + Content median (8 out of 10) + Grammar median (4 out of 5)

= final score (20 out of 25)

Note, again, that your final score is not the median of the scores that each individual peer assessor gave the response. Your final score is the sum of the median scores for all of the criteria.

Note: If a staff grade is provided in the assignment, either because a staff assessment step was included or because a member of the course team graded your response to override inappropriate peer assessments, peer assessments are not used for grading. If a staff grade exists, it is always your final grade.

How do I submit my response to an open response assessment?

  • Submit your response: Individual
  • Submit your response: Team (What’s a team?) Submitting your response is usually the first step in an Open Response Assessment (ORA) assignment. You must submit it before the due date listed, ‘Your response is due on…’

Submit your response to an Open Response Assessment question

  • Click Create response to begin working on your response.
  • Read each question carefully: Some course teams include important information in the question, such as how long a response must be, or specific topics that your response must cover. The total word count for your response cannot be more than 10,000 words.
  • Enter your response: For each question, enter your response into the field under Your Response. In some assignments, you can submit images or other types of files. If you’re stuck on this step, please review Submit a File with Your Response.
  • When you have finished answering all of the questions, select Submit response. Then click Submit response on the dialog box to confirm, or Go back to cancel.
  • If you need more time, you can select Finish the rest later and then come back and submit them later. Your draft auto-saves every 5 seconds. After you submit your response, the next step, which is usually either assessment training or peer assessment, becomes available. However, you do not have to start the next step right away. If you want to stop working and come back later, just refresh or reopen your browser when you come back.

For more information about the steps in an ORA assignment, review Steps in an ORA assignment.

Submit a team response

If your course uses teams, there is a Teams tab on the course navigational menu.

Submitting a team response is largely the same as submitting an individual response, with the following exceptions:

  • Only one response can be submitted per team so you will have to coordinate with your team members on a shared response.
  • Your text response is only visible to you while drafting a response. When you or a team member submit a response, the submitting member’s response will be used for all members of the team. Because of this, we suggest coordinating with your teammates on a shared text response and who will submit it.
  • If file uploads are enabled for the assessment, all members of the team can upload files. Files can be seen and downloaded by all members of the team but can only be deleted by the uploading member. After your team submits a response, it must be graded by a staff member (see How are open response assessments graded?) Once graded, you can return to the assessment to see the team’s grade and feedback in the “Your Grade” section of the assessment.

Learn to assess responses

Some open response assessment (ORA) assignments include a training step, so that you can learn how to effectively assess responses for a later peer assessment step.

In a training step, you evaluate example responses using a provided rubric as a guide for grading. After you complete the grading, you are shown how the grades you gave differ from the grades that a member of the course team gave. The goal is to learn how to assess responses similar to the way that course staff would assess them, using the same rubric.

To Learn How to Assess Responses:

  • Read each sample response and the rubric carefully, then for each criterion, select the option that you think best reflect the response.
  • When you are satisfied with your assessment, select Compare your selections with the instructor’s selections.
    • If all of your selections are the same as the instructor’s selections, the next sample response opens automatically.
    • If any option that you select is not the same as the instructor’s selection, you see the response again, with a message indicating that your assessment differs from the instructor’s assessment.
  • If your assessment did not match the instructor’s assessment, review the response again and consider why the instructor assessed the response differently than you did. Continue to assess the example response until the options you select for all criteria match options selected by the instructor. When you have successfully assessed the sample responses, the next step in the assignment becomes available.

Steps in an Open Response Assessment (ORA) assignment

Open response assessments can have several possible steps, which appear in the order that you must complete them. You won’t be able to proceed to the next step until you’ve completed the current step.

After you submit your response, the required steps may be any combination of a training step, a self assessment step, a peer assessment step, or a staff grade step.

You can read more details about each step:

  • Create Response: This is usually the first step in an ORA where you submit your response to the prompt.
  • Learn to Assess Responses: This is a training step where you’ll practice grading some responses. The goal is to learn how to assess responses similar to the way that course staff would assess them, using the same rubric.
  • Peer Grading: You grade responses that other learners in the course have submitted, and other learners in the course grade your responses.
  • Self grading: In this step, you assess your own response, according to a provided rubric.
  • Staff Grade: Members of the course team assess your response. If you receive a staff grade for your assignment, it always overrides any peer assessment grades that you receive. Note: Course staff can grade your ORA if you’re waiting on peer assessments. To request that staff manually grade your ORA, you should reach out to course staff through the course discussion forum.

Submit a file with your ORA response

If your ORA assignment requires or allows you to submit images or other types of files, you will find a File Upload section below the response section. You can click ‘Drag and drop your file here or click to upload,’ or drag a file to that section of the page to upload your file.

Note: You can upload up to 20 files per assessment with a max per-file size of 500 MB. Generally, files can be in .jpg, .gif, .png, or .pdf format, but may differ based on instructor criteria for your assignment. The ORA will warn you if unsupported file types are supplied or max size is exceeded.

Upload files in your response:

  • Below the Your response field, either click ‘Drag and drop your file here or click to upload,’ or drag a file to that section of the page to upload your file.
  • If you’ve clicked to upload a file, in the dialog box that opens, select the file that you want to upload, and then select Open.
  • A dialog box will prompt you to add a text description for your file. You must enter a text description of the file in order to click Upload files.
  • All uploaded files will be listed with their description and file size below Uploaded Files. You can click Download files to download all the files you just uploaded, or click the Delete icon to delete them.
  • To upload additional files, repeat steps 1-3.
  • Click Submit response to submit your response with the uploaded files.

Submit files with your team ORA response

If your course uses teams, there is a Teams tab on the course navigational menu.

If your team ORA assignment requires or allows you to submit images or other types of files, you find two buttons below the response field: Choose Files one one side of the page, and Upload Files on the other side of the page.

The process for team ORA assignments is the same as uploading and submitting files in an individual ORA assignment with the following exceptions:

  • All members of the team can upload files. Files can be seen and downloaded by all members of the team but can only be deleted by the uploading member.
  • File upload count, type, and limits are still enforced on an individual basis. Your team submission is still valid even if, collectively, there are more uploaded files than the individual limit. You and your team can replace the files that you uploaded with different files until you submit your response. Make sure to coordinate as a team to ensure that the desired files are submitted as part of your response.

View your staff grade

In some open response assessments, a staff assessment step is included for a member of the course team to grade your responses.

Once course staff has graded your response, on the open response assessment page there will be a Complete indicator and a message, ‘Great work! All assignment steps are completed. You have completed this assignment. Review your grade and your assessment details.’

Click View my grades to review your grades from staff in more detail.

Your final grade displays the number of points you earned over the number of potential points. Below Staff Grade are details about how you were scored on each of the criteria in the grading rubric.

If a Staff Grade step exists in your assignment, you receive your final assignment grade when staff grading is complete, even if your response has not been assessed by the required number of peer reviewers.

Note: Course staff can grade your open response assignment even if a staff assessment step is not included in the assignment. This might happen if, for example, you receive peer assessments of your response that are inappropriate. In such cases, course staff can perform an assessment of your response that overrides any peer assessment grades. If a member of the course staff has graded your response, a Staff Grade section appears in the grading details for your assignment.

View your submitted response

You can view your own response in an ORA assignment at any time after you submit it.

Click the Create Response heading on the assignment page to review your submitted response.

Your response appears below Your Response and you can navigate to any additional steps in the open response assessment through the top navigation.

What’s an open response assessment (ORA)?

We invite you to answer a survey on improvements we may make to open response assessment grading in future.

An open response assessment (ORA for short) is an assessment type that allows you to submit:

  • written essays

  • file attachments such as a PDF or image (read more: Submit a file with an ORA response)

  • links to work outside of edX.org (such as a lab notebook) When you submit a response to an ORA, your submission may be reviewed and graded by:

  • yourself! This is called the self grading step.

  • your peers in the course. This is called the peer grading step. You will be required to review your peers’ submissions, and you might have to wait a while for a grade from your peers.

  • your instructors. This is called the staff grade step. You might have to wait a while for a grade from course staff. The required review steps may be any combination of self assessment, peer assessment, or staff grades. There may also be a training step to guide you through how to submit.

Read more

  • When is my open response assessment due?
  • Can I preview or practice an open response assessment?
  • When will I get my grade?
  • Why can’t I submit my peer reviews?
  • Why can’t I upload a file to my open response assessment?

When is my open response assessment due?

Open response assessments have due dates fixed by the course team. Each step has its own due date which cannot be extended.

On the open response assessment page, the due date of the step you have to complete next is indicated next to In Progress.

For your initial response, it will read, ‘Your response is due on…’ in your local time zone and M/DD/YYYY format.

If there is a peer grading step, it will read ‘Peer grading is due on…’ in your local time zone and M/DD/YYYY format.

If there is a self-grading step, it will read ‘Self assessment is due on…’ in your local time zone and M/DD/YYYY format.

On the Dates tab in your course, the due date will show on the schedule as Weekday, Month Day, Year and the 12-hour datetime. For example, the above would show as due Wed, Nov 6, 2023 6:59pm EST.

The due date is set in UTC by default, but you can ensure it’s showing in your local timezone by updating your Site Preferences.

When will I get my grade?

We invite you to answer a survey on improvements we may make to open response assessment grading in future.

When you submit a response to an open response assessment, you may have to wait for your submission may be reviewed and graded by:

  • yourself. This is called the self grading step. You’ll receive your self-assessment score as soon as you assess yourself and submit.
  • your peers in the course. This is called the peer grading step. You will be required to review the submissions of other learners, and you may have to wait for a grade from your peers. In most courses, you can expect to wait one week.
  • your instructors. This is called the staff grade step. You may have to wait for a grade from course staff. If you view your assignment, a message will indicate Waiting if your submission is pending grading by staff or a peer.

On a peer-graded assessment, if you’re waiting a message will read, ‘You have graded the required number of your peers. You are waiting for your peers to finish grading your work. You will have completed this step once your peers have finished grading your response. Check back periodically to see if there is an update. Optionally, you may also grade more peers while you wait.’

On a staff-graded assessment, if you’re waiting a message will read, ‘All assignment steps are completed. Your final grade is not ready yet. Your final grade will be ready once the instructor has finished grading your response. Check back periodically to see if there is an update.’

Note: Course staff can grade your ORA if you’re waiting on peer assessments. To request that staff manually grade your ORA, you should reach out to course staff through the course discussion forum.

Create an edX Account

Can I create a new account using the same email address?

Please do not delete your account if you want to create a new account using the same email address or use the email address on a different account. Once an account has been deleted, its email address cannot be used again on any edX account.

Per the edX honor code, you should only remain active on one edX account, so only use one account going forward.

If you would like to use the same email address on a new account, you will first need change your email address on the existing account to any unused email address.

You can edit your email address from your Account Settings. Under Account Information, click on “Edit” next to “Email address (Sign in).” Type in your new email address, and click “Save.” There will be a message “We’ve sent a confirmation message to ‘email address’.” Click the link in the message to confirm your updated email address.

​

Once updated, your original email address will be available to use to create a new account.

Can I merge accounts?

No, it’s not possible for us to merge two different accounts on edX.

This means that any progress, payment or enrollment history from one account cannot be transferred or merged onto another.

The edX honor code prohibits using multiple edX accounts. If you have accidentally created an additional account or had a new account created by your school or employer, it is not possible to merge this into another account. We recommend that you choose just one of the accounts to use going forward.

Deleting Your Account and Personal Data

How do I delete my account and personal data on edX?

You may request that your edX account and data be deleted by clicking Delete my account in your edX account settings.

Deletion of your account and personal data is permanent and cannot be undone. edX will not be able to recover your account or the data that is deleted. Any certificates earned will be deleted.

It is not possible to open a new edX account with the same email address or username as the deleted account.

How do I delete my account and personal data on edX if my edX account is linked to a social media account (e.g., Facebook or Google)?

First, follow the instructions for unlinking your edX account from the associated Social account.

Second, because we require a password to authorize account deletion, follow the instructions for resetting your password to create a separate password for your edX account.

Third, request that your edX account and data be deleted by clicking Delete my account in your edX account settings and provide your password when prompted.

What happens when my account is deleted?

After deletion, edX will permanently deactivate your edX account, remove the active profile, retire your username, and remove you from edX email lists. edX will also permanently delete your edX account profile and the personal data we use to identify you on our system. Note that there are some exceptions to deletion, as further described in our Privacy Policy (for example, course data will be archived to serve the mission for scientific research, and records used for financial reporting, audit, and compliance purposes must be retained).

Can my account be retrieved after I delete it?

Deleting your edX account is a permanent action that cannot be reversed. edX will not be able to recover your account or the data that is deleted. You will no longer be able to use your account to take courses on the edX app, edX.org, or any other site hosted by edX. This includes access to edX.org from your employer or university system and access to private sites offered by MIT Open Learning, Wharton Online, and Harvard Medical School.

After clicking “Delete my account” is my account immediately deleted?

Account deletion, including removal from email lists, may take a few weeks to fully process through our system. If you want to opt-out of emails before then, please unsubscribe from the footer of any email. Despite this processing period, account and data deletion is still permanent, so your account and data will not be able to be recovered once you’ve submitted a deletion request.

What happens to my certificates if I delete my account?

You will lose access to verified certificates and other program credentials like MicroMasters certificates. If you want to make a copy of these for your records before proceeding with deletion, follow the instructions for printing or downloading a certificate.

Can I create a new account if I change my mind?

You are always welcome back to continue learning at edX. Due to the fact that your previous account has been permanently deleted, you will need to start fresh with a brand-new account with a different email address. You will not be able to use the same email or username for your new account.

What if I do not have an account but provided edX with my contact details for information on its courses, programs, news, and related products and services OR specific courses or programs?

To start the deletion process, please send an email to info@edx.org from the same email address you provided to receive such information.

How do I confirm my email?

After registering, you will receive a confirmation email at the email address you entered during registration from no-reply@registration.edx.org. Click on the link in the email to confirm your email address.

If you have recently registered but can’t find the confirmation email in your email inbox, please read Why haven’t I received my confirmation email?

If you can access your account but no longer have the confirmation email, you may instead click the link in a password reset email message to confirm. Either click “Reset Password” in your Account Settings or from the edx.org log in page, click Forgot my password. Either will send the reset message to your registered email address.

Clicking the link in the password reset message will confirm your email. (You don’t have to reset your password.)

How do I join edX?

edX courses are open to everyone. All you need is access to a computer, an internet connection and a desire to learn.

To begin, please register for an edX account. You will need to:

  • Enter your email address
  • Enter your full name
  • (Your full name will appear on any certificates that you earn.)
  • Create a public username for the forums (cannot have spaces)
  • Note: You cannot change your username once it has been created.
  • Create a password
  • Select your country
  • Select Create an account for free When you find a course that interests you in the course catalog, you can enroll in the course by selecting the Enroll button on the course About page.

Registering for an edX account is completely free! You can view many of our courses for free for a limited time, or you can choose to pay a fee to get access to graded assignments and pursue a certificate.

Earning Credit

As a student with a disability, who do I contact for help?

If you are a student with a disability, please open a support request through edX using one of the three following methods:

- Via the Help button present on every page of the Help Center.
- Via the [Contact Us](https://www.edx.org/contact-us) form
- Via [accessibility@edx.org](mailto:accessibility@edx.org)

Can I receive college credit or credit hours for my course?

While most courses don’t offer academic credit directly through edX at this time, we partner with a variety of institutions to offer credit-eligible courses. Please read below to learn more about how earning credit works through each of these partners.

  • MicroMasters Programs: MicroMasters programs are a series of graduate level courses from top universities designed to advance your career. They provide deep learning in a specific career field and are recognized by employers for their real job relevance. Students may apply to the university offering credit for the MicroMasters certificate and, if accepted, can pursue an accelerated and less expensive Master’s Degree. Credit is awarded after acceptance to the Masters program.
  • MicroBachelors programs: Our MicroBachelors™ programs are a series of career path focused courses that upon completion will award academic credit. After completing all courses in the program in the verified track and receiving a certificate, you may elect to receive credit from our credit partner at no additional charge.
  • edX undergraduate credit courses: EdX offers a few individual courses which learners can purchase academic credit after successful completion on the verified track. The individual credit courses which may be available are:
    • MIT - Introduction to Computer Science and Programming Using Python (Charter Oak State College Credit) Some educational institutions or organizations may offer you credit based on edX verified certificates. Students receive verified certificates after they earn a passing grade in a course that offers these certificates. If you’re currently associated with a college or organization, contact that organization directly to find out about credit options for verified certificates.

Undergraduate credit through MicroBachelors programs

Our new MicroBachelors™ programs are a series of career path focused courses that upon completion will award academic credit.

Each program comes with real, transferable college credit from edX’s university credit partner, Thomas Edison State University. Combined with previous credit you may have already collected or plan to get in the future, MicroBachelors programs put you on a path to earning a full bachelor’s degree.

After completing all courses in the program in the verified track and receiving a certificate, you may elect to receive credit from our credit partner at no additional charge. Please refer to each program page to see if credit is available yet. Please refer to this FAQ for more information on how credit works.

Learn more on the MicroBachelors™ program page.

edX Basics

Can I start for free and pay later?

Yes, you absolutely can start your course in the audit track and then upgrade to the verified track later, so long as the upgrade deadline has not passed.

How do I know the upgrade deadline?

Please note that upgrading grants you access to graded assignments required for the certificate. The sooner you upgrade, the more time you will have to work towards earning the certificate.

In self-paced courses, the upgrade deadline is usually a short time before the course’s final end date. In instructor-paced courses, the upgrade deadline may be much sooner.

Where do I find the upgrade deadline?

You can find the deadline on the right side of the course homepage under Important Dates listed as the Verification Upgrade Deadline.

If there’s no upgrade deadline listed there, then the upgrade deadline has passed.

It is not possible to upgrade after the deadline or the course end. If the course’s final end date is approaching or has already passed, it will be listed under Upcoming Dates as well.

Can I take a course for free?

There are a few courses on edX.org that you may browse without payment or without free access expiring*.

  • CS50’s Introduction to Computer Science: You can read more on the CS50 FAQs: How do I get my free CS50 Certificate?
  • Select Stanford University courses are available for free and offer Continuing Medical Education credits instead of certificates.
  • HarvardX courses Mechanical Ventilation for COVID-19 and Ventilación mecánica para pacientes con COVID-19 are available for free and offer no certificates.
  • OxfordX courses (English) International Standards on Freedom of Expression and Safety of Journalists and (Spanish) Normas Internacionales para la Libertad de Expresión y la Seguridad de los Periodistas are available for free and offer no certificates. These courses are also available in other languages.
  • PrincetonX courses are available for free and offer no certificates.
  • HP courses award a coupon, posted on the enrollment page below the title, that you may use to upgrade the course for free.
  • Select ACCA courses are available for free and offer no certificates:
    • Introduction to Bookkeeping
    • Intermediate Bookkeeping
    • Intermediate Management Accounting
    • Introduction to Management Accounting Please note: edX verified certificates of achievement are available on the paid verified track.

*Disclaimer: Availability is subject to change. Please review the course’s enrollment page and outline for more information.

Can I transfer to another session?

If you are not able to finish the course before the end date you can certainly enroll in the same course again in a future session, but you will need to pay again for the next session. Most edX courses do repeat.

If you had paid for the verified certificate track you would need to pay again to be eligible for a certificate in the new session. The upgrade fee helps support our mission, and does not transfer to future sessions. Work completed in the previous session also will not carry over.

edx.org status

If you are experiencing a problem with our website, check status.edx.org for status updates! If we’re aware of any issue with the site, we’ll post there.

Frequently Asked Questions (FAQs) about Data Protection

  • What is GDPR?
  • The term “GDPR” refers to the General Data Protection Regulation, a European Union (EU) and United Kingdom (UK) law about data privacy and security. Among other things, it regulates the international transfer of personal information across EU borders and establishes certain individual rights relative to personal information.
  • What is “CCPA”?
  • The term “CCPA” refers to the California Consumer Privacy Act of 2018, a U.S. privacy law that governs the collection and processing of California consumers’ personal information by businesses and, amongst other things, secures privacy rights for California consumers.
  • What data does edX have about me?
  • The information collected from users and visitors to edx.org is described in the Privacy Policy (under the heading “Information That edX Collects From and About You (including Personal Information)”).
  • Much of the information that edX stores about you is available via your edX account. This includes your profile information, account settings, and your dashboard. The dashboard lists all current and archived courses in which you are enrolled and includes links to any course and program certificates you may have earned. Through the dashboard, you can also access the course content and view your work and performance on assessments and your posts in public forum discussions. If you need assistance locating any of the information available via your edX account, please let us know.
  • As explained in the edX Cookie Policy, cookies and other similar tracking technologies are also used to track information about your activity on the edX website (for example, which pages were visited). Please read the “Types of Cookies Used by edX” section of the Cookie Policy for more information on the different types of cookies used and their purpose(s).
  • Does edX collect biometric data?
  • At this point neither edX nor any of our data processing vendors collect any data or conducts any specific technical processing (e.g., no 3D faceprint technology) that would qualify as biometric, except with respect to certain courses where facial recognition is used for identity verification where the Member has required that exams have remote proctoring. Learners are asked for specific consent for remote proctoring in applicable courses.
  • Does edX sell or give my personal information to advertisers?
  • As described in the edX Privacy Policy and Cookie Policy, in some cases, we disclose Identifiers and Usage Data to business partners in order to deliver ads that are more relevant to you and to measure and report on the effectiveness of our advertising campaigns. Under the CCPA, some of these data disclosures may be broadly considered a “sale” or “share” of information, even though these activities do not fit what most people would understand “sale” or “share” to mean. Except for these types of disclosures, we do not sell or share your information. To opt out of the sale or share of your personal information, please visit the “Your Privacy Choices” link at the bottom of each page on the edX site.
  • How do I get removed from the edX marketing lists?
  • When you first create an account on edX, you will receive a welcome email that contains links for unsubscribing from edX marketing emails and updating subscription preferences. All further marketing email you receive from us will contain unsubscribe links. You may also contact our support team for help.
  • Can I ask edX to delete my account and personal data?
  • Yes, you may request that your edX account and personal information be deleted by clicking the“Delete my account” button in your edX account settings. You can learn more about account and data deletion at the edX Help Center page for Account Deletion FAQs.

  • edX partners with several third parties to help us run the edX website and to track and understand learner activity on the site and how learners interact with our social media. We encourage you to visit the websites for these third parties and read their terms of use, privacy policies and, as applicable, information about tracking technologies (several are linked below). This list is not exhaustive and will be updated from time to time.

I am worried about the safety of a learner or instructor and threats of suicide or self harm - what can I do?

You may encourage the learner or instructor to seek free, confidential 24/7 support in the US by calling the National Suicide Prevention Lifeline at 1-800-273-TALK (8255) or suicidepreventionlifeline.org. Additional resources include:

  • Crisis text line at crisistextline.org
  • Live chat at imalive.org
  • Worldwide support by Befrienders at befrienders.org

A learner or instructor is harassing me - what can I do?

Please report your concern directly to the course team or contact us to report your concern, and we will notify the appropriate course team (or the course provider’s leadership, as appropriate). The course providers (and their respective course teams) are on the front lines of managing all instructor and learner course conduct in accordance with the edX Rules for Online Conduct and Strictly Prohibited Items. In addition, some course providers have participation policies, escalation protocols, and other institution resources for managing course behavior. All individuals are expected to abide by these terms and can be removed from a course or have their edX account terminated if they violate these terms.

Responding to Inappropriate Learner Behavior

Please report your concern directly to the course team or contact us to report your concern, and we will notify the appropriate course team (or the course provider’s leadership, as appropriate).

The institutions providing the courses (and their respective Course teams) are on the front lines of managing all instructor and learner course conduct in accordance with the edX Rules for Online Conduct and Strictly Prohibited Items. In addition, some course providers have participation policies, escalation protocols, and other institution resources for managing course behavior. All individuals are expected to abide by these terms and can be removed from a course or have their edX account terminated if they violate these terms.

What is my dashboard?

Your dashboard gives you access to the courses and programs that you have enrolled in and information such as course start and end dates, certificates, and email settings.

Access your dashboard by opening the dropdown menu next to your username at the top of any page and selecting Dashboard. The URL is home.edx.org

If you have an account through an enterprise organization, you will see additional dashboard options. Always choose the enterprise dashboard when enrolling in new courses to ensure any enterprise offers and completion tracking are correctly applied.

Courses page

The Courses page lists all the courses you have enrolled in, whether the course is current or has ended. This page provides links to your courses and allows you to change course settings, such as email settings.

Programs page

The Programs page lists any programs (such as MicroMasters, MicroBachelors, Professional Certificate, or XSeries programs), that you are enrolled in, or that are associated with courses you are taking. Programs appear on this page if you are enrolled in any course that is part of that program. Click on a program to go to the program details page.

edX for Business: Additional

Integrating edX with 360Learning

Prerequisites:

To successfully integrate your edX course catalog with 360learning, you need to have:

  • An agreement with edX covering access to the edX course catalog for your learners.

  • An agreement with 360learning covering access to the 360learning LMS/LXP product. Configuration Steps:

  • Generate your credentials in the edX Admin Portal:

    • login to the edX Admin Portal go to the “Settings” and click on “API Credentials”
    • Next, click “Generate API Credentials” and send the newly generated API credentials to your 360learning team.

  • Reach out to your 360learning team to complete setup using your newly generated edX API credentials. Technical Support:

If you need further assistance or have questions about 360learning, please contact your 360learning representative.

If you need assistance or have questions about edX, you may contact the edX Enterprise Customer Success Team via email at: enterprise-support@edx.org

  • An agreement with IBM covering access to the IBM YourLearning service. Configuration Steps:

  • Generate your credentials in the edX Admin Portal:

Integrating edX with Workday

Overview

Integration with edX and Workday is accomplished by setting up configuration reports that are pushed into your SFTP or email server at a scheduled time. Upon processing the files in your server, Workday collects the resulting files to create learning items in your Workday instance.

Prerequisites:

To successfully integrate your edX course catalog with Workday, you need to have:

  • An agreement with edX covering access to the edX course catalog for your learners.
  • An agreement with Workday covering access to the Workday LMS.
  • An SFTP or email server to receive edX files: catalog and learner’s progress report.
  • An Engineering team to process edX files.
  • A Workday Account Manager to guide you through the integration.

Configuration Steps:

Step 1: Choose File Transfer method

edX can send files via SFTP (secure file transfer protocol) or email server. Discuss with your engineering team the most suitable transfer method. SFTP is the most commonly used method.

Step 2: Set up Report Configuration in the edX Admin Portal

Visit Enterprise Admin Portal, and find the “Reporting Configurations” option. Add a reporting configuration according to the delivery method chosen in step 1.

SFTP sample delivery method:

Step 3: Mapping edX Catalog fields with Workday fields

The edX catalog file includes a list of courses. For every course, edX provides a list of fields describing the course (metadata). These fields are used to display course information in Workday LMS.

With the help of your Workday account manager choose the fields that are most relevant to your team. Workday account managers must map these fields with their equivalent in their system.

Here are the most common ones:

  • active: Either the course is active or not.
    • Mapping field in Workday: Status
  • end_date: The course end date.
  • enrollment_url: The external content link in edX. This field is mandatory to be able to access and enroll in edX courses.
    • Mapping field in Workday: Content URL
  • image_url: The static URL for the about page image for the course.
    • Mapping filed in Workday: Image
  • Key: An identifier for the course. For example, RITx+PM9003x.
  • short_description: The short description of the course and its content, HTML format
    • Mapping field in Workday: Description
  • Title: The title of the content item. For example, “Introduction to Plasma Physics”.
    • Mapping field in Workday: Lesson and Title. Sample fields:
Step 4: Customer creates Middleware software

In most cases your engineering team will create a middleware program (script) to process the received file. The process requires extracting the edX fields chosen in the previous step to create a new file that Workday will receive.

Workday team should inform you how they are going to access this new processed file.

Step 5: Workday creates learning items

Workday pulls the files created in Step 4 and processes them in their LMS to create learning items. These items are accessed by your employees to find learning opportunities.

Sample View in Workday:

Summary Integration Diagram

Technical Support

If you need assistance or have questions about edX, you may contact the edX Enterprise Customer Success Team via email at: enterprise-support@edx.org or via the “Help” button in this page

My system isn’t listed!

“I don’t see my system on the list?”

While we would love to say that we support all Learning Management Systems, Learning Experience Systems, Human Resources Information Systems, and more, unfortunately this isn’t the case. However, if your particular system is not listed, do not despair! Work through the following steps to determine what might be possible.

  1. Determine if your system is “bespoke”

“Bespoke” systems are solutions that an organization designs, develops, deploys, and operates on its own. In a bespoke system scenario, you likely have an engineering team (internal or contracted) responsible for construction and maintenance of the technology. Many bespoke systems have been deployed for several years or more, since organizations these days typically select “off-the-shelf” products for their HR and L&D needs. Your engineers for the bespoke system would utilize edX’s enterprise integration toolkit in order to enable discovery, access, and reporting of edX courses within that environment.

  1. Determine if your system is “off-the-shelf”

“Off-the-shelf” systems are easy to identify, because you pay a vendor to design, develop, and deploy the product, while you focus your time and attention on operating it. Most LMS/LXP/HRIS/etc. systems are “off-the-shelf” these days, and this means when you want an enhancement made, you need to work with either your vendor’s product team, your vendor’s technical services team (aka professional solutions, etc.), or perhaps an independent consultant.

There are two considerations with respect to achieving an integration in an “off-the-shelf” scenario:

The vendor does the work

Having your vendor perform the necessary integration work is the ideal scenario. As mentioned previously, edX offers a complete enterprise integration toolkit which enables discovery, access, and reporting for edX courses within an external system. Most vendors are eager to enable these types of third-party content provider integrations within their products. Speak with your vendor about the possibility for enabling edX within their solution. Alternatively, put us on the phone with your vendor and we can discuss various technical approaches and mechanisms on your behalf.

edX does the work

Having edX modify our own platform to connect with a vendor’s LMS/LXP/HRIS/etc. solution is possible, but not the ideal scenario. The edX product team would evaluate the vendor’s system to determine if/where a potential integration fits on edX’s roadmap, and if it’s of enough strategic importance to warrant doing the work. If approved, the job must be prioritized against other roadmap items and then scheduled. Depending on engineering resources, it can take months, and often entire quarters, before a new integrated channel is delivered.

edX for Business: Admin Assigned Content

Can an administrator change their mind about an assignment?

This article explains the context in which an administrator changes their mind about an assignment

Yes, an administrator can decide to cancel an assignment at any time between creating the assignment and the corresponding enrollment being completed by the learner. To cancel an assignment, administrators can:

  • Log into their administrator portal.
  • Click “learner credit management” on the left-hand navigation bar.
  • Select the budget from which the course was assigned and scroll down to see the “activity” tab.
  • Each assignment will be displayed with an option to “remind” the learner, or “cancel” the assignment. Should an assignment be canceled, the learner will receive an error should they attempt to enroll in the previously assigned course. The “allocated” portion of the learner credit will be returned to the remaining learner credit budget balance to be spent on a different enrollment.

For more information or assistance with Learner Credit, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

How do I assign courses with learner credit?

This article explains how to assign courses with Learner Credit

The ability for an administrator to assign courses through a learner credit budget is an option available to enterprise organizations that have selected this feature. This configuration is specifically applied to the respective Learner Credit Budget.

For organizations that opt for administrator-controlled learner credit budgets, the process is as follows:

  • Course Assignment: When administrators assign a course to a learner, the price of the course at the time of assignment is reserved from the learner credit budget. Administrators can assign courses to learners by logging into their administrator portal, clicking, “learner credit management” on the left-hand navigation bar, and then selecting the budget from which the course should be assigned. On the individual budget’s page, there will be a “assign course” call-to-action. Administrators can also initiate an assignment by selecting the desired course directly from the bottom of the budget’s page in the “courses” section.
  • Learner Notification: The learners are informed of the course assignment and are guided through the enrollment process. They receive a notification that guides them seamlessly through the steps to enroll in the assigned course. For more information or assistance with Learner Credit, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

How does learner credit handle different currencies?

This article reviews how Learner Credit handles different currencies.

All learner credit budgets are uniformly denominated in USD. This means that when dealing with learner credit, there is a common currency shared across the platform. This simplifies financial transactions, making them transparent and straightforward for all users.

Course Pricing in USD:

All courses available for purchase with learner credit, including Enterprise open courses and executive education offerings, have their prices set in USD. This ensures that learners have a consistent and clear understanding of the pricing structure, eliminating the need for complex currency conversions or calculations.

Administrator controlled Learner credit budgets:

For learner credit budgets configured to be administrator-controlled (top-down), the process is as follows:

  • Course Assignment: When administrators assign a course to a learner, the USD price at the time of assignment is reserved from the learner credit budget. This reservation ensures that the budget has sufficient funds to cover the cost of enrollment.
  • Learner Notification: The targeted learners are informed of the assignment and guided through the enrollment process.
  • Learner Credit Budget Deduction: Upon successful enrollment, the USD price of the course at the time of assignment is deducted from the budget. For more information or assistance, with Learner Credit submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

How many learners can be assigned a course at once?

This article explains how many Learners can be assigned to a single course at the same time

Course assignment through learner credit can be used for a single learner or for dozens of learners at the same time. The number of learners that can be assigned will be restricted by the remaining balance of an organizations’ learner credit budget.

For more information or assistance with Learner Credit Course Assignment, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

What does a learner need to do in response to an assignment?

This article explains the process Learners will need to follow after being given an assignment

When assigned a course by their administrator, an enterprise learner will receive an email notification informing them of the assigned course and prompting them to enroll. The learner will also be able to view all assigned courses via the enterprise learner dashboard.

The learner will be brought through the necessary steps to complete the enrollment including:

  • Logging in or registering.
  • Seeing the course on their enterprise learner dashboard.
  • Confirming their desire to enroll.
  • Accepting terms and conditions. The learner credit associated with the assignment will not be marked as “spent” until the learner has successfully accepted terms and conditions.

For more information or assistance with Learner Credit, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

What happens if my learner never enrolls following an assignment?

This article reviews what happens if a Learner never enrolls after an assignment.

If a learner does not enroll into their assigned course within 90 days, the assignment will be automatically canceled and the learner credit reserved from the learner credit budget at the time that the administrator will be returned to the organization’s learner credit balance to be used again.

For more information or assistance with Learner Credit, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

When is credit deducted from a learner credit budget in the assignment process?

This article explains when credit is deducted from the Learner Credit budget within the assignment process.

Creating an assignment as an administrator does not deduct credit permanently from a selected learner credit budget. No funds are “spent” until a learner completes their enrollment into an assigned course.

Between assignment and enrollment, the courses’ value in learner credit is moved to an “allocated state”, which ensures that organizations cannot spend past a zero-dollar balance. This also prevents learners from finding themselves unable to enroll due to a completely depleted budget.

For more information or assistance Learner Credit, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

For more information or assistance with this topic, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

I can’t access the Administrator Dashboard, why is that?

This article explains how to troubleshoot 404 errors in the Administrator’s Dashboard.

Encountering a 404 error while navigating the Administrators Dashboard can be frustrating. This error typically occurs when the user account lacks the necessary permissions to access certain pages. Below are some steps to help you troubleshoot and resolve this issue.

  1. Verify Account Permissions

First, ensure that you are logged into the correct edX account with the appropriate permissions. A common cause of 404 errors is using an account that hasn’t been granted access to specific administrative functions.

  1. Check the User Avatar

To confirm you are using the correct account, glance at the top right corner of your dashboard. Here, you should see your user avatar. If you have previously logged into a different account, the avatar displayed might not be yours. Click on the avatar to reveal a dropdown menu that shows the currently logged-in account.

  1. Switch Accounts if Necessary

If you identify that you are logged into the wrong account, click on the “Sign Out” option in the dropdown menu. Once signed out, re-enter your credentials for the correct edX account and log in. Ensure that the avatar now reflects the correct account.

  1. Contact Support for Further Assistance

If the issue persists even after confirming that you are using the correct account, there may be other underlying permission issues. In this case, it’s best to contact edX support for further assistance. Provide them with details about the error and the steps you’ve already taken to troubleshoot.

By following these steps, you should be able to resolve most 404 errors and regain access to the Administrators Dashboard. Remember, having the right permissions is crucial for smooth navigation and operation within the dashboard.

For more information or assistance with this issue, please submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable).

edX for Business: Analytics

Can I see only a specific learner’s # of daily sessions, learning hours and completions?

At this time it is not possible to see a specific learner’s daily sessions or learning hours. You can view a learner’s completions by searching the learner’s email in the Learner Progress Report.

How can I download the information from the Analytics tab for internal use?

This article explains Downloading Analytics tab for internal use.

You can download a filtered report of tables from the Enrollments, Engagements, or Completions tabs by selecting “Download CSV” on the bottom right hand side of the specific data table. Use any of the filters (i.e. Start Date, End Date, Date Granularity, or Calculation Type) to hone in your data parameters.

For additional questions specific to Downloading Analytics data, please submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

What type of information can I see on the Analytics tab?

This article explains the types of information found on the Analytics tab.

There are five different tabs of data: Enrollments, Engagements, Completions, Courses, and Skills. Each tab contains a summary for your enterprise at the top.

Use the filters, Start date, end date, Date Granularity and Calculation to customize your report output.

On the lower right hand corner of any tab, click “DOWNLOAD CSV” to download raw report data.

PRO TIP: Hovering over the plot area reveals an action menu. Clicking on the camera icon , you can save the plot as a .png file and use it in presentations and reports.

For additional questions specific to Analytics data, please submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

Frequently Asked Questions: Canvas

Frequently Asked Questions:

  • When is an assignment created in Canvas? Is it done when the first student finishes a quiz?
  • All assignments have the same name “Section Quiz“. Is it possible to inherit the name of each quiz so that this appears in Canvas?
  • Are assignments weighted or do they all weigh the same in the final grade?
  • All section quizzes are marked “Do not count this towards the final grade“. How is the final grade calculated and migrated into Canvas?
  • When are grades synced between edX and Canvas?

1. When is an assignment created in Canvas? Is it done when the first student finishes a quiz?

A: Yes, an assignment on Canvas is created when the first student in the course completes the corresponding graded subsection in the edX course. The integrated channel takes care of determining if the particular subsection has been transmitted previously to the individual customer’s Canvas instance. If it finds the grade in the integrated course then it posts a submission for the student being transmitted, otherwise it creates it.

2. All assignments have the same name “Section Quiz“. Is it possible to inherit the name of each quiz so that this appears in Canvas?

A: The assignment should have the name of the subsection on edX. We can investigate exactly where the field comes from but we suspect that the integrated course has these subsections named “Section Quiz”.

3. Are assignments weighted or do they all weigh the same in the final grade?

A: The exact pointing of the subsections is based on the amount of points possible and received on the edX course. Quizzes are omitted from the student’s final grade due to the fact that we also transmit a final edX course grade as an assignment, in the same way we report subsections. Because both the subsections and the completion reporting represent the same objects on Canvas, they would both affect the student’s Total grade on Canvas in the same weighted way. Students should be able to view how many points the quiz was worth and how many points they received on it, but they will not change the student’s grade in the course- the completion reporting will.

4. All section quizzes are marked “Do not count this towards the final grade“. How is the final grade calculated and migrated into Canvas?

A: As per the explanation above, final grades are reported by the completion reporting transmissions which are separate from the subsection reporting. However the method by which these tasks are triggered and transmitted are parallel. The integrated channel detects if the “Final Grade” assignment has been generated in the Canvas course, creates if not, and submits on behalf of the student.

5. When are grades synced between edX and Canvas?

A: Grades should be synced as the students go through the course on edX. We perform a catch all, bulk transmit that is intended to ensure any failed or lost transmissions are sent over, 4 times a day.

edX for Business: Code Management

Can I enroll my learners in a complete program? We use enrollment codes.

This article discusses how to enroll in a program using codes.

Learners can enroll in a complete program, however, they must enroll in each course singularly. If a program has 3 courses contained in it, the learner must have 3 single-use codes to enroll in each course to complete the program.

Upon completion of the required program courses, learners will earn the program credential offered by the program.

For more information or assistance with this function, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

An employee has left our company. How do I get their license back?

This article explains how to return an unused license when an employee leaves the company.

If a code has been assigned to a learner who is no longer employed with your company or part of your organization, you can revoke the code as long as it has not been redeemed.

*Please note: Codes cannot be revoked if they have already been redeemed. There is no way to revoke a code or remove a learner’s code enrollment once it has been redeemed to enroll in a course.

To revoke a code, please follow these steps;

  1. Navigate to the Code Management tool in the Administrator Dashboard.

  2. Select the code batch (if you have multiple batches) you wish to work with.

  3. After clicking into the specific code batch, filter for ‘Unredeemed codes’. You will see options to ‘Remind’ or ‘Revoke’ the specific code for a user. Click REVOKE.

PRO TIP: This action can be also be done in bulk by selecting multiple users/codes.

  1. Check the check box to the left of the users you wish to revoke.

  2. Use the BULK ACTION option at the top right of the Coupon Details screen section to revoke multiple licenses at once.

For more information or assistance with this process, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable).

How do I assign a code?

Code assignment is managed through the Administrator Dashboard under the Code Management section.

After you have clicked on the coupon batch that you would like to draw from, you can choose to individually assign or bulk assign codes using a CSV UTF-8 file with learner email addresses. You can also copy and paste a number of learner emails into the Code Assignment function – one email address per line.

How do I download my codes?

You are able to download your codes through the Code Management Tool on your Admin Dashboard. We do not recommend this method for code management as it can result in codes being assigned to multiple people or being used by one person while assigned to someone else. However, to download these codes, simply

  • Go to the Code Management Tool
  • Click on the code batch in question
  • Select whether you would like to download Unassigned Codes, Unredeemed Codes, or Redeemed codes using the dropdown Code Status filter on the left side.
  • Once you have selected which filter you want to use, click the “DOWNLOAD FULL REPORT (CSV)” button on the right side

How do I know if a learner successfully unenrolled for a refund?

Learners have the ability to unenroll through their course dashboard. If the enrollment occurred within the previous 14 days (2 days for Professional Education Courses) an automatic refund will be granted to the organization. If the enrollment occurred outside of that window, no refund will be granted to the organization.

If your organization has a data agreement with edX, you may go to the Learner Progress Report and click the “Download full report (CSV), where you can view the below related learner enrollment data to advise or confirm a learner’s refund status:

To review the full report, click “Download full report (CSV)” on the right hand side:

Related learner enrollment data:

unenrollment_date - The date the learner unenrolled from their course.

unenrollment_end_within_date - The date the learner must unenroll by, in order to receive a refund on the enrollment. This date is traditionally 14 days from the enrollment date or the course start date, whichever is later.

is_refunded - Whether or not the learner received a refund on their enrollment

course_list_price - The original price of the course, before any discount were applied

Other data that may be useful to filter by when searching a specific learner:

coupon_code - The code used for the specific enrollment

user_email - the learner’s account email

If a learner used single-use enrollment codes to enroll in a course, and unenroll within the 14 day refund window, they will be able to redeem the same code for one future enrollment, as long as the code expiration date has not passed. Code usage still requires to be used within the restrictions of the code catalog and expiration date. Admins may search the learner’s email in the Code Management tool to confirm they have an additional use available for the same code.

Please note that an additional code use will only be issued within the refund window for single-use codes. Administrators looking for replacement codes for multi-use codes, where a code refund is executed by a learner within the aforementioned 14-day refund window, should reach out to our Customer Success team via the “Submit a Request” button below.

How do I make and use a template?

You can make both Code Assignment and Code Reminder templates via the Code Management Tool on your Administrator Dashboard.

To make a Code Assignment Template, navigate to the assign function either by clicking on the bulk assign option or to assign a specific course. Begin filling out your template. This can include a customized template name, subject link, greeting, and closing. The body cannot be edited as this is where code details populate. Make sure you click “Save Template” in the bottom right.

You can also create a Code Reminder Template by going to the remind function, either bulk remind or individual remind, and create your template the same way.

Once your templates are saved, you assign and remind codes using these templates. Simply click “From Template” after navigating to the Assign or Remind functions and select a template from the dropdown list.

How do I revoke a code?

You can revoke a code by navigating to the Code Management tool in the Administrator Dashboard. After clicking into the specific code batch, you will need to filter for ‘Unredeemed codes’. You will see options to ‘Remind’ or ‘Revoke’ the specific code for a user. These actions can be done in bulk by selecting multiple codes.

Once you revoke a code, the learner will no longer be able to use it to enroll in a course. Please note that a code can only be revoked before it has been redeemed. There is no way to revoke a code once it has been redeemed for a course.

If a learner leaves my organization or company, can we get their code back?

This article explains how to retrieve a code when a learner leaves your organization or company.

If a learning code has been assigned to a learner who is no longer employed with your company or part of your organization, you can revoke the code as long as it has not been redeemed. This process ensures that valuable resources are available for current employees or members who need them.

Revoking an Unused Code

To revoke an unused code, follow these detailed steps:

  • Access the Admin Dashboard: Log in to your organization’s admin dashboard. This is typically found on your company’s learning management system or the platform where codes were initially assigned.

  • Navigate to the Code Management Section: Look for a section labeled “Code Management” or “Subsidy Management.” This is where you can view and manage all assigned codes.

  • Locate the Code to Be Revoked: Find the specific code assigned to the former employee. You can search by the learner’s name, email, or the unique code itself.

  • Select the Code and Initiate Revocation: Click on the code to bring up details and options. Select the “Revoke Code” option to initiate the revocation process.

  • Confirm the Revocation: A confirmation prompt will appear. Confirm that you want to revoke the code. Once confirmed, the code will be marked as revoked in the system. Important Considerations

  • Unredeemed Codes Only: Please note that you can only revoke codes that have not been redeemed. If a code has already been used to enroll in a course, it cannot be revoked or reassigned.

  • No Removal of Enrollments: There is no way to revoke a code or remove a learner’s course enrollment once the code has been redeemed. The enrollment is permanent and cannot be undone. Example Scenario

Imagine you assigned a learning code to Sarah, an employee who recently left your company. Sarah hasn’t used the code yet. Here’s how you can revoke her code:

  • You log in to your admin dashboard.
  • Navigate to the “Code Management” section.
  • Search for Sarah’s code using her email address.
  • Select the code and click on “Revoke Code.”
  • Confirm the revocation in the prompt that appears. Once revoked, the code can be reassigned to another employee who needs it, ensuring that your learning resources are utilized efficiently.

For more information or assistance about Code Management, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

My learner reported not receiving their code assignment email. How do I know if it was sent?

This issue is most commonly related to organizational firewalls. Please ensure edx-for-business-no-reply@info.edx.org is whitelisted by your organization and ask the learner to check their spam and “other” folders.

Try sending them a reminder email. If they have not received the assignment or a reminder email after 24 hours, please reach out to customersuccess@edx.org with the email of the learner, the approximate date and time the code was assigned, and the date and time the reminder was sent.

What happens if a learner unenrolls from a course in the Verified Track?

Learners have the ability to unenroll from courses through their course dashboard. If the enrollment occurred within the previous 14 days (2 days for Professional Education Courses) an automatic refund will be granted to the organization. If the enrollment occurred outside of that window, no refund will be granted to the organization.

Learners who used a single-use code to enroll in their course, then go on to unenroll within the refund window, will automatically receive a replacement code via email. Administrators looking for replacement codes for multi-use codes should reach out to customersuccess@edx.org.

What happens to a code if a learner changes their email?

Once a code has been assigned to an email address, it can only be redeemed by an edX learner with that particular email address.

If the learner’s edX account uses a different email address than the one where they received the code assignment email, they will need to change the email address on their edX account to use the code. Alternatively, the code can be revoked and reassigned to the proper email address.

Once the code has been used, the learner may change the email address in their edX account to any valid email address.

Why doesn’t my code work?

When using your code, please check the following items:

  • Is the code expired? Expired codes will not work.
  • Was the code assigned to a specific email? Codes will only work for the email to which it was assigned
  • Have you used this code before? Each code has a specified number of uses.
  • Are you enrolling in a program or a course? Codes are applied to individual courses only. A code cannot be used toward a program or multiple courses at checkout, only individual course upgrades.
  • Is your code able to be used for any course or a specific course? Check with your learning manager to confirm.

How to use Browse & Request (Video)

What is “Browse & Request”?

“Browse & Request” allows all potential learners to “window-shop” for available courses and programs through the organization’s learner portal. They do not need to have an active subscription license or assigned code to see all that their organization has to offer through edX. If potential learners find a course of interest, they can click to request access to the course. The organization’s administrator will be notified and can decide to approve or deny the request. If approved, the learner will automatically be assigned the subscription license or code they need to enroll in the course for free.

Who can leverage Browse & Request?

All customers using either subscriptions or coupon codes can leverage the new “Browse & Request” feature. Only organizations with a single subsidy type can use this feature (for now).
