edX for Business: Direct Integration

1. Video: Direct integration

This article is a video: watch it on YouTube.

2. Overview: Direct integration with edX for Business

Integration Overview Diagram:

edX for Business helps leading companies upskill their labor forces by making the world’s greatest educational resources available to learners through company learning management systems. EdX for Business was developed to support enterprises as they navigate the crowded corporate learning content marketplace. Our solution is designed to be an all-inclusive, seamless integration that enables learners to discover and access edX courses and empowers learning managers to track how their learners are using edX.

The key components of edX for Business are:

  • Discovery: displaying the curated edX course catalog
  • Access: accessing the curated course catalog via single sign-on (SSO) integration
  • Subsidy: course enrollment allowances for enterprise learners, providing flexible payment solutions (also referred as subsidy mechanisms)
  • Reporting: obtaining learner data reporting

1. Discovery

edX for Business enables learners to discover edX courses through an enterprise website (which may be an LMS, an employee portal, or other system), then enroll in and complete edX courses on the edX platform. The enterprise’s course catalog is a selection of some of the over 3000 courses and programs offered by edX, curated based on the enterprise’s needs. Your edX Enterprise Relationship Manager works with you to identify your organization’s learning needs and recommends specific content and curriculum to implement a targeted learning solution. After the course catalog is created, you use the edX Enterprise API to retrieve edX course metadata including titles, descriptions, subject areas as well as image and enrollment URLs and display the course metadata in your enterprise system.

The edX Enterprise API returns course data in JSON or XML format. To integrate your enterprise course catalog with your enterprise system, your application must consume the JSON or XML formatted course information and transform it into a format that can be displayed in the enterprise system. Course information can be displayed on your site in a format that fits your current model.

For more detailed information, see Displaying a Catalog of edX Courses.

2. Access via Single Sign-on Integration

Single sign-on (SSO) integration enables learners who are signed in to their enterprise LMS to easily register and enroll in courses on edX, using their enterprise identities and without needing to sign in again on edx.org. SSO also enables edX to identify which enterprise a learner is associated with, so that data about the learner’s progress and completion can be shared by edX with the enterprise.

edX for Business supports SSO using SAML 2.0. The SAML integration is simpler and is preferred if it is available. To implement the SAML SSO integration, you need to exchange SAML metadata with edX so that your SAML identity provider (IdP) and the edX service provider (SP) recognize each other as trusted entities.

edX works closely with your organization to collect all required information to ensure that SSO integration can be performed quickly and effectively.

For more detailed information, see Integrating Enterprise Sign-on with edX.

3. Subsidy Mechanism: Discounted Enrollment for Enterprise Learners

edX for Business offers a variety of options for learners to enroll in courses, depending on your enterprise’s needs. You can offer your learners course enrollment with no out-of-pocket cost, using edX enrollment codes that you pay for. You can instead offer learners course enrollment at a discounted price; the cost to you may depend on the estimated volume of transactions. You can also take advantage of subscription licenses with a fixed fee per learner that allows unlimited verified track enrollments for courses available in the subscriptions catalog. You may also offer a mix of these payment alternatives. The process for course enrollment discounts, pre-paid enrollment codes or subscription licenses is automated and does not require any additional integration work. Enrollment discounts are also available as a stand-alone service, separate from the SSO and Enterprise API integrations. You can offer your learners discounts on certified edX courses of any amount, including a 100% discount that makes the course free to the learner. When a learner from your enterprise enrolls in a course in your enterprise course catalog through the edX for Business integration, that enrollment counts against your enterprise seat allocation.

4. Reporting Workflow and Learner Data Sharing

In most cases, learning managers want information about how their learners are using edX: what courses they have enrolled in, what courses they have completed, and more. This information helps learning managers evaluate the success of the program and return on investment. It may also helps learners get credit in their enterprise for completing courses, if that is part of the enterprise learning policy.

When a learner enrolls in an edX course and edX identifies them as belonging to an enterprise, the learner is presented with the choice to consent to edX sharing their course progress data with the enterprise. If the learner consents, information about their course activity will be included in the enterprise’s learner data report sent by edX. If they do not consent, they cannot enroll in the course through the edX for Business integration.

edX offers a variety of reporting mechanisms for retrieving learner related data and metrics. For example, one option is to receive a periodic report of learner data in CSV format. Only data from courses that are within the enterprise’s catalog is shared with the enterprise.

Click here for more detailed information on the steps required to setup your Direct Integration.

3. Integrating with edX: Main steps

Integrating an enterprise system with edX for Business involves these main steps, each of which is described in detail in other articles in this Help Center.

  • Working with edX to create a course catalog (a selection of edX courses that the enterprise wants to offer learners through the enterprise learning portal) and getting access to the course catalog through the edX Enterprise API.
  • Setting up a single sign-on (SSO) integration, so that learners can use their enterprise credentials to sign in to edX and so that edX can report to the enterprise about how learners are using edX. EdX for Business supports SSO using SAML 2.0 (Service Provider initiated).
  • Designing and developing a flow that enables enterprise learners to discover and enroll in edX courses. For example, this may involve displaying the course catalog in the enterprise LMS. An enterprise uses the edX Enterprise API to retrieve information about courses in the course catalog and then transforms or formats the information in a way that works for the enterprise LMS.
  • Getting learner course data from edX.

Technical Support

If you need assistance or have questions about the integration, you can contact us via email at: enterprise-support@edx.org

3.1 Catalog Metadata Synchronization

What is Catalog Metadata Synchronization?

Catalog metadata synchronization is the process of extracting, transforming, and loading a customer’s curated catalog from edX into the targeted remote system, so learners are able to discover edX courses within that system.

By properly synchronizing metadata across these distributed systems, customers and vendors can ensure that catalog metadata is accurate, up-to-date, and consistent. With the right approach, catalog metadata synchronization can help customers to harness the power of edX to drive business growth and success.

Expectations of edX Catalog Metadata Synchronization

  • Environment: The Direct Integration synchronization process (ie, the code) will be implemented by either the customer or the customer’s service provider. The process will be hosted either in the remote system itself, or in a middleware service owned by the customer or the customer’s service provider.
  • Consistency: The Direct Integration synchronization process must ensure consistency in mapping data attributes, structures, and relationships to key objects in the remote system as presented by the tools available in the edX Enterprise Integration Toolkit.
  • Timeliness: The Direct Integration synchronization process should be performed on a daily basis utilizing the edX Enterprise Integration Toolkit to ensure that a customer’s curated catalog is always up-to-date in the remote system. Delayed synchronization often results in discrepancies and errors when launching edX courses, particularly as the size of the curated catalog’s metadata set increases.
  • Security: The Direct Integration synchronization process must be performed securely using the tools provided in the edX Enterprise Integration Toolkit to ensure that customer-specific catalog metadata is not compromised during the synchronization process.
  • Scalability: The Direct Integration synchronization process should be able to handle large volumes of data transmitted across the Internet. The proper integration tools should be utilized based on the size of the customer’s curated catalog. The edX Enterprise Integration Toolkit offers an API for smaller metadata sets, and a batch file transfer option for larger data sets.

4. Displaying a catalog of edX courses

EdX works with you to create a curated course catalog for your enterprise. You then use the edX Enterprise API to retrieve information about your course catalog and display it to your learners, so that they can discover edX learning opportunities.

Creating the Course Catalog

EdX offers over three thousand courses covering a wide variety of subjects, from supply chain management to cybersecurity to jazz appreciation. EdX can help an enterprise curate a list of courses and programs that are most relevant to the enterprise’s learning needs. The curated list of edX courses that a learning manager selects and makes available through the enterprise portal is called the course catalog.

About the edX Enterprise API

The enterprise uses the edX Enterprise API to retrieve its custom course catalog and display the course information in the enterprise LMS or other system. The edX Enterprise API is a REST web services API. You must provide an API client (a program, utility, or other tool that you can use to exchange messages with an API) to make requests and receive response data about courses in your course catalog from the edX Enterprise API. The Enterprise API returns response data in JSON or XML format. The enterprise’s integration must take this response data and display it to learners in the enterprise LMS or other system where learners can discover available edX learning opportunities.

The Enterprise API is designed for server-to-server communication. The best practice is to develop an API client that makes requests for the course catalog data not more frequently than daily. Do not embed Enterprise API calls directly in user-facing pages. Instead, design your system to cache course catalog information, which user-facing pages then retrieve from the cache. The edX Enterprise API is described in detail in the edX Enterprise API Guide.

For more information on how to access the API please refer to: Authenticating as an edX REST Web Service user

4.1 Get your Enterprise API credentials

API clients must authenticate when they make API requests to the Enterprise API. Authenticating allows the Enterprise API to verify the identity of the client and associate that identity with a specific edX for Business Customer.

To use the Enterprise API, you need to generate a set of API credentials, consisting of two values: client identifier and a client secret string.

Step 1: Generating Enterprise API credentials

To obtain your API credentials, complete the following steps.

Step 1.1 Register with edX

Register at www.edx.org by creating a new user account for your company. The name you enter in the Public Username field must match the name of your company.

For example, if your company is named Example, Inc., enter a name like Example or ExampleInc in the Public Username field.

Step 1.2 Fill out the API Access Form

After you have created an account, go to courses.edx.org/api-admin and fill out the API Access Request form. The name you enter in the Company Name field must be identical to the Public Username that you used to register on previous step edx.org.

A member of the edX team reviews your request. When approved you will receive and email confirming the status of your request approved or denied.

When approved sign in at courses.edx.org using the same account you used to request API access to generate your API client credentials.

Sample:

Step 1.3 edX creates a course catalogue

After you submit the API Access Request form, edX will also create a course catalogue for you and then send you an email with your client identifier and client secret string.

You can then use the client identifier and secret string to access your course catalog using the edX Enterprise API endpoints.

Step 2: Authenticating with your new credentials

The authenticating consist in generating an access token that enables you to reach out to the edX Enterprise API endpoints.

Step 2.1: Obtaining an Access Token

To obtain an access token, you submit a POST request to the /oauth2/v1/access_token authentication resource. Include a string in the message body of your POST request that contains your client identifier, client secret, grant type, and token type, as shown in the following example:

curl -X POST /oauth2/v1/access_token/\
-d "grant_type=client_credentials&client_id={client_id}
&client_secret={client_secret}&token_type=jwt" \
[api.edx.org](https://api.edx.org/{endpoint}/v1/access_token)

The response that you receive contains the access token value that you can use to submit further requests to the edX Enterprise API. For example:

{
"access_token":"g1Bb0usqwe345ty…",
"token_type": “JWT”,
"expires_in": "36000",
"scope": "read write profile email"
}

Important to note the access token expire after a period of time that is specified when you request them. After an access token expires, you must get a new token from the /oauth2/v1/access_tokenauthentication endpoint.

Step 2.2: Accessing your catalog, course metadata and learner data

The edX enterprise API has 6 endpoints that allow you retreat information about your course catalogs, courses and program metadata and learner data. You can download a Postman collection here to import in your local machine.

For a full endpoints description visit:

edx-enterprise-api.readthedocs.io

*Note: para revisar estas mismas instrucciones en español, ir a: Autenticarse como usuario del servicio web REST de edX (Spanish)

4.2 Using the Enterprise API to get and display course information

You can use the edX Enterprise API to get a list of all courses in your course catalog, including detailed information about each course. The edX for Business Enterprise API Reference contains detailed information about the Enterprise API, including the request, response data, and examples.

Displaying edX Course Information in your Learning System

After you have used the Enterprise API to retrieve information about edX courses in your course catalog, you can display the information in your enterprise LMS or other enterprise system so that your learners can discover and enroll in edX courses. How you do this depends on how you decide to implement course discovery for your learners. The Enterprise API returns its response data in JSON or XML format. To integrate your enterprise course catalog with your enterprise system, you need to consume the JSON or XML formatted course information and transform it into a format that can be displayed in your system.

2. EdCast Integration Configuration Steps

Prerequisites:

To successfully integrate your edX course catalog with EdCast, you need to have:

  • Agreements with edX and with EdCast covering the EdCast integration.

  • Approval of the edX integration from EdCast support. Configuration Steps:

  • Provide EdCast integration information to edX

  • Import edX content to EdCast content items

  • Configure SAML-based single sign-on (SSO) with EdCast Preview of the Course Discovery:

Technical Support:

If you need assistance or have questions about the integration, you can contact us via email at: enterprise-support@edx.org

3. Provide EdCast integration information to edX

EdX needs some information about your organization and your EdCast service so that we can configure the EdCast integration correctly. Send your edX account representative the following.

  • Organization Name - The name you want edX to display when your learners arrive at edx.org.
  • Organization Logo - The logo image you want edX to display when your learners interact with edx.org. The logo file should be in PNG format and should be 50K or less in size.
  • EdX will display your logo image with an absolute height of 100px and relative width. Please ensure that your logo image is sized appropriately to work within this constraint.
  • Course Selection Criteria - Choose from the list of edX subject areas provided by your edX account representative. EdX will use your course selection criteria in selecting the courses that will be imported into EdCast using the edX EdCast integration. Before you have made your actual selections, edX can test the integration using a default set of courses.
  • EdCast SAML Identity Provider (IdP) Metadata URL - The URL for the SAML configuration information that edX will use for Single Sign-On authentication of your learners. EdCast Support can provide you with this URL. Your EdCast Single Sign-On Service must be configured for SP-initiated SAML SSO. EdX will perform a daily refresh of metadata made available via this URL. Send the URL for both the production system and the test or staging system (if applicable). You may instead submit a static copy (file) that contains your SAML IdP metadata. For more detailed information, see Configuring SAML-based Single Sign-on with EdCast.

4. Import edX Content into EdCast Content Items

EdX works with you to create a curated catalog of edX courses that are relevant to your organization’s learners. You then work with your EdCast representative to import your edX course catalog into your EdCast account.

To begin, your organization needs to request API access to the edX Enterprise API and share the API client ID and secret (once approved) with EdCast. For more information, visit: Authenticating as an edX REST Web Service user

5. Configure SAML-based Single Sign-on (SSO) with EdCast

The edX EdCast integration uses SAML-based single sign-on between edX and EdCast.

To establish a SAML connection with EdCast, edX needs to configure your SAML identity provider (IdP) profile using your EdCast IdP metadata. The metadata includes configuration information such as your entity ID, endpoints, and signing certificate. EdX needs the identity provider metadata URL for both your beta environment and your production environment.

Likewise, EdCast needs to configure the edX SAML Service Provider metadata, that can be found here: courses.edx.org.

Important note: edX supports SP-initiated (Service Provider initiated) Single Sign On via SAML 2.0.

Contact EdCast Support to get your EdCast metadata URL so you can provide it to edX.

edX for Business: Inviting Learner Credit Budget Members

Can I see a list of members that I removed?

This article explains which members you can see after their removal.

You can see all the members that are removed with the ‘Show removed’ toggle. If a member does not accept their invitation to edX, their information will not be stored and you will not be able to see them in the member tab after 90 days.

For more information or assistance, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

How can I change my budget member permissions?

This article explains how you can change member permissions.

Currently, there is not a way for you to change member permissions. You will have to reach out to our support team to ask for a change. You will be able to switch the budget between first come, first serve or set a per member maximum spend.

For more information or assistance, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

How long does budget membership last?

This article explains how long a budget membership lasts.

Each budget has an expiration date, which can be found in the budget card.

When a budget expires, a member can no longer enroll in courses. The members will continue to be a part of the courses they were enrolled in even after the budget has expired.

For more information or assistance, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

What are the automated email reminders that are sent to the members?

This article explains the reminder email content and cadence.

If a member has not accepted their invitation to edX, they will receive email reminders from edx-for-business-no-reply@info.edx.org on day 5, 25, 50, 65, and 85. The email reminder will stop once the member has accepted the invitation or after 85 days.

Day 5 - Reminder: new courses available for you to browse on edX

Day 25 - Don’t get left behind—your invitation to browse and learn on edX is waiting

Day 50 - Don’t miss out on your free access to the best online courses

Day 65 - Your invitation to browse and learn on edX is expiring soon!

Day 85 - Last chance—start browsing and learning on edX today!

For more information or assistance, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

What can members of my budget do and see in their Learner Portals?

This article explains what your learner can expect to see in their Learner Portals.

The members of your budget will be able to see courses related to the budget and courses/programs they are enrolled in.

For more information or assistance, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

What happens when I remove a member?

This article explains what happens when you remove a member from a budget.

If the member has not accepted their invitation to edX and you remove them from the budget, the member will be marked as removed in the members tab. After 90 days of the invitation, they will be removed from our database and you will not be able to see them anymore.

If the member has accepted their invitation to edX and you remove them from the budget, the member will be marked as removed in the members tab. The member will still have access to any courses they enrolled in before they were removed.

For more information or assistance, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

What if a member does not accept the invitation?

This article explains what happens when a member does not accept their invitation to edX.

The member will have 90 days to accept their invitation before their invitation expires and their information is removed from our database. You will have to invite the member again for them to be a part of edX and the budget.

If the member already has an edX account, there is no action needed. The member will automatically be a part of the budget upon invitation.

For more information or assistance, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

Why are there members missing from my budget?

This article explains why certain members are missing from the budget.

If a member does not accept their invitation in 90 days, their information will be removed to be in compliance with our data privacy policy. Therefore, the invited member will be removed from the budget. If the member wants to join again, just send them another invitation.

For more information or assistance, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

edX for Business: Learner Credit

How can learner credit be spent?

This article explains how Learner Credit is spent.

How learners spend learner credit is governed by the Learner Credit budget. There are three means of budget customization that allow learner credit to be spent, as set and controlled by the learner credit budget:

Automatically applied Learning credit: Learner credit will automatically be applied to the learner account. When attempting to enroll into a course, learners will see that the price is 100% subsidized by their learner credit allocation and will be granted access at no cost to them. The learner credit will be deducted from the organization’s budget upon successful enrollment.

Course Assignment: If desired, enterprise administrators can have complete control on which courses are spent by which learners. Administrators can use our branded Enterprise administrative portal to see all courses associated with a particular budget of learner credit. Administrators can select a course they wish to grant access to, and then indicate which learners should receive the access. This marriage of course and learners will create “pending assignments” and will set aside the portion of the course cost to the learner credit budget. The targeted learners will be notified of the assignment and will be taken through a flow that leads to enrollment. Upon enrollment, the learner credit will no longer be reserved but deducted from the budget and learners will gain access to the course materials.

Learner Requests Access: Learner credit also facilitates learner-driven demand. An Enterprise administrator can let an infinitely large number of potential learners explore the courses available on the branded learner portal. Should a learner find a course or presentation of interest, they can click a “request” button which will notify their administrator. If the administrator approves the request, the learner will be granted enrollment, and the learner credit will be deducted from the organization’s budget.

For more information or assistance with Learner Credit Spend Methods, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

What courses are available for purchase with learner credit?

This article reviews the courses available with Learner Credit

Each learner credit budget can be customized with its respective course catalog. This means that each budget can be aligned with specific collections of courses. It provides a structured way to allocate learner credit for particular learning purposes.

Diverse Enrollment Options:

Learner credit empowers individuals within enterprise organizations to enroll in courses from two primary formats:

  • Course Library: Learner credit can be used to enroll in individual courses from the extensive course library, allowing learners to select courses that align with their professional development goals.
  • Cohort Learning: Cohort learning is also supported, allowing learners to engage in collaborative group-based courses. Learner credit can be utilized for these educational experiences as well. Customization with Course Catalogs:

Enterprise organizations have the freedom to set Learner credit budgets with the full catalog of each format, tailoring their learning offerings to the unique needs of their learners. Moreover, if desired, organizations can create a custom catalog through curation, handpicking courses that precisely meet their learning objectives.

Focused Course Discovery:

To ensure learners access the right courses, they will discover and enroll only in the courses associated with the budget.

Please contact your customer success representative to explore the set up of your course catalog further and to apply this to your learner credit budget.

For more information or assistance with Learner Credit, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

What is a learner credit spend limit?

This article explains how individual Learner Credit spend limits work

An organization’s learner credit’s remaining balance can never be spent below $0. Every learner credit budget has an overall limit which prevents spend beyond the contracted amount. Should it be desired by an enterprise, each budget can (but not required to) also include a per-learner spend limit. This per-learner spend limit sets a maximum amount that can be spent by any single learner, ensuring that all learners get equal credit allotments. Per-learner spend limits apply only to “automatically applied” credit and are not applicable to course assignments with learner credit or browse and request transactions.

Let’s take a closer look at the characteristics of learner credit spend limits when the budget is configured for auto apply:

Overall budget Limit: Each learner credit budget has an overall limit that prevents spending beyond the contracted amount. Per learner Spend limits ensures that learners do not overspend and helps organizations maintain control over their financial budget across learners. This overarching limit ensures that an organization’s budget constraints are respected.

Per-Learner Spend Limit within Budget: For added flexibility, each budget can include a per-learner spend limit. This per-learner spend limit sets a maximum amount that can be spent by any single learner. This feature ensures that all learners receive equal credit allotments.

Application Scope: It’s important to note that per-learner spend limits apply only to “automatically applied” credit. They are not applicable to admin controlled course assignments with learner credit or learner initiated browse and request transactions. This ensures that the per-learner spend limit does not hinder the learning experience when learners access courses in different ways.

For more information or assistance Learner Credit Spend Limit, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

What is learner credit?

This article reviews what Learner Credit is and how it’s used.

Learner credit is an Enterprise bank account that enables enrollment into individually-priced courses for learners belonging to an organization. Learner credit is enumerated in United States Dollars and can be redeemed across the course library and cohort learning interchangeably. Upon redemption and enrollment, the individual course price is deducted from the organization’s learner credit. Learner credit dollars are valued at one to one with course prices (there is no conversion necessary.)

To facilitate different use cases and grant the organization administrative control, learner credit can be divided into different “budgets.” These budgets pair with a catalog of eligible content, policies about how a learner can gain access to credit, and (if desired) spend limits for learners.

Here’s how it works:

  • Enumeration in US Dollars: Learner credit is denominated in United States Dollars and serves as the base currency for all catalog pricing and redemptions.
  • Flexible Use: Learner credit can be redeemed seamlessly across a course library and cohort learning options, offering the flexibility to enroll into courses that best suit the learner’s or administrator’s needs.
  • Direct Deduction: When learners use their allocated or assigned credit to enroll in courses, the cost of each course is automatically deducted from the organization’s learner credit balance.
  • One-to-One Value: Learner credit dollars are valued at a one-to-one ratio with course prices. This means that there’s no need for complex currency conversion; the redemption directly corresponds to the cost of courses. For more information or assistance about Learner Credit, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

edX for Business: Learner Credit FAQs

Can learner credit be refunded?

This article explains the refund process for Learner Credit

Yes, learner credit is automatically refunded in accordance with our unenrollment-based refund policies. Should a student choose to unenroll within 14 days off enrollment or course start (whichever comes later), learner credit will be automatically returned to their Enterprise’s learner credit budget.

This unenrollment can be initiated by the learner, from the respective course tile/card on their enterprise dashboard. Administrators who wish to have a learner unenrolled from a course can contact support for assistance with their request. Refunds after the 14-day period are not guaranteed and are governed by the refund policy.

For more information or assistance with Learner Credit Refunds, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

Enabling Access via Link (aka Universal Invite Link)

Administrators can choose to use a “universal link” to grant potential learners access to the portal. A universal link can be generated from the administrator portal under, “Settings > Configure Access > Universal Link / Access via Link”. This link allows anyone who can click it to gain access to your organization’s learner portal, link to your organization, and access use to your organization’s Learner Credit.

Note: As an added security measure, there’s a limit on the number of times a link can be used. This is noted in the portal beside each link that’s generated. It is possible to enable BOTH browsing experiences via SSO and via a Universal link if desired. Also, it’s possible to have multiple active links at once.

A learner is being prompted for payment even though they are under the cap we have in place for them. What’s going on?

Most enterprise offers are “enrollment” offers. They can only be used to enroll in a course. If a learner has a cap of $500 and they enrolled in 2 courses costing $200 each, they only have $100 remaining and would only be able to enroll in courses costing $100 or less. Please reach out to your Account Manager if you have questions or wish to discuss options for this learner.

If you have questions about this or who your Account Manager is, please reach out to customersuccess@edx.org.

A learner unenrolled for a refund but they are being prompted for payment in their enterprise learner credit. What’s going on?

Like a code, once used, the enterprise learner credit cannot be reverted to its unused state. Please speak with your Enterprise Contact for options to ensure learning is not interrupted.

If you have questions about this or who your Enterprise Contact is, please reach out to the Enterprise Customer Success team via the support request form found here: business-support.edx.org

Our learner enrolled in a program but didn’t get the program discount. What happened?

Our system can only apply one discount at a time and the discount provided by your enterprise is the only discount your learner is eligible for.

The Program Discount is only eligible for non-enterprise learners given the additional support and reporting enterprise enrollments provide.

What are the ways we can manage our investment amount with enterprise learner credit?

You can manage your spend in a number of ways:

  • Limit the total dollar amount spent by your organization (we recommend this option) so that you do not go over.
  • Limit the total number of enrollments your organization can have.
  • Limit the amount that an individual can spend
  • Limit the number of courses to which an individual can enroll To put any of these limits in place or to discuss which option is right for you, please talk to your Enterprise Sales contact.

You can also contact the Enterprise Customer Support team if you’re not sure who your Enterprise Contact is by completing this support request form found here: business-support.edx.org

What happens when my learner credit budget expires?

This article explains what happens when a Learner Credit Budget expires.

Enterprise learner credit budgets have an expiration date indicated on their initial contract and reflected in the administrator portal for the respective learning credit budget.

Should a budget reach expiration, learners will be unable to enroll in additional courses, and those administrators who have chosen to assign courses will become unable to allocate courses to learners. However, learners already enrolled in their courses through learner credit will maintain paid-access and will not be disrupted.

For more information or assistance Learner Credit Budget Expiration, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

A learner is unable to complete my course now and would like to transfer to a new session. Can we do that?

This article explains the standard enrollment/unenrollment process and parameters for OCM/ExecEd courses.

If a learner is unable to complete a course you have several options.

  • If you are enrolled in the audit track of a course, you can simply stop taking the course. There is no need to unenroll.

  • If you paid for the verified track of a course, you can unenroll and get a refund, if you unenroll within the refund eligibility period. After the refund eligibility period has passed, you can still unenroll from the course, but you do not get a refund. If a learner would like to later enroll within a session for a course they’ve attended previously.

  • Their previous progress within the course will not be carried over to the new session and they will need to start from the beginning.

  • If the learner wishes to enroll within a verified track please note the price for these paid course tracks are subject to change. For more information or assistance, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

  • Browse or search for the course you want to take.

  • Select Enroll Now on the course enrollment page. A message displays confirming your enrollment and asks if you would like to use your code.

  • Choose to Enroll Now. If a leaner is unable to enroll in a course, they must first check

  • They have an active Learner Account connected to your company’s Enterprise Account.

  • They have an eligible Enrollment Code(s) which can be determined by searching their email address associated with the Enterprise within the Admin Portal.

  • The course must be included within your Enterprise partnership’s catalog which should be outlined and reviewed during your Enterprise onboarding process. For more information or assistance with Enrollment Codes, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

What is proctoring in edX courses and other proctoring FAQs

This article provides a brief overview of proctoring in edX courses. For a complete learner guide and support with proctoring, learners should visit edX Help Center - Taking Timed and Proctored Exams

What is proctoring?

Proctored exams are timed exams that you take while proctoring software monitors your computer’s desktop, webcam video and audio. The data recorded by the proctoring software is transferred to a secure, 3rd party proctoring service for review. Proctored exams may or may not be required for your course and enrollment track.

What are the proctoring providers used by edX?

edX courses currently use two proctoring providers: Software Secure (PSI) and Proctortrack

Do learner need to install software in order to take proctored exams?

Yes, learners may need to install the proctoring service used in their course, if the course has proctoring, in order to take the proctored exam.

Tips:

  • edX Enterprise Customers should take steps to allow the installation of both vendors software when company computers are used.
  • If companies do not allow proctoring software installation - learners should use an alternative computer for the exam
  • Learners should check at the beginning of each course to determine if proctoring software is required.
  • Learners should visit edX Learner Help Center article Proctored exam rules and requirements here, which provides an overview of the computer and browser requirements.
  • If learners are experiencing trouble or need assistance, they should contact edX learners edX Learner Support here. The edX Learner Support team is the designated team to assist with proctoring concerns and escalations.

Proctoring System Requirements

Click here for Software Secure proctoring software system requirements

Click here for Proctortrack proctoring software system requirements

*The learner is responsible for making sure that the proctoring software works with your system before they take a proctored exam. If learners are experiencing trouble or need assistance, they should contact edX Learner Support via the edX Help Center here.

**For additional questions specific to proctoring and edX for Business learners, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)
