  • Open the invitation email (sent from edx-for-business-no-reply@edx.org)
  • Click the ‘Accept my invitation’ button
  • Navigate to the Learner Portal and enroll in courses For more information or assistance with Subscription Licenses, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

edX for Business: Learner Progress Report

Can our learner/employee retake a quiz/exam in their course?

We are not able to offer individual exam or assignment retakes, or extend deadlines of graded material. The course team has a fixed course schedule and grading policy.

We are not able to offer individual exam or assignment retakes, or extend deadlines of graded material. The course team has a fixed course schedule and grading policy.

Each course team sets a fixed number of attempts on each graded assignment. Once a learner has used up all available attempts on an assignment, it isn’t possible to retake the assignment. It’s also not possible to reset a learner’s Progress score. They will need to wait for the next session to retake the course. Please note learners will need to pay the verified certificate fee again to upgrade to future course sessions.

In most courses, it is possible to miss an assignment or two and still receive a passing final grade. Some courses automatically drop one or more of the lowest graded assignments. Please instruct your learners to consult their course’s grading policy, syllabus, or progress page for specific details.

If learners have further questions regarding graded material, they should consult their course team to assist further. Learners may reach out to course staff through the course discussion forum.

If learners require additional support regarding in course material or contacting their course team, please direct them to our Learner Support team at courses.edx.org, where they can “Submit a request” to our designated Learner Support team.

Configuring Reports in the Admin Dashboard

The edX Admin Portal allows you to configure your own catalog metadata, and learner progress reports for receipt on a cadence of your choosing, delivered as a password-protected compressed (ZIP) file in JSON or CSV format via email (SMTP) or secure file transport (SFTP).

To configure these reports, follow the instructions below:

  • Login to the edX Admin Portal: portal.edx.org using your admin account
  • Using the Left toolbar, navigate to Reporting Configurations (contact your edX Account Management representative if you need help obtaining access to this feature).
  • Select “Add a reporting configuration”
  • Complete the fields required for the configuration, as follows: - Active: Checking this box will ensure the report is sent. This can be unchecked at later time if you do not wish to receive the report anymore. If left unchecked, the report will not be active and will not send as configured. - Data Type: The type of report you want to receive. - Catalog: This will send a catalog metadata report, including all of the courses available to your organization in your edX Curated Catalog - Progress: This will send a learner progress report with information about the courses learners at your organization are enrolled in. - Delivery Method: The transmission mechanism to use for delivery of the report, which can be either Email or SFTP. Regardless of the selection, the report file will be delivered as a password-protected compressed (ZIP) file. - Report Type: The representation format to use, which can be either CSV or JSON - Frequency: The interval for which to deliver the report, which can be either daily, weekly, or monthly. - Day of Month: The day of the month to send the report (Required and only valid when the frequency is monthly) - Day of Week: The day of the week to send the report (Required and only valid when the frequency is weekly) - Hour of Day: The hour of the day to send the report, in Eastern Time (24-hour format). This field is required for all frequency settings
  • If your chosen Delivery Method is email, you will also need to complete the following fields - Email(s): The email addresses where the report should be sent. Please include one address per line - Password: This password will be used to secure the compressed (ZIP) file. Please specify the password in clear-text format. It will be encrypted by the edX system when stored in the database. If lost, you will need to submit a new password.
  • If your chosen Delivery Method is SFTP, you will also need to complete the following fields - SFTP Host Name: The host name of the SFTP service to deliver the report. Please provision a unique SFTP service account specifically for edX’s use. - SFTP Port Number: The connection port made available by the SFTP service. The standard port number for SFTP services is 22. - SFTP Username: The service username used to securely access the SFTP service. Please provision a unique SFTP service account specifically for edX’s use. - Decrypted SFTP Password: The service password used to security access the SFTP service. Specify the password in clear-text format. If lost, you will need to submit a new password. - SFTP File Path: The path (directory) on the SFTP service to store the report file.
  • If your chosen Data Type is catalog, you will also need to complete the following fields - Enterprise Customer Catalogs: Select which catalogs should be included in your report. If left blank, all catalogs will be included.
  • Confirm you have checked Active at the top, and click Submit at the bottom.
  • Once you have Submitted, allow up to 24 hours for the report to send as configured. (Note: the hour of the day is in Eastern Standard Time (EST).

How can I see who has completed a course?

In the Administrator Dashboard, you can see who has completed a course, in addition to other learner data.

  • On the left side of the Admin Dashboard, click the Learner Progress Report tab
  • Enter the learner’s email address in the Filter by email field to search for the learner For courses that they have passed, a date will appear in the Passed date column

For more information about the data that is included in the learner progress report, see the Working With Learner Data article.

Note: If you do not see the tab for Learner Progress Report on your admin portal, your organization may not have an active data sharing package included in your contract. Please contact your edX sales representative if you would like to learn more and inquire about adding the data package to a current or future agreement.

How can I support a learner who has failed a course?

This article explains the use of the Learner Progress Report to support learners.

For most assignments, grades are calculated instantly by the system. This progress is visible on the Learner Progress Report and updates every 24 hours. A learner will have a status of ‘Failed’ for course progress if the course End Date has passed and they have not met the minimum grade requirement to receive a certificate.

It is not possible to retake the course in the same run, but the learner is welcome to join a future session of these to try again.

For more information or assistance with this feature, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

How can I tell if a learner enrolled with a code, a subscription license, or something else?

In the Learner Progress Report, select Download full report (CSV) to download the report in CSV format.

Within the report, the ‘seat_delivery_method’ column indicates the type of subsidy used by the learner to enroll in the course.

For more information about the data that is included in the learner progress report, see the Working With Learner Data article.

How can I understand Learner Progress at the course level?

There are three statuses for learners as reflected in the Learner Progress Report. The report is updated every 24 hours and reflects the learner progress at that time.

Status

Explanation

In Progress

Course End Date has not passed and learner has not yet achieved a passing grade OR if the learner completed the course but has not achieved a passing score

Passed

Learner has received the minimum passing grade for a Verified Certificate

Failed

Course End Date has passed, and learner does not have a passing grade

.

How do I know if a learner completed a course assignment?

This article explains how you know if a learner completed a course.

At this time, the learner progress report does not include the status of course assignments, including individual exams and open-response assignments. Student progress can be found Using the learner progress report

Admins are welcome to check directly with a learner to inquire about the completion of any specific assignment, which can be found on the learner’s individual course progress page.

For more information or assistance with how you know if a learner completed a course, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

How do I track the account spend?

Today the process for checking your current total spend is a bit manual, and something that we are in the process of automating and making much easier to see.

For the moment, it can be done through the Administrator Dashboard. For a quick approximation of spend, you can simply download your Learner Progress Report, which can be found under “Download full report (CSV)” on the right hand side of the Learner Progress Report tab (as demonstrated below), and add up the column (P) with the course cost (course_list_price).

For spend amount regarding a specific Coupon Name, you can filter column (n) “coupon_name” to isolate the specific Coupon Name(s) you wish to total against your spend limit.

If you would like additional assistance please reach out to customersuccess@edx.org

Using the Learner Progress Report

The Learner Progress Report has two sections, the Overview and the Full Report.

The Overview section displays:

  • The number of learners from your organization who have registered at edx.org using their ID with your organization. This does not include any learners who may have registered using their personal email accounts.

  • The number of learners from your organization who have enrolled in at least one edX course.

  • The number of of learners from your organization who have been active in a course at least once in the past week

  • The number of courses that learners from your organization have completed The Full Report section lists every learner from your organization, who linked to your organization and has enrolled in an edX course. For each learner, the Full Report lists:

  • Email address

  • Course title

  • Enrollment date

  • Date they passed the course

  • Enrollment mode (verified or audit)

  • The price of the course (after discounts, or list price?)

  • The name of the edX for Business coupon, if any, that was applicable to their enrollment

  • The coupon offer that the learner used in their enrollment (for example, 100% discount) You can sort the Full Report by any of the report columns by clicking on the up or down arrow in the column heading.

Select Download full report (CSV) to download the report in CSV format. The downloaded report includes additional fields for each learner. For more information about the fields included in the downloaded report, see learner-summary Endpoint in the edX Enterprise API Guide.

Note: The Learner Progress Report is updated on a 24-hour cadence. If you have a learner who has enrolled in a course, completed assessments, or completed the course it will appear within 24 hours. If you do not see the tab for Learner Progress Report on your admin portal, your organization may not have an active data sharing package included in your contract. Please contact your edX sales representative if you would like to learn more and inquire about adding the data package to a current or future agreement.

Where can I see the date the learner enrolled and the date by which they must unenroll for a refund?

You can see the date a learner enrolled in a course in the Learner Progress Report. Select Download full report (CSV) to download the report in CSV format.

The ‘enrollment_date’ column indicates the date in which the learner enrolled in the course. The ‘unenrollment_end_within_date’ column indicates the date by which the learner must unenroll for your enterprise to receive a refund on the enrollment. This date is traditionally 14 days from the enrollment date or the course start date, whichever is later.

For more information about the data that is included in the learner progress report, see the Working With Learner Data article (https://business-support.edx.org/hc/en-us/articles/360005425093-Working-with-learner-data).

Why are there blank entries on the Learner Progress Report?

This article explains Blank/Null Entries in the Learner Progress Report.

A blank (or null) entry on a Learner Progress Report that shows an enrollment without an associated email indicates that the user did not consent to share their enrollment data for that specific enrollment. Blank entries will show for learners in the audit path, or that used a personal credit/debit card payment to enroll in a course while linked to an enterprise, and opted not to share that enrollment with the linked enterprise.

You can confirm under seat_delivery_method the type of subsidy used by the learner for each applicable enrollment.

Note: If you do not see the tab for Learner Progress Report on your admin portal, or you do see the tab with the message “There are no results.”, your organization may not have an active data sharing package included in your contract. Please contact your edX sales representative if you would like to learn more and inquire about adding the data package to a current or future agreement.

For additional questions specific to blank/null values within the Learner Progress Report, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

Why don’t I see a specific learner on my Learner Progress Report?

The Learner Progress Report is updated on a 24-hour cadence. If you have a learner who has enrolled in a course, completed assessments, or completed the course it will appear within 24 hours. If it has been longer than 24 hours, please reach out to customersuccess@edx.org with the learner’s email and the course you are expecting to see so that our team can investigate for you.

Working with learner data

In most cases, an enterprise’s learning managers want information about how learners are using learning resources that the enterprise offers them (what courses they have enrolled in, what courses they have completed, etc.) This information helps learning managers evaluate the success of the program and helps learners get credit for completing courses. edX for Business provides periodic reports for these purposes.

Learner consent

When a learner enrolls in an edX course and edX identifies them as belonging to an enterprise, the edX system presents the learner with a form requesting their consent to share their course progress data with their enterprise. If the learner consents, their course data will be included in the enterprise’s learner data report sent by edX. If they do not consent, they will not be enrolled in the course.

Retrieving learner enrollments from edX

EdX offers a variety of reporting mechanisms for retrieving learner related data and metrics:

  • learner-summary endpoint of the Enterprise API. For more information on how to use the endpoint and what information is retrieved, please reference: edx-enterprise-api.readthedocs.io
  • Learner Progress Report is available to download in CSV format from the Admin Dashboard. The learner progress report (updated on a 24-hour cadence) includes for the following information each learner enrollment:
  • enrollment_id - A unique identifier for this enrollment
  • enterprise_enrollment_id - A unique identifier for this enrollment, specific to enterprise enrollments
  • is_consent_granted - Whether the learner has granted consent for edX to share information about their course enrollment and progress with the enterprise.
  • paid_by - Whether the enrollment was paid for by the enterprise, the learner, or a third party.
  • user_current_enrollment_mode - The learner’s enrollment mode in the course, audit or verified (eligible for a verified certificate).
  • enrollment_date - The date the learner enrolled in the course.
  • unenrollment_date - The date the learner unenrolled from their course.
  • unenrollment_end_within_date - The date the learner must unenroll by, in order to receive a refund on the enrollment. This date is traditionally 14 days from the enrollment date or the course start date, whichever is later.
  • is_refunded - Whether or not the learner received a refund on their enrollment
  • seat_delivery_method - The type of subsidy used by the learner to enroll in their course.
  • offer_name - The offer ID used by the learner to enroll in their course. (Not applicable for Subscriptions or Codes)
  • offer_type - The offer type and discount percentage used by the learner to enroll in their course. (Not applicable for Subscriptions or Codes)
  • coupon_code - The enrollment code string used by the learner to enroll in their course. (Not applicable for Subscriptions or Offers)
  • coupon_name - The name of the enrollment code batch used by the learner to enroll in their course. (Not applicable for Subscriptions or Offers.)
  • contract_id - A unique identifier for the contract that this enrollment is associated with
  • course_list_price - The original price of the course, before any discount was applied
  • amount_learner_paid - The amount the learner paid towards the enrollment if any.
  • course_key - The unique identifier for the overall course.
  • courserun_key - The unique identifier for the individual courserun.
  • course_title - The title of the edX course
  • course_pacing_type - Whether the course is self-paced or instructor-paced.
  • course_start - The first day the course is open to start submitting assignments.
  • course_end - The last day to submit materials for the course.
  • course_duration - The duration in weeks, for instructor-paced courses, or the expected duration for self-paced courses.
  • course_max_effort - The estimated maximum effort required by the course, in hours per week.
  • course_min_effort - The estimated minimum effort required by the course, in hours per week.
  • course_primary_program - The primary program a course belongs to. (Not applicable to courses that aren’t part of a program)
  • course_primary_program_type - The type of program this course belongs to, such as a MicroMasters Program or Professional Certificate Program.
  • course_primary_subject - The subject category this course falls under when searching on edX.org
  • has_passed - Whether or not the learner has passed this course
  • last_activity_date The most recent date the learner was active in an edX.
  • progress_status - Whether the learner is still working on the course, has passed, or has failed.
  • passed_date - The date the learner passed the course.
  • current_grade - The learner’s current grade, which will update as the learner proceeds through the course.
  • letter_grade - The learner’s letter grade, if they have passed the course.
  • enterprise_user_id - The learner’s ID assigned by edX.
  • user_email - The learner’s email address.
  • user_account_creation_date - The date the learner created their edX account.
  • user_country - A two-letter country code.
  • user_username - The learner’s user name on edx.org.
  • enterprise_name - The enterprise account name.
  • enterprise_customer_uuid - The enterprise account ID assigned by edX.
  • enterprise_sso_uid - The learner’s ID in the enterprise’s Identity Provider. (Only applicable for customers using Single Sign On)
  • created - The date and time the learner progress report was last updated.
  • course_api_url - The complete url for the course when using the edX API Retrieve Course Metadata endpoint.
  • total_learning_time_hours - The numeric hours the learner has spent learning in the course (i.e. 1.0 = 1 hour)

edX for Business: Learner Selected Content

Can a learner enroll in courses outside of those that have been assigned?

This article reviews whether or not Learners can enroll using Learner Credit for courses outside of those that have been assigned.

When an enterprise organization chooses to leverage course assignments, their learners are prevented from spending learner credit through other means. Learners who have been assigned a specific course can enroll in that course exclusively. The other courses associated with the organizations’ learner credit budget are visible, but will not allow enrollment.

For more information or assistance with Learner Credit, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

How can we allow individual learners to enroll in courses beyond the cap we have in place?

If you wish to enable learners to learn past your organizational cap, please reach out to your Enterprise Sales Contact who can work with you to find the best option.

You can also contact the Enterprise Customer Support team if you’re not sure who your Enterprise Contact is by completing this support request form found here: business-support.edx.org

edX for Business: Learners

Enrollment and Spending Limits

When attempting to access a course subsidized by your organization, you may be prevented from enrolling due to limits set by your organization.

Limits can be set based on:

  • Spending, which means that each learner is designated a specific amount of money or enrollment codes.
  • Enrollment, which means that each learner is limited to a specific number of course enrollments. We recommend reaching out to your learning administrator to inquire about accessing more funds for enrollments.

How can I see what my learners are seeing?

This article explains how to replicate your learner’s view of the Learner Portal

If you are using codes or licenses and using the Learner Portal, you are able to see what your learners see by navigating to enterprise.edx.org while logged into your admin edX account. Assigning yourself a subsidy (code or license) from portal.edx.org will enable you to see courses appear under “free to me” in addition to being able to filter by “all courses”.

If you are looking for a view into what your learners see via your LMS, you will need to refer to your internal processes as we do not have insight into off-edX platform products. Typically you will need to masquerade as a learner to view things as a learner in your LMS. Refer to your LMS documentation or administrator for help.

For more information or assistance with this feature, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable).

How do I enroll in a complete program?

This article discusses how to enroll in a complete program using enrollment codes

The course enrollment codes can be applied to an individual course, but not an entire program or multiple courses at the same time.

Learners can upgrade using codes and complete the program courses one by one. After they have earned a verified certificate from each course in the program, they automatically receive the program credential at no further cost.

Learners don’t need to complete all of the courses at once in order to earn the program credential. Courses will be re-offered on an ongoing basis, and all certificates count towards the program credential. There is no overall deadline to complete the program, but individual courses do have deadlines that you can see when enrolled.

Learners are able to take a program by enrolling in each of the courses that makes up a program. A learner earns a program certificate by successfully completing all courses within a program.

For more information or assistance with this topic, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

How do I unenroll from a course?

This article explains the process to unenroll from a course run

  • Steps to unenroll
  • Will I get a refund?
  • Change sessions if you’ve purchased a program bundle

Steps to Unenroll

To unenroll from a course, follow these steps:

  1. Visit your learner portal*

  2. On the Courses page of the dashboard, find the course that you want to unenroll from.

  3. In the upper right hand corner of the course card you’ll find a Course Actions drop down button. Click this and then select Unenroll, then confirm.

Note: The Unenroll option will not be available after you’ve earned the verified certificate in the course.

Unenrolling and Refunds

If you unenroll from the verified track of a course within the refund eligibility period, within 14 days after payment or within the first 14 days of the course, whichever comes last*, you are unenrolled from the course and automatically receive a refund for your course payment.

*(Up to 180 days after your payment maximum)

If you unenroll from the verified track of a course AFTER the refund eligibility period has passed, you are unenrolled, but will not receive a refund.

If you have earned a certificate the unenroll option will not be available and we are unable to provide a refund.

Other Options

In courses that were purchased as part of a program bundle, you will have the ability to change course sessions in addition to unenrolling for a refund. For these courses, if the refund eligibility period has passed (meaning that you will not receive a refund), the unenroll option is not available.

Degree Program Students

If you are a Master’s student and you were enrolled by your institution, you will not be able to directly unenroll yourself on edX.org. Please reach out to your advisor or designated contact at the institution to assist you with dropping a Master’s course.

If you are a Boot Camp, Bachelor’s, please reach out using the contact information for your specific program, which can be found on your program’s specific About page.

For more information or assistance with this feature, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable).

I asked the edX team to update my catalog but I don’t see it reflected in the Learner Portal. What’s going on?

This article explains the expected time it may take for requested changes to your course catalog to update.

Any updates made to your catalog can take up to 24 hours to go into effect. If it has been more than 24 hours, try filtering by all courses, search the course. If you are still not seeing the added course, please email our Enterprise Customer Success team at customersuccess@edx.org and include the course name and a screenshot so that we can best assist.

For more information or assistance, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

I don’t see anything show up when I search a course. What happened?

If a course does not appear when searching the course title, this could be due to a few things:

  • That specific course is not included in your organization’s catalog
  • You could be searching under “Free to Me” instead of “All Courses”.
  • It could also mean that there is not an active run of the course open for learners to enroll in. If the course is not included in your organization’s catalog, administrators may request that a course be added to their catalog by completing the support request form found here: business-support.edx.org and including the name of the course(s).

Why do I have a limited number of courses under “All Courses”? I thought edX had thousands of courses.

This article explains why you may have a limited view of courses within the Learner Portal.

When searching for courses in your Learner Portal, “All Courses” will show the courses that are included in your organization’s catalog.

Only courses in your catalog are those that are eligible for your learners to enroll in with use of a code or subscription license.

For more information or assistance, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

Why is my subscription license deactivated?

Subscription licenses can become deactivated for one of two reasons:

  • The license has been revoked by the Learning Administrator

  • The license has expired without a renewal A deactivated license means that:

  • You will not be able to continue enrolling in additional courses at no cost

  • Any courses that were in progress will be moved to the “audit track.” In the audit track, the license holder is prevented from passing further assessments and earning a certificate A deactivated license does NOT:

  • Delete the former license holder’s account

  • Invalidate or delete previous accomplishments. All earned certificates remain available for download and associated with your learner account

  • Prevent you from taking future courses on edx.org, as an individual pursuing your personal learning journey

  • Prohibit the administrator from re-assigning a license to you at a later date

edX for Business: Moodle

1. Overview: Moodle Integration with edX for Business

Integration Overview Diagram:

Prerequisites:

To successfully integrate the edX course catalog with Moodle, you need to have:

  • An agreement with edX covering the Moodle integration.

  • A Moodle Administrator level account in your Moodle system with access to the Site Administration interface.

  • A Moodle user account configured as a service account for use by the edX Moodle Integration. We recommend this be a new, clean account. What to expect once the integration is setup:

  • Once the edX integration goes live, you should expect to be able to:

  • Find edX courses (as per your subscription catalog or any other catalog you have

  • access to), under the Site Home page.

  • As an admin (instructor), be able to add users as “Participants” to the courses.

  • As a learner (student), discover the courses that were assigned to me, and launch the course to complete the enrollment process on the edX platform. Each course will display the edX course title, description and a link ‘Go to edX Course page’; via this link students can enroll in the edX course on the edX platform, after using a login with username that matches

  • their Moodle login. Click here to view the steps required to setup your Moodle Integration.

2. Moodle Integration Configuration Steps

Configuration Steps:

  • Configure Single Sign-On to enable learner access
  • Enable external web services (such as the edX Moodle Integration) to talk to your Moodle system over REST APIs
  • Enable protocols
  • Create a specific Moodle user
  • Check user capability - Create a Moodle Role that enables all the permissions needed by the edX Moodle Integration
  • Add the edX Moodle Integration web service to Moodle
  • Add functions to the web service
  • Select a specific user (as the authorized user for the web service)
  • Create a token for the web services user (that allows edX to act as the Moodle User created in Step 3)
  • Enable developer documentation
  • Provide your Moodle URL and token to edX via the edX Admin Portal Technical Support:

If you need assistance or have questions about the integration, you can contact us via email at: enterprise-support@edx.org

2.1 - Enable external web services to talk to your Moodle system over REST APIs

Enable external web services to talk to your Moodle system over REST APIs

  • Sign in to Moodle with an Administrator level account
  • Navigate to “Site administration > Advanced features” screen
  • Check “Enable web services” then click ‘Save Changes’

2.10 - Provide your Moodle URL and token to edX via the edX Admin Portal

  • Login to the edX Admin Portal: portal.edx.org using your admin account (contact your edX Customer Success representative if you need help obtaining access)
  • Using the Left toolbar, navigate to select LMS Integration Configuration option:
  • Click on the + sign next to Moodle to add your moodle instance configuration:
  • Complete the fields required for the configuration, as follows: - Moodle Instance URL: make sure the URL begins with https:// (secure). - Web Service Short Name: this should match the service short name in Site administration > Plugins > Web services > External services > External service - Moodle Category ID: this is the category ID that the edX courses will be added under. If you want the edX courses to be in a separate/new category, please provide that ID in this field. - Webservice username and password are not required as long as you provide the Webservice Auth Token created in Step 8. - Once all the key pieces of information are provided, select the Active check box at the top - Click the ‘Submit’ button

2.2 - Enable protocols

  • Navigate to “Site administration > Plugins > Web services > Manage protocols”
  • On the Manage Protocols screen, under the “Enable” column, click the eyeball icon in the “REST” row. This enables REST API functionality that the edX Moodle Integration requires to connect to Moodle.

2.3 - Create a specific Moodle user

  • Navigate to “Site administration > Users
  • In the Accounts category, click the “Add a new user” link
  • Set the username as: edxwebservice
  • Choose a password (you will not be required to share this username and password, as long as you use them to generate a token that you will provide to edX).
  • For email address use: enterprise-integrations@edx.org

2.4 - Create a Moodle Role that enables all the permissions needed by the edX Moodle Integration

We need a Moodle Role that enables all the permissions needed by the edX Moodle Integration.

You can either create a new system role or assign existing role(s) to the user. This will focus on creating one that is limited to the permissions we need.

  • Navigate to “Site Administration > Users > Define roles”
  • Click on the “Add a new role” button near the bottom
  • There’s no real need to choose a role as this will just assign all of the permissions needed, so click the “Continue” button.
  • Check the boxes “System”, “User” and “Category” next to the “Context types where this role may be assigned” section
  • Scroll to the bottom and search for the permissions. Check the box next to each of the permissions. The permissions needed are as follows: - moodle/category:manage - moodle/category:viewhiddencategories - moodle/course:create - moodle/course:changecategory - moodle/course:changefullname - moodle/course:changeidnumber - moodle/course:changeshortname - moodle/course:changesummary - moodle/course:delete - moodle/course:visibility - moodle/course:update - moodle/course:tag - webservice/rest:use - moodle/webservice:createtoken - moodle/user:update - moodle/course:enrolconfig - moodle/course:manageactivities - moodle/course:useremail - moodle/course:view - moodle/course:viewhiddencourses - moodle/course:viewparticipants - moodle/course:visibility - moodle/grade:edit - moodle/grade:viewall - moodle/user:viewdetails - moodle/user:viewhiddendetails - mod/assign:grade - mod/assign:view - moodle/site:accessallgroups

  1. Once finished, click Add o(Save Changes) at the very bottom. You’ll then see the list of permissions for this role so double check all permissions have been assigned.

  2. Next navigate to Site Administration > Users > Assign system roles

  3. You should see your new system role on the next screen. Click on the name to assign users.

  4. This will then take you to another user selection screen. Move the user you created for edx webservice usage over to this role by highlighting their username and click “Add”.

  5. Changes are saved automatically, so after this you should be done configuring the appropriate permissions and role to the user.

Well done!

2.5 - Add the edX Moodle Integration web service to Moodle

  • Navigate to “Site administration > Server > Web services > External services”
  • From the “External services” screen, click the link “Add” to add a new custom service.
  • Fill out the form to add a new service with the following fields: - Name: “edx” - Check the box next to “Enabled” - Check the box next to “Authorised users only” - this will mean only users specified can use the API.
  • After completing the form, click “Add service”.

2.6 - Add functions to the web service

  • On the External Services screen, “Site administration > Server > Web services > External services” , find the new external service named “edx” that you just created in the table. Click on the Functions link.
  • From the “Add Functions” form, add the following functions to give the edX Moodle Integration the permissions it needs as follows - core_course_create_courses - core_course_delete_courses - core_course_delete_modules - core_course_get_categories - core_course_get_contents - core_course_get_course_module - core_course_get_courses - core_course_get_courses_by_field - core_course_search_courses - core_course_update_categories - core_course_update_courses - core_enrol_get_enrolled_users - core_grades_update_grades The specific functions the user needs to add are visible in the below screenshot:

Note: multiple functions can be added at once by using the “multi select” search bar on the form):

  1. From the “Add Functions” form, once all functions have been selected, click the “Add Functions” button.

2.7 - Select a specific user (as the authorised user for the web service)

Select a specific user to authorise it for the web service:

  • Navigate again to web service page (“Site administration > Server > Web services > External services” )
  • Click on the “Authorized users” link in the row of the edx web service:
  • You should see a page with two boxes that you can move users between. You need to choose to move the account that you would like to have access to use the web service (the account you created for edX, your account or some other account with enough permissions) by highlighting it in the “Not authorized users” list on the right and click “Add”.
  • The page will show you any permissions a user is missing at the bottom (Missing capabilities) - If you followed the instructions in Step 4, your user should have all the required capabilities.

2.8 - Create a token for the web services user (that allows edX to act as the Moodle User created in Step 3)

  • Navigate to “Site administration > Server > Web services > Manage tokens”
  • Click the “Add” link
  • User: search for the user created in Step 3 (edX webservice).
  • Service: select the newly created web service in Step 2 (edX)
  • Do NOT enable expiration as this will cause disruptions to edX’s API.
  • With those two pieces of information filled in, click on Save changes.
  • You will see the newly generated Token display on the screen.

2.9 - Enable developer documentation

  • Navigate to “Site administration > Server > Web services > Manage protocols”
  • Check the box next to “Web services documentation”. Despite its name, this is actually used to obtain more helpful errors.

Additional Considerations: Moodle

  • Grading/Completion Reporting Configuration
  • Moodle Documentation Reference
  • Revoking edX Access
  • Credential Rotation
  • Moodle Cloud requirement
  • Additional setting for logging

Grading/Completion Reporting Configuration

In order for edX to send the final grade of a course to Moodle, an assignment needs to be created in Moodle (their API does not support creation of assignments).

As such, we decided on a simplified standardized assignment name: (edX integration) Final Grade

The instructor of each course can create this assignment as follows:

  • Open the course via the Dashboard, My Courses, or even from the Home screen.

  • Over on the far right should be a button that says “Turn editing on” - click that.

  • Also on the right, you should now see a link that says “Add an activity or resource” - click on that.

  • You’ll see a pop up window with a bunch of icons of activity types, click on “Assignment”. From here you should see a form with a lot of options, the only fields you need to fill in are specified as follows:

  • Assignment name - this is the field that requires the name: (edX integration) Final Grade

  • In the Availability section, uncheck every “Enable” box.

  • In the Submission Types section, uncheck “File submissions”

  • In the Submission Settings section, ensure all 3 fields are “No” and/or “Never”

  • In the Grade section:

    • The “Type” should be Point
    • The Maximum value should be 100.
    • And the Grading Method should be Simple direct grading You can add other options here, but these are the bare minimum required.

Once those details have been filled in, click on “Save and return to course” at the bottom.

Any users enrolled in the edx and Moodle course will now have their grades transferred to Moodle.

Moodle Documentation Reference

These instructions, specifically for edX setup, are based on the documentation for Moodle. This additional reference material may be found at: docs.moodle.org

Revoking edX Access

To revoke edX’s access, you should delete the generated token from step 8.

Credential Rotation

If credential rotation is needed, you will need to generate and provide edX a new token without immediately deleting the old one to allow for a no-downtime changeover. Once the changeover has been completed, the old token may be removed.

Moodle Cloud requirement

For Moodle Cloud setups specifically, you have to login as the webservice user and agree to the site policy.

Additional setting for logging

To improve logging over the API, you should enable debug logging.

  • Navigate to Site Administration > Development > Debugging
  • Next to “Debug messages” select “ALL: Show all reasonable PHP debug messages”
  • Click “Save changes” at the bottom of the screen.

edX for Business: Portal Appearance

Highlights FAQs

This article reviews some frequently asked questions pertaining the Highlights function.

What are Highlights & Highlight Collections?

Highlight collections are groups of courses or programs selected by an administrator with a specific purpose in mind. They are displayed prominently (highlighted) to help learners find content that is recommended to them, or relevant for them based on their organization’s learning goals. Administrators can leverage highlights to bring content to the forefront of learners’ attention, while obscuring other learning options that might not fit their needs.

Who Should Create Highlight Collections?

Any organization that has spent time curating content could benefit from using highlight collections. In many instances, learners are faced with choosing from a significant amount of learning content. Highlight collections are an excellent way to match learners to the content you intend for them to leverage

How do I Create Highlight Collections?

Highlight Collections are created by clicking on the “book” icon in the navigation bar on the left side of the administrator portal. On the “Highlights” page, click the “+New” icon to begin creating a highlight collection.

How Many Highlights Can I Display?

Each edX organization can have up to 8 highlight collections, containing up to 12 courses, programs or pathways each. There is no minimum for the number of highlights to be displayed.

Where do I Manage Highlight Collections?

Highlight Collections are managed by clicking the “book” icon in the navigation bar on the left side of the administrator portal. After clicking, administrators will see any existing collections they have created. If no highlights have been created yet, there will be a button available to click and create

What Name Should I Give to a Highlight Collection?

We recommend naming collections to accentuate their purpose or identify their target audience. For example, “Recommendations for Enterprise Marketing” or, “Leadership Skills Development.” This will allow learners to find collections that are relevant to them. Titles can be a maximum of 60 characters long.

How do I see Information about the Content I am Highlighting?

While creating your highlight, you can click the title of each content option and be directed to the content ‘About’ page. You can also see this information retroactively by opening an existing highlight collection from the main menu. Each content title will be clickable directing you to the contents about page

Where do Highlights Appear?

Once a highlight collection has been created, it will appear at the top of the “Find a Course” page in the enterprise learner portal.

In Which Order will Highlights and Collections Display?

Highlight collections will display on the learner portal “Find a Course” page from newest to oldest (top to bottom.) Within a highlight collection, content will display from left to right based on the order in which it was selected by the administrator.

How do I Delete a Highlight Collection?

To delete a highlight collection, click the “book” icon in the navigation bar on the left side of the administrator portal. Then, select the tile for the existing highlight collection to be deleted. Click “Delete” in the top right corner and confirm when prompted by the modal. As mentioned, deleting is a permanent action and cannot be undone.

What Happens if I Don’t Create Highlights?

Highlights and highlight collections are entirely optional for administrators. The learner experience will remain unaltered unless a collection is created and saved.

What is “Content Visibility?”

Content visibility is a setting section found within the “Highlights” tab of the Enterprise administrator portal. The default setting allows highlight collections to be viewed as additive alongside edX’s courses and programs that are available to learners on the “Find a Course” page of the enterprise learner portal. The second setting is for administrators who wish to restrict learners’ access to only those courses and programs included in highlight collections. When enabled, learners will be unable to search and courses outside of highlights will be removed from the “Find a Course” page.

What if my Organization has Purchased Multiple Catalogs of Content?

Certain organizations purchase a range of edX education options for different audiences. When creating a highlight collection, administrators will see a superset of all courses available to their organization. When the collection is displayed, learners will not see any courses to which they personally do not have subsidized access.

For more information or assistance with Highlights, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

How can I match the portal’s theme to my organization’s branding colors?

This article reviews the methods for matching the portal theme and your organization’s branding colors.

Within the Administrator Portal section Settings > Portal Appearance, you will be able to configure the colors for the Administrator and Learner Portals. Select your three colors and input the corresponding hexadecimal codes in the related fields.

Please note that your Custom Theme will need to meet the Web Content Accessibility Guidelines 2.0.

Hexadecimal code, or a hex triplet is one of the ways colors are represented to applications. If you think the validator is incorrectly saying your color is invalid, make sure you have a pound sign (#) at the beginning and that there are no spaces before or after.

The links below are some helpful websites to find hex colors from an image, if you wish to upload your logo or a screenshot from your website to pick the colors.

imagecolorpicker.com

redketchup.io

(Please note that these above liked resources are outside links and not associated with edX or 2U)

Accessibility

We highly encourage that all custom themes meet the Web Content Accessibility Guidelines AA standard. The two colors (button and banner) are checked against the size of the font used on these elements against the text color so that all students can perceive, understand, navigate, and interact with the content.

If your color is not meeting the standard, try finding a shade of that color that is darker or lighter, or consider adding this color as the accent, which does not need to meet any accessibility standards. Read more about edX’s accessibility policy here.

For more information or assistance with Portal Appearance, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

Upload your logo or select a different theme to change the theming for your organization’s portal. You may insert a logo by dragging and dropping a file, or clicking to upload from your computer.

Logo images must be in .png format and not exceed 500KB in size.

For the logo, we encourage you to use a horizontal logo with a transparent or white background. Here is a resource to create a transparent background from an uploaded image.

The two colors (button and banner) are checked against the size of the font used on these elements against the text color so that all students can perceive, understand, navigate, and interact with the content.

I need reports emailed to me. How do I set that up?

This article describes the steps needed to acquire learner reports via email.

The edX Admin Portal allows you to configure your own catalog metadata, and learner progress reports for receipt on a cadence of your choosing, delivered as a password-protected compressed (ZIP) file in JSON or CSV format via email (SMTP) or secure file transport (SFTP).

To configure these reports, follow the instructions below:

  • Login to the edX Admin Portal: portal.edx.org using your admin account
  • Using the Left toolbar, navigate to Reporting Configurations (contact your edX Account Management representative if you need help obtaining access to this feature).
  • Select “Add a reporting configuration”
  • Complete the fields required for the configuration, as follows: - Active: Checking this box will ensure the report is sent. This can be unchecked at later time if you do not wish to receive the report anymore - Data Type: The type of report you want to receive. - Catalog: This will send a catalog metadata report, including all of the courses available to your organization in your edX Curated Catalog - Progress: This will send a learner progress report with information about the courses learners at your organization are enrolled in. - Delivery Method: The transmission mechanism to use for delivery of the report, which can be either Email or SFTP. Regardless of the selection, the report file will be delivered as a password-protected compressed (ZIP) file. - Report Type: The representation format to use, which can be either CSV or JSON - Frequency: The interval for which to deliver the report, which can be either daily, weekly, or monthly. - Day of Month: The day of the month to send the report (Required and only valid when the frequency is monthly) - Day of Week: The day of the week to send the report (Required and only valid when the frequency is weekly) - Hour of Day: The hour of the day to send the report, in Eastern Time (24-hour format). This field is required for all frequency settings
  • If your chosen Delivery Method is email, you will also need to complete the following fields - Email(s): The email addresses where the report should be sent. Please include one address per line - Password: This password will be used to secure the compressed (ZIP) file. Please specify the password in clear-text format. It will be encrypted by the edX system when stored in the database. If lost, you will need to submit a new password.
  • If your chosen Delivery Method is SFTP, you will also need to complete the following fields - SFTP Host Name: The host name of the SFTP service to deliver the report. Please provision a unique SFTP service account specifically for edX’s use. - SFTP Port Number: The connection port made available by the SFTP service. The standard port number for SFTP services is 22. - SFTP Username: The service username used to securely access the SFTP service. Please provision a unique SFTP service account specifically for edX’s use. - Decrypted SFTP Password: The service password used to security access the SFTP service. Specify the password in clear-text format. If lost, you will need to submit a new password. - SFTP File Path: The path (directory) on the SFTP service to store the report file.
  • If your chosen Data Type is catalog, you will also need to complete the following fields - Enterprise Customer Catalogs: Select which catalogs should be included in your report. If left blank, all catalogs will be included.
  • Submit and wait for your report! For more information or assistance with Report Configuration, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

Removing licenses in bulk

This article explains how to remove assigned licenses in bulk using the administrator dashboard

  1. In your administrators dashboard, click on SUBSCRIPTION MANAGEMENT

  2. Open the desired license batch (if you have multiple) and click MANAGE LEARNERS on the far right of the screen.

  3. On the left of the screen is the FILTERS box. Check PENDING. This will show you a list of users who have not activated their assigned license.

  4. To select all learners at once, check the box at the top of the list contained in the grey bar. You will then notice each name (email address) is now checked. Alternatively, you can manually select the names (emails) you wish to remove in bulk by checking the box to the left of the learner’s name.

  5. Once you are completed checking the boxes you wish to remove, notice at the top right of the section 2 boxes (REMIND) (ENROLL) to the left of those two boxes are three dots. Click on that icon. A new box will appear (REVOKE). Clicking this button will revoke license for each learner you had checked previously.

For more information or assistance with this feature, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable).

What is a Learner Credit?

This article explains what a Learner Credit is and how to utilize them.

Learner Credit, also referred to as “offers”, enable learner to enroll in a course without asking for payment or a code. Learner Credits are established by the administrator and are controlled by spend budget, available courses or catalogs.

*PRO TIP: Once a Learner Credit budget has been established, it cannot be changed. Contact edX for Business Support for guidance.

NOTE: As of October 1, 2023, this function is only possible if the employer is using single sign-on (SSO) or a link for learners to be able to be tied to the enterprise (Universal Link). Future releases of the platform will improve this function and learner experience.

For more information or assistance with this feature, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

edX for Business: Program Design

Could you please explain what is meant by “subsidy” or license, code, and learner credit?

A subsidy is how your organization enables learners to enroll in a course. Licenses, codes, and learner credit (also referred to as “offers”) are all different subsidies.

License refers to our subscription licenses. It offers unlimited access to many of our most popular courses. Our subscription catalogs only include self-paced courses, and excludes most MicroMasters courses. Please see the full catalog for Business clients here and the full catalog for Education clients here.

Codes or coupon codes are typically 100% discount codes and do not have a specific value attached, though we do create discount codes if the contract specifies such. They cannot be used for programs as a whole but can be used for the courses within a program, but rather issued to cover the individual courses which may be applied to upgrade individual courses that are part of a program in order for learners to complete the program’ courses. Codes are managed by a designated admin at the customer’s company or organization. Learn more about Code Management here.

Learner Credit, also referred to as “offers”, enable learner to enroll in a course without asking for payment or a code.

NOTE: As of October 1, 2023, this function is only possible if the employer is using single sign-on (SSO) or a link for learners to be able to be tied to the enterprise. Future releases of the platform will improve this function and learner experience.

How do I know which codes are for which courses?

This article explains how course names are determined and can identified within the Admin Portal

Our naming convention for codes is typically:

  • CLIENT NAME
  • # of Codes
  • Type of Catalog (ALL, ECC for Enterprise Curated Catalog, or Specific Course, Subject, or Partner Name)
  • Date of creation For example, ACME-50ALL-NOV2021 means 50 codes that can be used for all courses. If you had only one course you purchased, it might look something like ACME-100ExercisingLdrshp-NOV21 for 100 codes for Exercising Leadership from HarvardX.

For more information or assistance with Code Denominations, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

Learning Program Best Practices

This article explains the best practices to adhere to for Learning Programs

Best Practices: Program Design

Best Practices: Reinforcement

Establish clear expectations

Tie learning to employee goals and other measurable outcomes. Encourage managers to include the completion of edX courses in employees’ quarterly goals.

Behavior Modeling

Have leadership, managers and other high-profile employees enroll in courses; share their experiences; and serve as resources.

Set finite amounts of time for self-paced course completion

Courses with defined, speedy times to completion see higher rates of success.

Conversely, while self-paced courses allow for greater levels of flexibility, they also can see higher attrition and loss of learner motivation due to the long timeframe.

Use the expected course length as a guidepost, adding ~2 weeks of additional time for self-paced courses (e.g., give 10 weeks for an 8 week course).

Reinforcement through social/in-context learning

Provide opportunities to apply new skills and knowledge on the job to cement learning. Understanding how courses and programs help to solve work-related problems creates a connection to the course material, underscores the value of learning programs and supports motivation, engagement and completion. Mentorship also reinforces and expands upon learning and how it applies to the organization. Mentorship can be a formal program or simple activities to connect learners and subject matter experts such as discussion forums on internal communications channels or lunch-and-learns.

Develop company-specific cohorts

Enroll learners at the same time. This helps you:

• Track how your learners are pacing against the course and each other

• Facilitate social learning across the org

• Create opportunities to apply and share new skills on the job

• Apply positive “peer pressure”

Use insights from the administrator dashboard to make decisions and take action

Use the administrator dashboard to track how learners are progressing and ping them before course end dates as well as to check in on a regular cadence. Learners report that these gentle nudges have a positive impact on their motivation and performance.

Make space for learning during the workday

Research shows there is a statistically significant positive relationship between workday learning and course completion rates, even if just a few minutes.

Celebrate Success

Recognition and motivation go hand-in-hand. Even the smallest acknowledgements help to support learner engagement. Further, it can serve as encouragement and endorsement of the learning program and lead to wider employee participation.

Select first AND second courses

Learners’ engagement and completion rates gain significant momentum after completing their first course. Support success by helping your learners move quickly into their second course.

Use edX programs as learning pathways

edX programs are designed by course teams from the best institutions in the world to develop specific skill sets and knowledge bases. Use program curriculum as a pathway for your learners.

For more information or assistance with Learning Program Best Practices, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

Setting up a Successful Program

This article explains the steps for setting up a successful program

Most customer contracts run for one year. In addition, learners take on average 6-12 weeks to complete a course. For easier management of your time, program and investment, and to keep your employees on the path to success, a best practice is to plan activities in a quarterly cadence.

Quarter 1: Program Launch and Kick-Off

  • Make learners aware of the subsidy and courses that they can enroll in

  • Take a look at last activity date to ensure employees are engaged Quarter 2: Learner Progress and Encouragement

  • Nudge learners who are noticeably behind on the course schedule

  • Re-engage learners who have not been active for 2+ weeks Quarter 3: Celebrate Success and Enrollments

  • Collect & share success stories as social proof in your company & consider sharing with edX!

  • Survey your learners about their experiences

  • Check-in with the Customer Success or Account Management team Quarter 4: Program Evaluation

  • Work with your customer success associate and account manager to evaluate the performance of your program.

  • Identify the key skills and knowledge learners have gained throughout the program.

  • (Where applicable) Use any remaining prepaid funds. For more information or assistance with Program Success, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

Template: Learner Success Stories

This article provides a template for Learner Success Stories

We have found that recognition and motivation go hand-in-hand. Even the smallest acknowledgements help to support learner engagement. Use the attached template as a model for a learner success story you can create and share with your organization.

For more information or assistance with Learner Success Stories, submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

edX for Business: Single Sign-On (SSO)

Additional Resources to learn about SSO

To get more familiar with SSO we recommend the following articles:

  • From OKTA (SSO provider): A deep dive article on how SSO works, type of SSO, explanation of common SSO myths and the benefits and challenges of SSO.
  • From OneLogin (SSO provider): SAML explained in plain English. It also includes a diagram to learn about how service providers and identify the provider establish a “Trust relationship”.
  • Youtube Video for a deep dive on Technical Explanation on SAML with clear diagrams.

Configuring Single Sign-on (SSO) within the edX Admin Portal

Single sign-on, or SSO, enables learners who are signed in to their enterprise LMS or other system to easily register and enroll in courses on edX without needing to sign in or maintain an additional set of credentials. edX for Business uses SAML 2.0 to implement SSO between an enterprise system and edX.org.

Implementing SSO using SAML

In an exchange of authentication and authorization data using SAML, an identity provider (IdP) securely asserts the identity and access rights of a set of users to a service provider (SP) that allows access to users on the basis of the credentials provided by the identity provider. In the context of edX for Business, your enterprise represents the identity provider, and edX represents the service provider. To use SAML for SSO with edX for Business, your identity provider must use the SAML 2.0 standard for authentication.

To setup the integration between your SAML IdP and edX, will need to have the following information:

  • IdP Metadata: The URL for your SAML Identity Provider metadata XML file. See Exchanging Metadata.
  • User Attributes: A list of the values that your SAML system asserts when users sign in to edX. For more information about how you can work with edX to configure user attributes effectively, see Configuring User Attributes. If you will be setting up SSO in both your test and production systems, you will need Metadata XML files/attributes, and 2 edX configurations in order to validate the integration.
Getting Started

To create a new SSO configuration, you will need administrator access to your organization’s edX Admin Portal. SSO configuration can be found in the left side menu under “Settings”. To set up a new SSO integration click the (+). If you do not have this tab available, please reach out to your Customer Success Representative.

1) Input your Identity Provider Metadata

Begin by selecting your Identity Provider (If you don’t see yours listed select “other”) Next, enter the Identity Provider Metadata URL or upload the Identity Provider Metadata XML file.

2) Configuring User Attributes

A SAML identity provider sends a set of user attributes in an assertion document each time it interacts with a SAML service provider. By default, edX expects that the assertion document will include at least the following user attributes:

Attribute

Name

URN

User identifier

userid

urn:oid:0.9.2342.19200300.100.1.1

Full name

commonName

urn:oid:2.5.4.3

First name

givenName

urn:oid:2.5.4.42

Last name

surname

urn:oid:2.5.4.4

Suggested username

userid

urn:oid:0.9.2342.19200300.100.1.1

Email address

mail

urn:oid:0.9.2342.19200300.100.1.3

Country

country

urn:oid:0.9.2342.19200300.100.1.43

If your identity provider is configured to send attributes under a name other than the defaults listed above, configure the names that should be expected on each login.

3) Add edX as a SAML Service Provider

Download the edX Service Provider metadata XML file and upload it to your system’s SSO configuration to authorize edX as Service Provider. Once edX has been configured as a Service Provider, check the box and continue to testing. It may take a few minutes for setup to complete, so look for an email in your inbox letting you know that it is time to test.

4) Test the SSO Connection

Use an incognito browser to validate the SSO connection for edX. If possible, screen record your test and share with your Customer Success Representative if you run into any issues.

Single Sign-On and Learner Identity Verification

Normally, learners who want to qualify to earn a certificate for an edX proctored course must verify their identity, using a process that requires a webcam and a government-issued photo ID.

If you have implemented an SAML SSO integration, edX can optionally configure your edX for Business account so that your learners who access edX through the SSO integration do not need to separately verify their identities. Instead, the SAML SSO authentication process is sufficient to verify their identities for the purposes of qualifying for an edX certificate.

For more information please contact customersuccess@edx.org

  • Which is your Identity provider?
  • SSO attribute mapping

edX for Business: Subscription Licenses

Can I restrict our subscription catalog?

IIt is not presently possible to restrict your subscription catalog on the edX platform. If you paid to integrate with an LMS, you are likely able to limit the courses that are visible to learners there.

How do I assign a course to my learners?

The edX platform is built to empower individuals in owning their own learning, so for this reason they must enroll using their own accounts.

Currently, only organizations with a Subscription Plan have the ability to assign courses to learners.

You can do so by navigating to the Subscription Management screen, selecting the desired learners and then clicking ‘Enroll’.

How do I assign a license?

This article explains how to assign a license to a learner.

You can assign subscription licenses to users by navigating to the Subscription Management tab in the Administrator Dashboard.

Once you have navigated to your subscription plan, you will be able to Invite Learners by inputting their email addresses, or uploading a CSV UTF-8 file. The system will notify you if a learner has already been assigned a license.

For more information or assistance about Assigning a Subscription License submit a support ticket to enterprise-support@edx.org, or reach out to your account manager (where applicable)

How long do pending license assignments stay active?

Due to GDPR regulations, we can only keep user emails for 90 days. If a learner has not activated their license within this period, the pending assignment will be purged. The pending license will be added back to the number of licenses you have available for assignment.

Is there a limit to the number of enrollments an Administrator can make using the Bulk Enrollment tool?

Yes. Administrators will be able to assign up to 800 course enrollments at a time. Course enrollments can be understood as (number of courses) x (number of learners). We expect this scale to increase in subsequent versions.

If you plan to exceed this limit, you will have to break up your assignments into groups of less than 800 enrollments.

My learner is in the audit track even though the course is included in the license. Why are they being prompted for payment?

This sometimes occurs if the learner does not enroll from within the Learner Portal. Learners can follow the below steps to enroll in the Verified track from the Learner Portal:

  • Please go to enterprise.edx.org. Once you are at enterprise.edx.org, please ensure there are no dialogue messages letting you know that you have an inactive subscription license.
  • Do not click on your course or the continue learning button. Instead, click Find a course to search your course, find the course, and click View course.
  • You will know you are in the correct place if you see a banner with your organization name across the top. Please only enroll in courses if you see your organization’s banner at the top, and the URL begins with enterprise.edx.org, or you may continue to encounter this issue.

One of my learners is having trouble activating their subscription license. Are there steps to troubleshoot?

Subscription licenses can only be activated by the email address that the license has been assigned to. The learner should ensure that the email they have been sent the license activation link matches the email address on their edX account.

If they have not yet created an edX account, they will need to register here with the email address that was assigned the license. If they have an edX account with a different email, they may also choose to edit their edX account email to match the email their licenses was assigned by following the instructions linked below:

support.edx.org

Once the email on their edX account matches the email the learner was assigned a license, they may click the activation link while signed into edX.org to activate their license.

Once activated, the learner will be prompted to provide data sharing consent if applicable. From there, the learner will be in the Learner Portal and can select “Find A Course” to begin using their subscription to enroll in courses. The learner will know they are in the correct place if the URL starts with enterprise.edx.org, and they see a banner with your organization name across the top (otherwise, they will be enrolling in courses through their personal edX account).

If the learner continues to experience difficulty activating their license, please open a request below by clicking “Submit a Request”, including a full page screen shot of their difficulty and including the URL link, and we would be happy to troubleshoot. You may also direct the learner to our Learner Support team where they may submit a help ticket at courses.edx.org

What happens when a learner no longer has an active subscription? (revocation, expiration)

When a subscription expires, learners will no longer have Verified access to the uncompleted courses they have enrolled in using your subscription license. Any course learners have enrolled in through a subscription will revert to the audit access only.

If they wish to continue in a course in the Verified track, and the course’s verified upgrade deadline has NOT passed, they may choose to upgrade to verified and pay the upgrade fee at their own cost.

If learners earned a course certificate during their subscription, they can access the certificate from their dashboard.

You can learn more in our help center article here.

What happens when I revoke a license?

In relevant organizations, administrators have the ability to revoke subscription licenses from learners any time during their active contract. Once a subscription license has been revoked, it can then be re-assigned to a different learner for active use. Don’t worry, edX will know the transition has occurred and keep the data for the two learners separated. However, revoking a license does change the user experience significantly.

Revoking a subscription license will:

  • Prevent the former license holder from enrolling in additional courses at no cost

  • Downgrade any courses that were in progress to the “audit track.” In the audit track, the former license holder is prevented from passing further assessments and earning a certificate Revoking a license does NOT:

  • Delete the former license holder’s account

  • Invalidate or delete previous accomplishments from the former license holder. All earned certificates remain available for download and associated with the learner

  • Prevent the former license holder from logging into your organization’s learner portal in a “view only” mode

  • Prevent the former license holder from taking future courses on edx.org, as an individual pursuing their personal learning journey

  • Prohibit the administrator from re-assigning a license to the learner in question at a later date
