3. SAP SuccessFactors Integration Configuration Steps

The edX for Business integration with SAP SuccessFactors enables you to offer a curated catalog of edX courses to your organization’s learners through your SAP SuccessFactors learning platform.

Main Steps

To integrate edX for Business with your SAP SuccessFactors instance, follow these main steps:

Step 1. Configuration requirements: make sure you have met the prerequisites for the integration.

Step 2. Locating relevant pieces of information for configuration Find and gather the relevant Keys and IDs to set up SAML and OCN access.

Step 3. Configuring SAML-based Single Sign-on. This enables your organization’s learners to access the edX platform without needing to create a separate edX account.

Step 4. Enabling the Open Content Network in your SAP SuccessFactors instance.

Step 5. Importing edX content to SuccessFactors LMS learning items.

Step 6. Learn More about the Open Content Network (OCN)

Technical Support

If you need assistance or have questions about the integration, you can contact us via email at: enterprise-support@edx.org

Step 1. Configuration requirements

Prerequisites

To successfully integrate the edX course catalog with SuccessFactors, you need to have:

  • An agreement with edX covering the SuccessFactors integration
  • SuccessFactors release Q2 2017 or later
  • SuccessFactors super administrator privileges for your team

Step 2. Configuring a SuccessFactors integration using the Admin Portal

You will need to locate and input the following fields so to successfully configure the SuccessFactors OCN integration.

  1. Course Selection Criteria - Choose from the list of edX subject areas provided by your edX integration manager. EdX will use your course selection criteria in selecting the courses that will be imported into SuccessFactors using the edX SuccessFactors OCN integration. Before you have made your actual selections, edX can test the integration using a default set of courses.

  2. SAML IdP & OData API Access Information - The URL for the SAML configuration information that edX will use for Single Sign-On authentication. edX will perform a daily refresh of metadata made available via this URL. You will need the URL for both the production system and the test or staging system. You may instead submit a static copy (file) that contains your SAML IdP metadata.

To establish a SAML connection with your SuccessFactors instance, edX needs to upload your identity provider (IdP) metadata. The metadata includes configuration information such as your entity ID, endpoints, and signing certificate. edX needs the URL for both the production system and the test or staging system.

For more detailed information, see How to locate your SuccessFactors metadata URL.

To enable learner account auto-registration, edX needs to be registered as a client application in SuccessFactors. In addition, you need to generate and upload to edX certificate values and an API key. For information about how to do this, see Configuring OData API Access.

  1. OCN API Access Information - EdX needs this information to import course metadata and to transmit learner completion data. You can find this information in your SuccessFactors instance at SuccessFactors > Admin Center > Learning Administration > System Admin > OAuth Token Server. You need to provide edX with the following information for both the production system and the test or staging system.
  • Your Learning (LMS) hostname, which you can copy from the location bar of your web browser. For example, if the URL is: partner123.example.successfactors.com, then the LMS hostname is partner123.example.
  • Company ID, Client ID, Client Secret, and Client Secret Hash values. Note that if you generate a new Client Secret, that will invalidate any existing Client Secret value, which may be in use by other clients, applications, or OCN providers that you have configured. For more information see section 7.2.2.1, “Required OAuth Token Parameters”, of the Web Services for Open Content Network Partners SAP SuccessFactors Learning Integration Guide.
  1. SuccessFactors API Configuration - edX needs a SuccessFactors Admin account so that we can use the OCN API to push course metadata and learner completion events to your OCN database. For more information about how to do this, please see: SuccessFactors API Configuration

Step 2.D) How to locate your SuccessFactors metadata URL

To locate your SuccessFactors metadata URL, follow this procedure.

  1. In the SuccessFactors Admin Center menu, select Learning, then select Learning Administration.

  2. In the SuccessFactors navigation menu, select System Admin.

  3. In the left menu, select Configuration, then select System Configuration.

  4. Under Configuration, find the BizX option and click the view button (the magnifying glass icon).

  5. The BizX configuration file opens. In the configuration file, scroll down to the SAML Single Sign On section.

  6. Find the metadataProviders.IDP1.value entry. The value should look like the sample URL below:

https://performancemanager321.successfactors.com/idp/samlmetadata?company=<company_id>``7.\``Copy and paste the metadata value into an email to your edX integration manager.

  1. Your edX integration manager will reply with your organization’s unique edX OrgID. You will need your unique edX OrgID to complete the Open Content Network property file configuration, as described in Step 4. Enabling the Open Content Network.

Step 2.F) Configuring OData API Access in SuccessFactors

Learner account auto-registration provides a smoother experience for your learners to enroll in edX courses that they discover through the OCN. To enable learner account auto-registration, you need to register the edX SuccessFactors OCN integration as a client application in SuccessFactors.

To enable learner account auto-registration

  • In SuccessFactors, navigate to SuccessFactors > Admin Center > Company Settings > Manage OAuth2 Client Applications > Register Client Application.

  1. Under Application Name, enter edX OCN Integration.

  2. Under Description, enter OAuth configuration information to enable account auto-registration for the edX Open Content Network provider.

  3. Under Application URL, enter https://www.edx.org

  4. Click Generate X.509 Certificate:

  5. Under Common Name, enter your SuccessFactors company identifier (for example, SFPART011327). The remaining fields, such as Organization Name, are optional.

  6. Click Generate

  7. Click Register, then View to obtain the API Key value.

  8. Paste the X.509 certificate file contents as the ‘sapsf_private_key’ and API Key using the SSO Configuration Form for SAP SuccessFactors SAML setup.

Perform this procedure for both the production system and the test or staging system.

Be sure to record a separate calendar entry or reminder to remind you when it is time to renew the certificate before it expires. See F) OData API Access Information in the SAP SuccessFactors Help Portal for more information.

Step 2.G) SuccessFactors API Configuration

For edX to push new content and learner progress into SuccessFactors LMS, a system user must be provisioned, and API access must be granted. In this section, we will review the steps required to authorize communication between the two systems.

Before proceeding, ensure you have configured and enabled SSO for your edX account. If you enable content syncing before completing the SSO configuration, course launches will fail.

Create OCN Admin Role

To create an OCN admin profile in SuccessFactors, take the following steps:

  • Log in to the SuccessFactors Learning Administration environment and go to System Administration > Security > Role Management.

  • Click Add New.

  • In the Role ID field, enter “OCN_ADMIN”.

  • Click Add.

  • Select Permissions, then click the link under Add Permissions to the Role.

  • Under Add Permissions to Roles, enable Learning Activities, Search, and People Management. :

  • Set Status Restriction to “Active” for each activity under Permission Restrictions:

  • Add Item

  • Add Item Libraries

  • Edit Item

  • Edit item Libraries

  • Edit Item Finance

  • Edit Item Cost/Price

  • Edit Item Custom Fields

  • Edit Item State

  • Open Content Network Import

  • View Item

  • View Item Libraries

  • View Item Finance

  • View Item Cost/Price

  • View Item Custom Fields

  • Search User

  • Add History Record

  • Add Learning History (more info in SAP’s Help Center)

  • Click Apply Changes.

Create System Admin User

To create a system admin user in SuccessFactors, take the following steps:

  • Log in to the SuccessFactors Learning Administration environment and go to System Administration > Security > Administrators.
  • In the upper right-hand corner of the screen, click Add New.
  • In the Admin ID field, enter “edx_admin”.
  • Enter a new password in the password fields.
  • Click Add.

  1. Select the Assigned Roles tab. If this integration is your first SuccessFactors instance, click Edit.

  2. In the Role ID field, enter “OCN_ADMIN”, then click Add.

  3. Select the Summary tab.

  4. Scroll to the bottom of the page and select Generate a new Client Secret. Save the client secret to your clipboard or desktop for later reference.

IMPORTANT:

Do not copy the hashed secret value. The client secret should not contain dollar signs or any special characters.

Step 3. Configuring SAML-based single sign-on with SuccessFactors

The edX SuccessFactors OCN integration uses SAML-based single sign-on between edX and SuccessFactors. edX does not currently support Single Log-Out; however, the edX platform does terminate stale SuccessFactors sessions.

Configure SAP SuccessFactors with the edX Assertion Consumer Service URL.

You can do this in SuccessFactors at SuccessFactors > Provisioning > Company Settings > Service Provider Settings > Authorized SP Assertion Consumer Service Settings.

Set the ACS URL value to – courses.edx.org

Note – The SuccessFactors Customers who do not have access to the Provisioning can open dialog with the SuccessFactors team by creating a support ticket (https://launchpad.support.sap.com) and copy & paste the SSO configuration values from the edX interface into the template below.

================================================================

SuccessFactors Support,

My organization would like to integrate with edX via the Open Content Network. To complete the single sign-on setup, a new authorized SP assertion consumer service needs to be configured in your back-end provisioning settings. Below you will find the relevant details provided by edX for the assertion consumer service configuration:

ACS URL value to – courses.edx.org

Regards,

Your Signature

================================================================

Step 4. Enabling the Open Content Network

After you have completed the SSO configuration for your edX SAP SuccessFactors integration, the next step is to enable the Open Content Network (OCN). OCN is a joint initiative between SAP SuccessFactors, edX, and other online learning companies to integrate high-quality educational content with a SuccessFactors instance.

To enable OCN in your SuccessFactors instance, complete the following steps:

  1. In the SuccessFactors Learning Administration navigation menu, select System Admin.

  2. In the left menu, select Configuration, and then scroll down to System Configuration and select it.

  3. In the configuration file, find the Open Content Network option and click the edit button.

  4. In the configuration file, locate the edX Provider section.

  5. Edit the values in the edX Provider section to match the following:

`# edX Provider

providers[EDX].enabled=true

providers[EDX].name=edX

providers[EDX].orgID=[use the orgID provided by edX]`

You should not need to modify any other providers[EDX] values.

  1. Select Apply Changes.

  2. Scroll Down and make any necessary modifications to the configuration. For Example:

  • What will be the Domain of the courses: defaultValues.domainID[default]=
  • Do you want the courses to have an Approval Process? If yes, set: defaultValues.isApprovalRequired[default]=true
  • What will be the Approval Process associated with these courses: defaultValues.processDefinitionID[default]=
  • Set the Item Type: defaultValues.itemType[default]=
  • Set the Item Completion Status: defaultValues.itemCompletionStatus[default]=
  • Set the Item Failure Status: defaultValues.itemFailureStatus[default]= Click Apply Changes.

Step 5. Importing edX content to SuccessFactors learning items

After you have configured SSO and configured the SuccessFactors LMS to load edX course content, you can import edX content into SuccessFactors LMS learning items.

To import content items into the SuccessFactors LMS, complete the following steps:

  1. Run the “Open Content Network Content Synchronization” Automatic Processes:
  • Click System Admin > Automatic Processes > Open Content Network Content Synchronization
  • Schedule this Process > Apply Changes
  1. Importing edX courses via Learning tab:
  • In the SuccessFactors Learning Administration navigation menu, select Learning.
  • In the left menu, select Content Network.
  • Select edX, and then select Search.
  • Select the content items that you want to import.
  • Click Import Selected.

Step 6. Learn More about the Open Content Network (OCN)

If you are an SAP SuccessFactors customer, you can learn more about the SuccessFactors Open Content Network in these resources in the SAP Support Knowledge Base.

  • Open Content Network (OCN) Implementation: help.sap.com
  • LMS OCN Open Content Network Knowledge Support and Tips - apps.support.sap.com
  • FAQ-OCN and Personal Information Data – apps.support.sap.com
    • This Knowledge Base article includes links to the following PDF documents.
      • Web Services for Open Content Network Providers – apps.support.sap.com
      • Open Content Network Overview – apps.support.sap.com

Enrolling in edX Courses or Programs

Are there prerequisites for my course?

For most edX courses, there are no firm prerequisites. In other words, you won’t be blocked from enrolling without completing the prerequisites.

If a course has any prerequisites, they are listed on the course introduction page under At a glance.

These prerequisites are recommendations to ensure your success in the course, but in most cases this is not a strict requirement. For advanced capstone courses (such as the final course in a MicroMasters program), the course team may not allow learners who have not completed the required program courses to enroll.

Can I re-take a course?

Yes! You may re-take any of your courses when it’s offered in a future session. Each offering of a course is assessed independently and most edX courses do repeat. Please note that payment for one session doesn’t apply to a future session: Can I transfer to another session?

However, it’s not possible to start fresh or reset your course progress in the same session, so make sure you study and prepare well before taking graded assignments.

Note: Un-enrolling from and re-enrolling in the same course session won’t reset your course progress.

For updates on new and repeat course runs, keep watching for our newsletter updates via email. You can also keep up-to-date by “liking” edX on Facebook or following us on Twitter.

Can I take more than one course at a time?

Yes! You may take multiple edX courses. We recommend checking the requirements on each course description page to balance your availability and schedule with the course requirements and pacing.

Can you help me choose a course?

Search the course catalog

We welcome you to enroll in any and all courses that you think will be interesting or helpful for you. We encourage you to first enroll in the free audit track to preview the course and decide if it’s right for your goals.

If you decide a course is not right for you, there’s no penalty for un-enrolling or not participating in the course.

If you upgrade to the verified track, please note that there are requirements in order to obtain the certificate.

Search the course catalog

You can browse all of our courses in the course catalog at edX.org. You can toggle your catalog search by:

  • Subject (View all our course subjects and topics here)
  • Skill: Select a skill you’d like to learn.
  • Partner (Browse all our partners here)
  • Program (Browse all our programs here)
  • Level (Introductory, Intermediate, or Advanced)
  • Availability (Available Now or Upcoming)
    • Available Now: These courses are active and you can start learning right away.
    • Upcoming: These courses aren’t active yet, but they will be soon and you can enroll now.
  • Learning Type: Choose from open courses, programs, Boot Camps, online Degree programs, or Executive Education.
  • Language: Select a language to limit your search to only courses offered in that language.
    • We offer courses in over 20 languages. While most of our courses (3000+) are offered in English, we also offer hundreds in Spanish. Click on a course or program to learn more on the course enrollment page or program enrollment page.

A course I am interested in has already started. Can I still enroll?

Yes, you can still enroll! Many of our self-paced courses can be joined months after the start date.

For our instructor-paced courses, you may join late but you will not be able to turn in any assignments or exams that are past due. If it is early in the course, you might still be able to complete enough assignments and exams to earn a certificate.

For detailed information on how to find any due dates in your course, please read this article: Does my course have due dates?

How can I find or meet other learners?

All edX courses have discussion forums where you can chat with and help other learners within the framework of the honor code. For more information, see How do course discussions work?

Your course team might also have other ways of creating communities in your course, such as a Meetup group, or a Facebook page. We don’t recommend sharing personal, private information such as your phone number or mailing address in the forums.

Some courses include team projects, which you complete by working in small groups with other learners.

How do I choose a course session?

Contents of this article

  • Choosing a session
  • Upgrading to verified from the audit track
  • Joining a session within 24 months
  • Changing or leaving sessions
  • Unenrolling and getting a refund

Choosing a session

Note: This article applies only to courses purchased as a program bundle that offer the ability to change sessions.

A course session is the period during which a course is held. A course might offer several sessions scheduled over the next year or so. For example, a course might have two available sessions:

  • Session 1: January 10, 2022 to May 12, 2022
  • Session 2: August 22, 2022 to December 23, 2022 When you purchase a program you may receive the ability to choose a session instead of being directly enrolled in the current course. Some courses only show the currently available session, but some courses might offer multiple sessions over the next year.

For these courses you see options to select a session, change to a different session, or leave a session. You must select a session before you can access the course.

To select a session, click Select session.

Then you will find different sessions listed by their start date.

Once you have made a selection, click the Confirm Session button.

If there are no sessions available, the Select Session button will be inactive and there will be a message, ‘There are no sessions available at the moment.’

Upgrading from the Audit Track to Verified
  • If you were already enrolled in the audit track of a program’s course and you purchase that program as a bundle, we automatically enroll you in the verified track of the course, in the same session. You can change to a different session if the one we enrolled you in does not work for you.
Joining a Session Within 24 Months
  • After you purchase the verified track for a course, you have 24 months from the date of purchase to join a course session. After 24 months, if you have not joined a session, your purchase expires, and you can no longer access the course.
Changing or Leaving Sessions
  • After you join a course session you can change to another session or leave the session within 14 days, as long as you have not completed the course.
  • Leaving a session is not the same as unenrolling from the course. If the current session does not work for you, you can leave a session and wait to decide on joining a later session of the course.
  • To do so, click Change or Leave Session below the course’s title. After you click Change or leave session, a modal will pop up. If you wish to select a different session, select one of the other sessions listed and click Confirm Session.

To leave the session without choosing another, select Leave session.

To cancel changing the session, click Nevermind.

Note: When you change sessions, your course progress is not carried from your current session to your new session.

Unenrolling and Getting a Refund
  • After selecting a session, you can unenroll from the course entirely and get a refund of your course purchase, as long as you have not been in a course session for more than 14 days*.
  • If the refund eligibility period has passed (meaning that you will not receive a refund when you unenroll), the unenroll option is not available.
  • And your original payment is within 180 days.

How do I enroll in a course?

You can search through our many courses in the edX course catalog. When you find a course that looks interesting, click the course thumbnail to view more about the course.

On the course introduction page, you’ll view detailed information about the course. To register for a course, click the Enroll button.

​​

Some courses may have more than one session available. In this case, there will be multiple Enroll buttons with the start and end date clarified for each.

Clicking the Enroll button will lead you through registering on edx.org if you haven’t done so already. Once completed the course will be listed on your dashboard.

If the course is archived, if enrollment is still open you can click View Course Materials. What does archived mean?

Please note that while the course will display on the dashboard before it starts, you will not be able to open the course or access materials until the course start date passes. If the course has started you will find a View Course button. Click this to enter the course and begin.

How do I unenroll?

  • Steps to unenroll
  • Will I get a refund?
  • Change sessions if you’ve purchased a program bundle

Steps to Unenroll

To unenroll from a course, follow these steps:

  1. Visit your edX personal dashboard*

(If you have an edX subsidy through your employer or academic institution, you may see a popup redirecting you to your enterprise dashboard.Click cancel”)

  1. On the Courses page of the dashboard, find the course that you want to unenroll from.

  2. In the upper right hand corner of the course card you’ll find a Course Actions drop down button. Click this and then select Unenroll, then confirm.

  3. You can optionally give feedback on why you’re unenrolling, or you can select Skip survey.

Note: The Unenroll option will not be available after you’ve earned the verified certificate in the course.

Unenrolling and Refunds

If you unenroll from the verified track of a course within the refund eligibility period, within 14 days after payment or within the first 14 days of the course, whichever comes last*, you are unenrolled from the course and automatically receive a refund for your course payment.

*(Up to 180 days after your payment maximum)

If you unenroll from the verified track of a course AFTER the refund eligibility period has passed, you are unenrolled, but will not receive a refund.

If you have earned a certificate the unenroll option will not be available and we are unable to provide a refund.

Other Options

In courses that were purchased as part of a program bundle, you will have the ability to change course sessions in addition to unenrolling for a refund. For these courses, if the refund eligibility period has passed (meaning that you will not receive a refund), the unenroll option is not available.

If you are a Boot Camp, Bachelor’s, or Executive Education learner, please reach out using the contact information for your specific program, which can be found on your program’s specific About page.

How do self-paced courses work?

If a course is self-paced, that means:

  • All the course content is available as soon as the course opens.
  • You can work at your own pace until the final end date of the course.
  • A suggested schedule helps keep you on track.
  • Upgrade to the verified track to submit graded assessments any time before the end date* and to qualify to receive a certificate.
  • In the paid verified track, the certificate will be awarded as soon as you earn the minimum passing score, but you can continue working up until the end date to improve your score. Note: *The due dates on open response assessments are fixed. The suggested schedule does not apply to open response assessments, but rather the course instructor manually sets these due dates. These due dates cannot be shifted.

Read more:

  • What is self-paced or instructor-paced? Which is my course?
  • How long do I have to finish? When does the course end?
  • How does the suggested schedule work?
  • Does my course have due dates?

What does archived mean?

Many edX courses remain available as archived courses after the course ends. When does my course start and end?

In an archived course you can view most of the course materials, such as lectures and readings for free in the audit track.

However:

  • You cannot complete assignments for a grade (the Progress score won’t update)
  • The course staff will not be active on the forums
  • You cannot upgrade to the verified track or earn a certificate
  • Some assignments or links may no longer work If you enroll in the verified track while a course is active, you’ll still be able to access the course after it is archived.

If enrollment is no longer available on the course enrollment page, there will be a message Future dates to be announced.

What happens if I need to stop taking a course?

There are no academic penalties associated with not finishing a course on edX. If you cannot complete a course, you have several options.

  • If you are enrolled in the audit track of a course, you can simply stop taking the course. There is no need to unenroll.

  • If you paid for the verified track of a course, you can unenroll and get a refund, if you unenroll within the refund eligibility period. After the refund eligibility period has passed, you can still unenroll from the course, but you do not get a refund. If you’ve purchased a program bundle, you may have the option to change course sessions or leave the current session. In these courses, in addition to unenrolling from the course, you have these options:

  • You can change to another available session of the course

  • You can leave your session and wait until a session that works for you becomes available Note: If the refund eligibility period has passed (meaning that you will not receive a refund), the unenroll option is not available. For more information, see How do I choose a course session?

What is self-paced or instructor-paced? Which is my course?

edX courses are either self-paced or instructor-paced. Pacing refers to how course teams run the course, and whether you need to complete course materials on a set schedule.

How do I know whether my course is self-paced or instructor-paced?

What’s the difference?

Self-paced

Instructor-paced

Content availability

Everything available as soon as the course starts

Content may be published periodically

Due dates on assessments

Flexible (read more about the suggested schedule)

Fixed

Certificates available

As soon as you pass

After the course ends

Course duration (read about end dates)

Several months or years

3 - 16 Weeks

Many edX courses are instructor-paced. These courses set a fixed schedule. The course team sets specific due dates for assignments and exams, and you complete the course within a defined time period, such as 8 to 12 weeks. In instructor-paced courses, course materials become available at specific times as the course progresses. In most instructor-paced courses, certificates are generated for learners who have passed the course shortly after the end of the course.

In contrast, self-paced courses do not set a fixed schedule for assignments. All of the course materials are available as soon as the course starts. You can read more about self-paced courses here: How do self-paced courses work?

How can I tell what kind of course this is?

Before you enroll, check the course enrollment page details just below the course’s title. It will read Self paced (Progress at your own speed) for self paced courses or Instructor-paced (Instructor-led on a course schedule) for instructor-paced courses.

What languages are edX courses taught in?

At this time, most edX courses are in English. Some courses are offered in other languages such as Spanish, French, or Chinese, with English subtitles available for the videos.

Before you enroll in a course, you can check the course introduction page under At a glance to confirm the language of the course and its video transcripts.

For example, a course may be offered in Spanish but provide video transcripts available in Spanish or English.

You can also search for a course in your preferred language by using the Language filter in the course catalog.

If you click the Language button on the course catalog search page, a list of language options will be displayed with checkboxes. Select your preferred language from this list and you’ll only be shown courses that are offered in that language.

For translation assistance, we recommend a tool such as Google Translate.

Note: Course language options are determined by the course team responsible for the course. While you can change the language of the edX platform in your account settings, this will not change the language of course content.

What’s the course Enrollment page?

When you browse courses in the edX search catalog and click the course’s thumbnail card, you’ll view the course Enrollment page. On this page you can enroll in the course or view course details.

Enroll in the course

Click the Enroll button to enroll in the course. If you have not signed into edX.org, clicking this button will redirect you to sign in or create an account. Once enrolled, the course will appear on your edX Dashboard.

The start and end date will show above the Enroll button:

If the course is self-paced, the start date will show today’s date.

If more than one session is available, you may choose from any available session:

The end date may not show if the course is not ending any time soon.

If the course is archived, instead of Enroll it will show View course materials.

Details on the Course Enrollment Page

The course Enrollment page provides detailed information about the course including:

  • The estimated length. This is how long the course team estimates it takes a learner to successfully complete the course. It’s not a strict requirement or an indication of when the course ends.
    • If you enroll in the free audit track, you will have access to the course for this length in weeks. You can read more about the verified vs audit enrollment tracks here.
  • Pacing type. You can read more about how courses are paced here.
  • Whether the course offers certificates through an optional upgrade.
  • About - At a glance:
    • Partner institution offering the course
    • Subject
    • Level
    • Prerequisites
    • Language
    • Video Transcript
    • Associated Programs. Read more about our programs.
    • Associated Skills.
  • What you’ll learn. This is a brief description of the course.
  • Syllabus. An outline of the course syllabus.
  • Instructors. Learn more about the instructors who designed the course.
  • Ways to Enroll. Click here to read the Price to upgrade to the verified track to pursue the certificate.
  • The start and end date of all available sessions. Read more: When does my course start and end?

Where can I find my course?

  • Courses you’ve enrolled in on edX.org
  • Boot Camps and Executive Education courses
  • Find new courses to enroll in

Courses you’ve enrolled in on edX.org

If you’ve enrolled in a course on edX.org, you can find it on your edX dashboard. For more information about how to start learning, please read How do I start my course?

If you’ve enrolled in a course on edX.org but you’re not finding it on your dashboard, it’s possible you may have enrolled under a different edX account.

Boot Camps and Executive Education courses

Some edX courses are hosted on our partner sites. Boot Camp and Executive Education courses will not appear on your edX dashboard.

Boot Camps: Please visit your Boot Camp’s direct program page to access your course. You can explore our Boot Camps on edX.org.

Executive Education: Please visit your Executive Education course’s Online Campus to access your course. You can explore Executive Education courses on edX.org.

Find new courses to enroll in

Read more on how to find the right course for you: Can you help me choose a course?

Why is enrollment listed as “Not Currently Available” for a course? Can I still get access?

We keep as many courses as we can open for registration so that students can audit the material after the course is finished. Unfortunately, we can’t do this for all courses and any which show “Not currently available” instead of an enroll button are closed and cannot be accessed. The course may be opened again in the future.

If you’d like to only browse currently available and actively maintained courses, you can toggle the Availability filter in our course catalog search and select Available now.

Enterprise Learners

Enterprise Data Sharing Consent questions

Enterprise Data Sharing Consent questions

If you come to edX through your employer, school or as part of a bundled offering through a company that offers learner support services, edX may ask your permission to share data about your learning achievements with your employer, school or bundled learning support provider. This typically occurs when your employer, school or another company sponsors your enrollment in edX courses or programs.

By consenting to share your data, you allow edX to share information about your learning progress, such as when you enroll or complete, whether you finish specific parts of the course, the time you spend in edX courses, your final grade, or whether you earn a certificate. Consenting to share data with your course or program sponsor enables you to redeem a company, school (or other) benefit and enroll in the course through your sponsor’s gateway. If you do not consent, then you will not be able participate in the course or program through your employer, school or bundled learning support provider.

More about the consent process

  • Your permission applies only to data from sponsored courses or programs and not to data from any edX courses or programs that you take on your own.
  • Once you grant your permission to allow data to be shared with your employer, school or bundled learning support provider, you may withdraw your permission by fully un-enrolling from any courses or programs that are sponsored by that entity.
  • If you decline to consent, that fact may be shared with your course or program sponsor.

What to do after you decline to consent

If you decline to consent, you can restart the enrollment process at any time, with the present course or program or with another course or program offered through your employer, school or bundled learning support provider.

If you have concerns about sharing your data, please contact your learning manager or administrator.

If you can’t find the answer to your question, or if you have general comments or feedback, you can contact the edX Learner Support team by clicking Submit a Request at the end of this page. If you are signed in to edX.org you may also click the Support tab on the left side of any page.

How do I redeem an enrollment code?

There are two methods of redeeming an enrollment code:

Enter a Coupon Code Outside of the Learner Portal (on edx.org)

  • Browse or search for the course you want to take.
  • Select Enroll Now on the course enrollment page. A message displays confirming your enrollment.
  • Select Pursue a Verified Certificate. A payment page appears.
  • In the coupon box, enter the enrollment code and select Apply.
  • Your cart will show $0 and you can now proceed to the course.

Enroll using a Code Inside of the Learner Portal (on enterprise.edx.org)

  • Browse or search for the course you want to take.
  • Select Enroll Now on the course enrollment page. A message displays confirming your enrollment and asks if you would like to use your code.
  • Choose to Enroll Now.

Subscription 404 Error

If your company or organization has purchased an edX subscription (only available through Enterprise currently) and you see a 404 message when clicking the acceptance link, this likely is one of two situations:

  • You have not yet registered for an account on edX.org. Registration needs to be completed separately from the subscription invitation. You must register using the email address you were invited with at authn.edx.org
  • You are signed into a different edX account. You must be signed in with the account connected to the email address where the invite was sent.
    • Log out and log in to the correct account
    • If you have an existing edX account under a different email address, you can change the registered email on the account to match that on the invite
    • If you are logged into the correct account and still seeing the 404 error, try clearing your browser cache and cookies, close and reopen the browser, then log in again, or try an incognito window.
    • If you continue to have difficulty, contact us using the Help link in the lower right!

Financial Assistance

Can I transfer my financial assistance to a new course?

Financial assistance is granted on a per-course basis. Once granted, it cannot be transferred to another course.

If you would like to apply for financial assistance for another course, enroll in the audit track for that course, and then complete the financial assistance application again.

Do I need to apply for assistance for each course?

Yes, financial assistance is granted on a per-course basis. You will need to complete a new application for each course.

How do I apply for Financial Assistance in a program?

To receive financial assistance for a program, you will need to enroll in each program course separately. On the program enrollment page scroll down to Courses in this program. Click on the title of one of the courses listed to expand it. Click View the course to view the course enrollment page, where you can Enroll in the free audit track.

Once you have enrolled in the audit track in one of your program’s courses, complete the financial assistance application for that course. You’ll need to repeat this for each course in the program.

Financial Assistance coupons can only be applied to upgrade single courses.

How do I apply for Financial Assistance?

How to apply for Financial Assistance

  • Enroll in your desired course in the free audit track
  • Complete the Financial Assistance application
  • Allow up to a week for a decision via email

About Financial Assistance

edX offers a discount on our verified certificates to learners who cannot afford to pay full price. Assistance is available in most courses that offer verified certificates, however, a few courses and programs are not eligible.

You may be approved for Financial Assistance up to 5 times in a 12-month period.

We encourage you to review the edX Honor Code.

Note: To view the application you must have confirmed your email address. If you have not doen so and can’t find the email in your inbox you may request a password reset instead which will send an email to your registered addres.

Upon clicking the link in the password reset email your account will be confirmed.

FAQs

  • How do I enroll to apply for Financial Assistance?
  • How do I complete the Financial Assistance application?
  • Why don’t I see my course in the drop down to apply for Financial Assistance?
  • Which courses are eligible for Financial Assistance?
  • Can I receive a 100% discount?

How do I complete the Financial Assistance application?

You can complete the application on the edX.org Financial Assistance Application.

  1. First, make sure that you’ve enrolled in your desired course, or a course from your desired program. How do I enroll to apply for Financial Assistance?

  2. Select your course from the Course dropdown. You can only apply for one course at a time. If you do not see your course in the dropdown, it may not be eligible for Financial Assistance.

  3. In order to submit the application, you must check off I certify that:

  • Paying the verified certificate fee for the above course would cause me economic hardship
  • I will work diligently to complete the course work and receive a certificate
  • I have read, understand, and will abide by the Honor Code for the edX site

How long do I have to wait for my Financial Assistance decision?

After you submit the Financial Assistance application, a decision can take up to 4 weeks. We recommend keeping the upgrade deadline in mind when you apply.

If your application is accepted, you will receive an email with a coupon code for a discount. You can then apply the code on the payment form for the discounted rate. You can read instructions on how to apply your coupon code in this article.

If your application is not accepted, you will receive an email response with the reason why.

What courses offer edX financial assistance?

Most courses that offer verified certificates award Financial Assistance for learners who cannot afford the verified certificate fee. If a course is not eligible it may not appear as an option on the form after you have enrolled.

Most Programs offer Financial Assistance

You can read more on how to apply for a program: How do I apply for Financial Assistance in a program?

Ineligible Courses and Programs

It’s not possible to apply for assistance through the edX Financial Assistance application for:

  • Professional Education courses
  • Executive Education
  • Boot Camps
  • any course unavailable in the free audit track
  • any courses or programs from our partner DoaneX
  • any courses from our partner TEFLx
  • TUMx Lean Six Sigma Green Belt Certification: Business Project Professional Certificate program courses
  • select MicroMasters™ program courses (most are eligible)
    • The MITx MicroMasters in Finance. This program does not participate with edX Financial Assistance but offers their own separate financial assistance program.
    • The final capstone course of the CurtinX MicroMasters in Internet of Things (IoT), IOT6x: IoT Capstone Project does not qualify but the first five courses of the program do qualify.

What is the deadline to apply for financial assistance?

You are welcome to apply for financial assistance before the deadline to upgrade to a verified certificate. This deadline varies by course. If the upgrade deadline has not passed, you can find the upgrade deadline on the right side of the course homepage under Important Dates.

Please be sure to apply in advance, as a decision can take up to 4 weeks.

Why don’t I see my course in the drop down when I apply for Financial Assistance?

If you’ve enrolled in a course in the free audit track, but the course does not appear in the course dropdown in the financial assistance application, this means either:

  • the upgrade deadline has passed or
  • this course is not eligible for financial assistance If the upgrade deadline has passed, you can check for a new session of the course in the course catalog.

I want to apply for Financial Assistance in a program

If you need Financial Assistance for a program, please read: How do I apply for Financial Assistance in a program?

General

Do I have to take all of the courses in a program at once?

You do not need to take all of the courses in a program at once. You can take the courses in a MicroMasters, MicroBachelors, Professional Certificate, or XSeries program one at a time as they are offered.

You can read about your options for purchasing a program.

All courses which are part of a program credential are expected to repeat each year. So, although sometimes all of a program’s courses run simultaneously, you don’t have to enroll in them all at the same time. You have the option to enroll in just one course and wait for the next sessions before you enroll in the next course.

Most courses only show the currently available session, but some courses show the available sessions over the next year or so, so that you can choose the session dates that work best for you.

If you’ve purchased a program bundle, please see more information on selecting sessions here: How do I choose a course session?

Error when selecting session

If you have purchased a program and receive an error when attempting to join a session: “There was an error. Please reload the page and try again,” this is usually because you have not yet completed account confirmation.

Check your email account for a message titled Action Required: Confirm your email and click the link to confirm.

If you cannot find this email, reset your password from your Account Settings or the Log In page. Clicking the reset link in your email will also confirm your account.

How do I enroll in a program?

You join a program on edX.org simply by enrolling in any course that is part of that program – there’s no application.

Program credentials offered on edX.org

You can browse all of our available programs in the course catalog. For more information about the catalog search, please read Can you help me choose a course?

After you enroll in a course that is part of a program credential, that program on your Programs dashboard here.

Click the name of any program for more details and the option to Enroll Now in any related courses, or upgrade all remaining courses.

To complete a program you must earn a verified certificate in each of the individual courses. After you earn a verified certificate in each course, you automatically receive your program certificate.

For additional information, see How do I take all the courses in a program?

MIT’s MicroMasters Program in Data, Economics, and Development Policy (DEDP) Moved

The courses in MIT’s Data, Economics, and Development Policy (DEDP) program have been moved off edX.org to MITx. To access the courses now, please follow these steps:

  • Visit the new MITx Online site at mitxonline.mit.edu and create an account.
  • Check your inbox for a confirmation email and finish your profile.
  • Click on any course to see more details.
  • Click the red “Enroll Now” button to enroll as an auditor.
  • If you want to earn a certificate, you must complete your payment on the MicroMasters program site here: micromasters.mit.edu. Once you have created our MITx Online account, you may login here to link your account and complete payment. For questions, please reach out to micromasters-support@mit.edu.

Options for purchasing a program

You have two options for purchasing one of our edX-issued program credentials: as a bundled purchase, or upgrading each course one by one.

- Option 1: Purchase the program bundle
- Option 2: Upgrade course by course
- [Can I purchase the program for someone else?](https://support.edx.org/hc/en-us/articles/4418234822807)
- Receiving the program certificate
- Do I have to complete all the available courses right now?

Whether you purchase the program as a bundle or upgrade its courses one by one, upon completion you’ll earn the same program credential.

Option 1: Purchase the program bundle

Most programs offer a one-click bundle purchase to add all the program courses at once. If this is available, you can scroll down and click the Pursue the Program button on the program introduction page.

  • Lets you purchase all the courses at once for the discount indicated on the program page
  • Lets you select the course session that best fits your schedule Please read How to select course sessions for detailed information on selecting course sessions and completing a program with this option.

Please note that enrollment coupon codes and Financial Assistance coupon codes cannot be applied to program bundle purchases.

If this Pursue the Program button is not available on a program’s page, then bundling is not an option for that particular program.

Option 2: Upgrade Course by Course

You can also purchase the program courses one by one.

To do this from the program introduction page, scroll down to Courses in this Program. Click on the title of one of the courses listed to expand it. Click View the course to view the course introduction page. There, you can click the Enroll button to enroll in that course.

You can Upgrade to the verified track at the time you enroll in the course, or you can start for free and upgrade to the verified track later so long as the upgrade deadline for the course has not passed.

Receiving the program credential

After you’ve earned a verified certificate from each course in the program, you’ll automatically receive the program credential at no further cost. (Where can I find my program certificate?)

Whether you purchase the program as a bundle or upgrade its courses one by one, upon completion you’ll earn the same program credential.

Do I have to complete all the available courses right now?

You do not need to complete all of the courses at once in order to earn the program credential. The courses will be re-offered on an ongoing basis, and all certificates count towards the program credential.

What does a program certificate look like?

Below is an example image of a program certificate.

Program certificates include the following information:

  • The full name you entered for your edX account.
  • The name of the program.
  • The edX partner institution that offered the program.
  • The names and signatures of one or more members of the program team.
  • The month and year that the certificate was issued.
  • A certificate ID (this is not a link)

Read More

  • Where can I find my program certificate?
  • Viewing and sharing your program record
  • What’s a program? How is a program different from a course?

What’s a program? How is a program different from a course?

On edX you have the option to enroll in single courses, or you can pursue multiple courses that are part of a series. After earning verified certificates in all of the courses in a series, you’ll also automatically earn the program credential.

When you complete a course that belongs to a program, it’ll display on your program record. Standalone courses that don’t belong to any program credential don’t appear on your program record. How do I know if my course belongs to a program?

Standalone courses on edX.org

Standalone courses are individual courses that aren’t part of any series. These courses will cover one topic in depth, and you can earn a verified certificate of achievement in the course.

Program credentials issued on edX.org

XSeries Program

The XSeries Program is usually a group of 3 or more courses that explore a certain skill, topic, or field. These programs are typically offered by our university partners.

Professional Certificate Program

The Professional Certificate Program is a corporate-sponsored program that focuses on career development in areas such as programming, web development, data science, etc. The programs are usually groups 3 or more courses that become more advanced as you progress.

MicroBachelors Program

The MicroBachelors programs are a series of 3 to 5 courses with content designed to help you be job ready. Additionally, after completing these courses you can elect to earn undergraduate credit through our credit partner institutions.

MicroMasters Program

The MicroMasters Program is a series of 3 or more graduate level courses offered by major universities and are sponsored by well-known companies in the field. Some of the MicroMasters programs may also offer transferrable course credit if you do choose to continue into an on-campus Master’s Degree program.

Online Master’s Degrees

We also now offer fully online Masters programs from top rated universities. Instead of certificates awarded on edX, you’ll earn a diploma that’s identical to the diploma awarded to on campus students.

Boot Camps and Executive Education

Boot Camps and Executive Education courses are hosted on our partner sites. Credentials for these won’t be issued through edX.org.

What’s the Program Enrollment page?

edX offers several different types of programs. When you browse programs in our course catalog and click on a program tile, you’ll navigate to the program enrollment page.

The program introduction page provides detailed information about one of our program credentials including:

  • The pacing type of the program’s courses
  • The estimated length. This isn’t a strict requirement or an indication of course end dates, but rather it’s an estimation made by the offering partner of how long it takes to succeed in the program.
  • The price: The discounted price for purchasing the program bundle displays first, then the next displayed price with the strike-through is the price to upgrade the courses one by one.
  • Courses in this program.
    • Expand any of these courses listed to view more details, including dates and prerequisites, on the course introduction page by clicking the link View the course. For Professional Certificate programs: Click Job Outlook to read more details about the job outlook from the relevant industry.

For MicroMasters programs: Click Certificate & Credit Pathways to learn more about leveraging the MicroMasters into a full Master’s.

Getting Started

Can anyone use edX?

edX courses are open to all worldwide, except where prohibited by government regulations. To comply with international law edX.org is not intended for children under the age of 13.

Most edX courses are considered undergraduate college or masters level.

How can I get help with my course?

The best way to get help is in the Discussion forum within your course. You have a global community of classmates that you can engage with, as well as support from the course staff who monitor the forum. Take a look at the course’s discussion forum to review questions, answers, and comments from fellow students, as well as post a question.

To access the forum, select Discussion at the top of your course:

Note: We are not able to provide direct contact information for professors, teaching assistants (TAs), or other course staff.

Is there a walk-through of a sample course?

edX offers a demonstration course that is designed to show learners who are new to edx.org how to take a course.

Enroll in the DemoX course and try it out! You will learn how to navigate the edX platform and complete your first course.

Hello, and welcome to edX!

Register for an Account

If you haven’t already, please register for an edX.org account. You’ll need to fill in the information required (email address, username, your full name, country), and then confirm your account via the email sent to the email address you entered.

  • Didn’t receive a confirmation email? Follow these instructions!

Take a look at our course catalog to find the right course for you. We offer courses of many different subjects from a growing list of schools around the world! Courses you enroll in can be found on your edX dashboard.

  • Feeling overwhelmed? Use the drop-down options on the top of the page to find exactly what you’re looking for.
Enroll in a Course

When you find a course, click on it to be taken to the course registration page. Here you can find information on the course. Click on the “Enroll Now” button, then choose to go on the verified or audit track.

  • Please keep in mind that almost all edX courses can be taken for free! You can upgrade to earn a verified certificate while auditing a course. Please refer to the “Verification Upgrade Deadline” on the right side of the course outline page to know how long you have to upgrade.

Enroll in DemoX! This mock course serves as a great way to familiarize yourself with the edX platform before taking on the real thing.

Pursue a Certificate

Please explore the rest of our Help Center to help answer any other questions you may have.

What are the system requirements and supported browsers on edX?

Most current browsers will work on edX.org. For best performance, we recommend the latest versions of:

  • Chrome (version 100 or later)

  • Firefox We also support the latest versions of:

  • Microsoft Edge

  • Safari (version 15 or later)

  • Chrome for Android

  • Safari for iOS

Troubleshooting browser problems

Many problems with missing form fields, buttons that do not function, or other page display issues are caused by your browser. Here are some things to try to fix viewing problems:

  • Make sure your browser is the most current version.
  • Check your browser settings to make sure both JavaScript and cookies are enabled.
  • Clear your browser’s cache. Here are instructions on clearing your cache in Chrome or Firefox.

edX app support

The edX app is free and available for smartphones and tablets on both Android and iOS platforms.

While smartphones and tablets can be used to access and take courses, some more advanced problem types and content features may not function properly.

Note: If your course includes timed exams or proctored exams, you cannot take them using the edX app.

What does it cost to take a course?

Except for Professional Education, Boot Camps, and Executive Education courses, there is no cost for taking edX open courses when you enroll in the free audit track, which does not offer certificates or provide graded assignments.

However, if you want to be able to earn a certificate for a course and complete assignments, there is a fee that will vary depending on the course. The fees for the verified track usually range between $50 USD and $300 USD. You can confirm the price of an edX open course if you click Ways to Enroll on the course enrollment page.

Please note that some courses (such as Boot Camps, Professional / Executive Education, and Bachelors / Masters) may have a higher price.

The fees for upgrading to the verified track or enrolling in a Professional Education, Boot Camp, or Executive Education course are the only charges on edX. There are no other fees associated with edX courses.

You can find more information about the different certificates offered by edX: What types of certificates does edX offer?

edX has a financial assistance program for a discounted fee to help learners who demonstrate financial need to earn verified certificates.

What if I have a technical problem viewing a page on edX.org?

First, check our status page to be sure there is not an outage or larger technical issue.

Many problems such as missing fields, buttons that do not function, server error messages or other page display issues are caused by your browser or browser extensions. Here are some things to try:

  • Disable ad-blockers or any other browser extensions. You can try browsing in a private window to do this:
    • Instructions for private browsing on Firefox
    • Instructions for private browsing on Chrome
  • Make sure your browser is a current version of Chrome or Firefox.
  • Check your browser settings to make sure both JavaScript and cookies are enabled.
  • Clear your browser’s cache. Here are instructions on clearing your cache in Chrome or Firefox.
  • Check our system requirements to ensure you meet our browser, computer, and mobile requirements.

What is edX?

edX is a mission-driven, massive open online course (MOOC) provider. We partner with the world’s leading universities and organizations to offer high-quality online courses to learners across the world.

To explore our 3,500+ courses, please visit our course catalog. Courses consist of video and text content, discussion forums, and a number of problem and assessment types. Click on the course you are interested in to see more details and to enroll. You can read more here: Can you help me choose a course?

Learn more on our Welcome to edX page.

The majority of edX courses are entirely free to access and most offer an optional paid verified certificate track with graded assignments and the opportunity to work towards a certificate for a fee that varies per course. The Verified track awards a certificate after you successfully pass the course. Verified certificates are a great way to showcase the skills and knowledge you’ve gained, and can help advance your professional or academic career.

What is the edX Honor Code?

Verified track learners will be asked to agree to the edX Honor Code when accessing the first graded assignment of their course.

The edX Honor Code, together with edX’s Terms of Service and Privacy Policy, are agreements between you and edX. By using the edx.org site, you are agreeing to be legally bound by these agreements, whether or not you are a registered user.

Put simply, the edX Honor Code states that, by enrolling in an edX course or program, you agree that you will:

  • Complete all tests and assignments on your own, unless collaboration on an assignment is explicitly permitted.
  • Maintain only one user account, unless edX has approved the use of a separate user account in connection with an employer-sponsored or university-sponsored program.
  • Not let anyone else use your username and/or password.
  • Not engage in any activity that would dishonestly improve your results, or improve or hurt the results of others.
  • Not post answers to problems that are being used to assess learner performance. You’ll find the entire edX honor code here. Each course might also have specific agreements for learner collaboration and discussion.

Where are edX certificates recognized?

We encourage you to learn more about how our learners have benefited from edX certificates in our Learner Stories blog.

Students worldwide have shared verified certificates on resumes or CVs, in applications for work or school, and on sites such as LinkedIn. Upgrading to the verified track is also a great way to give yourself an incentive to complete the course and celebrate your success.

edX is a mission-driven organization and your purchase goes toward improving edX and increasing access to high-quality education for others worldwide.
