How to take a screenshot

Screenshots may be helpful when communicating with edX support about issues you are having, or when sharing your progress with employers, schools or others.

You can take a screenshot on all computers and devices. Here are instructions on how to take a screenshot on the most common systems:

PC

To capture just the active window, press Alt+PrtScn. (Some machines may require you to tap Fn at the same time.)

Mac

How to capture a portion of the screen

  • Press and hold these three keys together: Shift, Command, and 4.
  • Drag the crosshair to select the area of the screen to capture.
  • To take the screenshot, release your mouse or trackpad button.

Linux

Press ⇧ Shift+PrtScn to select what you capture. You’ll be able to click and drag a selection box to determine what is captured in the screenshot. A file with the image you captured will be created in your Pictures folder.

iOS (iPhone/iPad)

Press both the sleep/wake button and the Home button simultaneously. Once your iOS device recognizes the screenshot command, the screen will briefly flash white

Android

  • Press the Power button and Volume down key at the same time.
  • Hold them down until you hear an audible click or a screenshot sound

What if my question isn’t answered here?

If you can’t find the answer to your question in our Help Center, you can contact the edX Learner Support team through our Contact Us form or click the Need Help button in the lower right hand corner of this page.

There’s a link to the Contact Us form on the bottom of any page on the main edx.org site where you browse courses.

If you have a question about an assignment in a course, or want to ask your instructor a question, post in the course discussion forum.

Why can’t I play the videos in my course?

The videos are stalling or freezing

If the video is available but playback is slowing or stalling, we recommend trying again later or taking these steps to ensure your browser is not preventing the video from playing. The stalling may be temporary.

If your internet bandwidth cannot stream the video smoothly, you can download the video or its video transcript to review on your computer.

Let us know about it

If the problem with video playback stalling persists after some time has passed, you can report this to edX Support with details about the browser and operating system you’re using.

Optional: Additional details that are helpful in identifying video issues include a screenshot of the Javascript console from your browser. You can find instructions on taking a screenshot in this article.

  • Here are steps to view the Javascript console in Firefox.
  • Here are steps to view the Javascript console in Chrome.

Download the video or video transcript

If the video is available for download, below the video player there will be a link Download video file. If the video’s transcripts are available for download, below the video player there will be links Download SubRip (.srt) file or Download Text (.txt) file.

If any of these download links are available, you can click them to download the file to your computer. Then you can watch the video or read the video’s transcript on your computer.

Learner records

Viewing and sharing your program record

After you have earned a certificate for a course which is part of an edX program, you can view your certificates on the My Learner Records page. (How do I know if my course belongs to a program?)

The My Learner Records page displays your course completion status for any courses that are part of a program, such as a MicroMasters. On the My Learner Records page, you can choose to send your program record to an institution that accepts courses in that program as a step towards admission or completion of a degree program. You can also share your program record as a link to your program record page.

To view your program record

  • At the top of any page, select the dropdown menu icon next to your username, then select Profile.
  • On the Profile page, select View My Records.
  • On the My Learner Records page, find the program record that you want to share and select View Program Record.
  • You can also navigate directly to records.edx.org.

To send your program record

  • On the My Learner Records page, find the program record that you want to share and select View Program Record.
  • On the Record page for the program, select Copy program record link. For detailed information about who can see your program record and what they can see, see When I share my program record, what information is shared?

After you send a link to your program record to someone, they can view your record for that program. Once a person has the link to your program record, they can view it at any time, and you cannot revoke their access to your program record.

When I share my program record, what information is shared?

When you share your program record, the institution you share it with receives the following information about your performance in each of the program’s courses.

  • Your name, edX user ID, and email address.
  • The course name and course ID.
  • The name and type of the program that the course is a part of.
  • The institution that offered the program.
  • The number of times you started the course as a verified learner.
  • The date that a certificate was awarded to you for the course.
  • Your percent grade for the course. If you started the course more than once, this will be the highest earned grade that you earned.
  • Your letter grade for the course. If you started the course more than once, this will be the highest earned grade that you earned. You can view and share your program record on the My Learner Records page. You can find a link to your learner records on your edX profile page or by navigating directly to this link: credentials.edx.org

You can view your edX profile page by clicking your username in the upper right and selecting Profile. Click View My Records to view your program records.

Note: After you have shared your program record, you cannot retract the information that you shared.

Manage Your Account Settings

Changing your local time zone

In your account settings, you can set your local time zone so that course dates and times are displayed in your local time.

You can access your Account Settings by selecting the drop down in the upper right hand corner of any page and then Account. Then select Site Preferences, where you can click Set time zone to change your time zone.

If you do not select a time zone, the following dates and times are shown in your web browser’s time zone:

  • Course dates and times in the body of courses you are enrolled in
  • Dates and times on the Progress page in your courses
  • Course dates and times on your Dashboard page If you do not set a time zone, some dates and times elsewhere on edx.org might use Coordinated Universal Time (UTC). To convert UTC to your local time, check World Time Server or other Internet time services.

Time zone indicators

Dates and times that use your local time zone or the browser time zone are in military (24-hour) time and are followed by a time zone indicator. For example, 17:00 Sept 16, 2018 IOT.

Dates and times in UTC do not have a time zone indicator.

How can I change the language?

You can change the language for the edX site to any of the supported languages on the Account Settings page.

Additionally the new Universal Translation feature lets you set your preferred language for the site and video transcripts all in one place.

Look for the Site Language Button (globe icon) on the right of the Course Header at the top of the page:

  • Clicking the globe button will open a language selection window.

  • Select your preferred site language from the list of currently supported site languages and click submit. Once you have selected a site language, you will then see a Unit Translation Button within the course content:

  • The unit translation will automatically default to your site language (the one you set in the header).

  • You can change the translation back to the original course language at any time by clicking this button. If the course content is not available in your selected site language, the button will show a “not available” message and default the unit back to the original course language.

How do I access my personal data?

The information collected from users and visitors to edx.org is described in the Privacy Policy (under the heading “Information That edX Collects From and About You (including Personal Information)”). edX requires personal information only in limited circumstances as described in the Privacy Policy.

Much of the information that edX stores about you is available via your edX account. This includes your profile information, account settings, and your dashboard. The dashboard lists all current and archived courses in which you are enrolled and includes links to any course and program certificates you may have earned. Through the dashboard, you can also access the course content and view your work and performance on assessments and your posts in public forum discussions. If you need assistance locating any of the information available via your edX account, please contact edX support using the Help button in the lower right.

As explained in the edX Cookie Policy, cookies and other similar tracking technologies are also used to track information about your activity on the edX website (for example, which pages were visited). Please read the “Types of Cookies Used by edX” section of the Cookie Policy for more information on the different types of cookies used and their purpose(s).

How do I add a recovery email address?

You can add a recovery email address in your edX Account Settings under Account Information. This provides an additional location to receive a password reset in case you lose access to the email address originally connected to your account.

Next to Recovery email address, click Edit. Then enter your recovery email and Save.

The recovery address can be any email that’s not connected to an existing or previously deleted edX account.

We’ll send you a confirmation email, ‘Confirm your recovery email for edX.’ Click Confirm Email to update your recovery email address.

If you don’t receive the confirmation email:

  • Check the spam folder at that email
  • Make sure you have not previously used this email on edX.org. It’s not possible to change an account email address to use an email connected to another edX account, or a previously deleted account. If you entered an email address that is already in use, the confirmation will not be sent.
  • Try adding no-reply@registration.edx.org to your contacts or your approved senders lists, go back to the Account Settings and add the address again to resend the confirmation

How do I change my account settings?

To change or view your edX account details click the arrow next to your username in the top corner and choose Account.

In your edX account settings you can update:

  • Your full name

  • Your email address

  • Your password

  • The language used on the edX site Note: This does not change the language that course content is presented in.

  • The country or region where you live

  • Your local time zone

  • The social accounts linked on your profile

  • Other profile information Here is a direct link to your Account Settings.

How do I change my email address?

To change your email address connected to edX

  • At the top of any page, select the dropdown menu icon next to your username.
  • Select Account.
  • In the Email Address field under Account Information, select your current email address and replace it with the new address.
  • To save your changes, click Save. The message “We’ve sent a confirmation message to ‘email address’”.
  • If it’s successful, you’ll receive an email from no-reply@registration.edx.org Email Change Confirmation for edX. Click the link in the email to complete the change.

Why didn’t I receive the confirmation email?

  • It’s possible that you mis-typed the desired email address. Double check the steps again.
  • If this email address is already in use on another edX account, you won’t receive the confirmation email. This includes:
    • If the email is the recovery email address on another account
    • If the email was associated to a previously deleted account

How do I change my name on my account?

To change the full name in your edX account:

  • At the top of any page, select the dropdown menu icon next to your username.
  • Select Account. You can also navigate directly here: courses.edx.org
  • Next to the Full Name field click Edit, then make the changes you want to your full name.
  • When done, click save ​​​

Note: You can change your full name, but you cannot change your public username.

  • Upon updating your Full Name, you may see the following message and be asked to verify your identity:
  • Please click ‘Continue’ to move through the edX ID verification process. You can learn more about that process here.
  • Once your ID verification photos are approved, your name change will be reflected on your Account page.

How do I link or unlink my edX account to a social media account?

To log in to edX using a social media account, you first need to link that account in your edX Account Settings.

To link your social media account to your edX account

  • After logging in to edX, select the dropdown menu next to your username, then select Account.
  • On the Account Settings page, click Linked Accounts on the left hand menu or scroll down to Linked Accounts.
  • Select Sign in with Apple to link your Apple ID, Sign in with Facebook to link your Facebook account, Sign in with Google to link your Google account, or Sign in with Microsoft to link your Microsoft account. Microsoft linking is only available for Microsoft 365 accounts. Note: After you link your accounts, whenever you sign in to your social media or other account, you also automatically sign in to your edX account.

To unlink your social media account from your edX account

To unlink your edX account from a social media account, select Unlink below that account name.

If there’s an error message, try logging out of edX and logging back in again. If you check your Account Settings page after logging out and back in, it should show unlinked for you. If not, please contact Support.

How do I unsubscribe from email?

edX sends two different kinds of emails:

  • Promotional messages about edX.org offerings. Anyone who agrees to marketing messages when they create their account may receive these. Read more about how to unsubscribe from marketing messages.
  • Course updates sent by the partner team running a course. You’ll only receive these messages if you’re enrolled in a course that’s sending out updates. Read more about how to unsubscribe from course updates.

Steps to unsubscribe from edX promotional emails

You can unsubscribe from edX marketing emails by clicking the Unsubscribe button at the bottom of the message.

With some email providers like Gmail, these options might not be visible in the initial message view. Instead, there might be a “[Message clipped] View entire message” link at the bottom of the page. Select this link to view the entire message, including the unsubscribe option.

You’ll know you’ve successfully unsubscribed if a new tab opens with a confirmation message, “You’ve successfully unsubscribed from our emails.’

Steps to unsubscribe from course updates

To unsubscribe from course update email messages, follow these steps:

  1. Visit your edX personal dashboard*

(If you have an edX subsidy through your employer or academic institution, you may see a popup redirecting you to your enterprise dashboard. Click cancel”)

  1. On the Courses page of the dashboard, find the course that’s sending unwanted messages.

  2. Select the Settings ‘gear’ icon next to the View Course or View Archived Course button. For screen readers, select Course options for the course that’s sending unwanted messages.

  3. Select Email Settings. For screen readers, from Available Actions select Email Settings.

  4. This will open a modal for the email settings for the course. Unselect Receive course emails and then select Save Settings.

​

Information Deletion Requests for non-registered users

If you do not have an edX account, but provided edX with contact details for information on our courses, programs, news, and related products and services OR specific courses or programs and wish to have your information deleted:

To start the deletion process, please send an email to info@edx.org from the same email address you provided to receive such information.

If you have registered for an edX account see How can I delete my account?

Why can’t I unlink my edX account from a social media account?

If you’re trying to unlink your social media account from your Account Settings page, and you encounter an error message:

There was a problem disconnecting this account. Contact support if the problem persists.

Try logging out of your edX.org account and signing back in. Then the account should show unlinked on your Account Settings page.

Participating in Course Discussions

Adding responses or comments in a discussion

To participate in an ongoing course discussion, you can reply to an existing post by adding a response, or expand on a response by adding a comment.

The same options for formatting the text and for adding links or images are available for responses and comments as for posts. For more information, see Adding posts in a discussion.

Add a response or comment on the discussion page

You can add a response or comment to course-wide or content-specific discussion topics on the Discussion page.

On the Discussion page, find the post that you want to contribute to. To help you decide where to add your thoughts, review the current responses and their comments.

  • To add a response to the post, select Add A Response below the post. When you have finished entering your response, select Submit.
  • To add a comment to a response, select the Add a comment button below the response. When you have finished entering your comment, select Submit.

Answering questions in discussion posts

Anyone in a course can answer questions that are posted in the course discussions. To respond to a question that has been asked by someone else in the course, add a response with your answer to the question post.

If you have received a helpful or correct response to a question that you yourself have asked, you can like the response.

Editing or deleting posts, responses, or comments

You can edit or delete your own posts, responses, or comments in the discussion forum.

To edit or delete a post, response, or comment

  • Locate the contribution that you want to edit or delete, either in the body of the course or on the Discussion page.
  • In the upper corner of the contribution, select the “More” icon (…). A menu opens.
  • Edit or delete the contribution.
    • To edit the contribution, select Edit, make the changes that you want in the text editor that opens, and then select Submit. For posts, you can change the post topic and the post type as well as the text of the post.
    • To delete the contribution, select Delete, and then select Delete in the confirmation box. If you select Cancel the post won’t be deleted.

Flagging inappropriate discussion posts

Report Discussion Misuse

On rare occasions you might find inappropriate contributions in the forum. You can flag any post, response, or comment for a discussion moderator to review. To do so, view the contribution select the ellipsis icon More, and then select Report.

The discussion moderation team can investigate any flagged posts, responses, or comments and take the appropriate action.

How can I find my own discussion posts?

To find posts that you added to course discussions, select the My Posts tab.

You can filter or search your own posts: Searching, sorting, and filtering discussions

How do I add a post in the discussion forum?

You can access your course’s discussion forum from the ‘Discussion’ tab in the top menu within your course on edX.org.

Then click the Add a Post button.

If there’s no Add a Post button, the discussion forum may be closed. Some instructor-paced courses may temporarily close the forums during an exam period. We also recommend checking if the course has ended.

When you add a new post in a course discussion, you choose:

  • The type of post: question or discussion
  • The topic of the post Note: We don’t recommend posting personal, private information such as your phone number or address in the forum.

Deciding on the type of your post

Decide whether you want to ask a question or start a discussion.

  • Question posts raise an issue so that course team members, TAs, or other learners can provide answers.
  • Discussion posts start a conversation by sharing thoughts and reflections, and invite community participation. Think about whether you want to raise an issue or question that needs an answer, or start an open-ended conversation.

If you’re asking a question about the course and need an answer from the course team, be sure to create your post as a question, so that the course team sees that a response is required and responds appropriately.

Deciding on the topic for your post

Every post in a course discussion must be associated with one of the topics that the course team has created. You can leave the default topic area or select from the dropdown list of Topic Areas.

Adding Images to your Post

To add an image to your post, click the Insert/edit image icon in the text editor.

Then a popup modal will allow you to select the image.

  • To insert an image hosted on the web by its URL, paste the URL in the Source field.
  • To upload an image from your device, select Upload. Then you can drag an image there or browse your device for the desired image.
Be Inclusive

If you add an image to your post, in order to save it you’ll be required to include a description or select Image is decorative. This allows your peers who use screen readers to read a description of the image.

Once you have selected the photo and completed required fields, click Save to add the photo to your post.

The maximum size for an uploaded file is 1 MB.

Anonymous Posting

In some courses, you can add posts, responses, and comments anonymously. If this is the case, a Post Anonymously option is available under the field where you enter your text. When you post anonymously, the discussion moderation team can see your username, but other learners cannot.

It’s up to the course team to allow anonymous posting in their discussion forums or not.

Math Expressions

For tips on entering mathematical expressions in the forums, refer to Adding Math Expressions to Course Discussion Posts in the DemoX course wiki.

(You’ll need to be registered with edX.org and enroll in the DemoX course.)

How do I follow discussion forum posts?

Follow posts

To follow posts in your course discussions, select the post from the left hand menu, then click the star icon below the post, the Follow button.

The star icon fills black to indicate you are now following this post. When you view this post in the left hand panel, the star also shows as filled black. For more information about the various indicators in the navigation pane, review How to join course discussions.

Filter discussion posts

In the discussion All Posts tab, you can lists only the posts that you are following, regardless of the topic that they apply to. Click the filter icon across from All posts by recent activity.

How do I like a discussion post?

To provide positive feedback for a post or one of its responses, you can like it. Open the post or response, and click the Like button (thumbs-up).

To remove your vote from a post, click the Like button again.

You can like your own posts. You can also filter posts by Most Likes: Searching, sorting, and filtering discussions

How to join course discussions

Course discussions give you the opportunity to start conversations with other learners, ask questions that other learners or members of the course team can answer, and interact with other learners in the course.

Where do I find course discussions?

In your course, click the Discussions tab.

Tab Layout

On the left side of the Discussions page, the navigation pane displays four tabs to help you use the forum:

  • My posts tab shows posts you’ve made.
  • All Posts tab shows all posts in the course.
  • Topics tab shows the hierarchy of discussion topics set by the course team.
  • Learners tab shows a list of learners who have contributed to this course discussion forum.
What are discussion topics?

Discussion topics are created by the course team and organize all posts in the course discussion. Discussion topics can be course-wide or content-specific.

How do I join my course’s discussions?

You can ask a question or start a conversation within a topic, or respond to someone else’s post or response. For more information, please review:

  • Adding posts in a discussion
  • Adding responses or comments in a discussion

Searching, sorting, and filtering discussions

To more easily find the posts that you are looking for, you can search, sort, or filter posts on the Discussion page.

You can filter the My Posts and All Posts tabs using the Filter icon across from All posts by recent activity. Learners cannot filter the Topics or Learners tabs.

You can search on every tab using the Search bar.

Search

Before you search you must select a tab.

  • Select My Posts to only search for a word or phrase within posts you’ve made in this course.
  • Select All Posts to search for all posts in the course.
  • Select Topics to search by topic name.
  • Select Learners to search for a specific learner’s posts by their username or part of their username.

Filter

Before you filter you must select a tab (My Posts or All Posts).

You can then filter by:

  • Post type (Discussions or Questions)
    • You can read more about post types in this article.
  • Status (Unread, following, unanswered)
    • You can read about following posts in this article.
  • Activity (Recent activity, most activity, most likes)
    • You can read about responding to posts and comments or read more about liking posts.

Payments

How can I pay?

You can pay for the certificate track in an edX course with a credit/debit card or PayPal.

To complete a payment, enroll in the Verified Track or click Upgrade to Verified next to the course on your edX dashboard or on the right side of the main page within the course.

We currently accept credit and debit card payments from the following networks:

  • American Express
  • Discover
  • Mastercard
  • Visa Unless otherwise provided for in these Terms, all fees will be charged in the available currency selected at checkout. Fees are non-refundable, except as provided by our Refund Policy or as required by law.

If you are having difficulty completing the payment please see Why is my payment failing?

We are not able to accept bank transfers or other payment methods.

What is the Security Code on the payment form?

The Security code on the form is the CVN or CVV number from the back panel of your credit card (or front on American Express cards)

​

Why is my payment failing?

edX accepts all credit or debit cards that have a Visa, MasterCard Discover or American Express logo and PayPal payments.

Most common reasons for failed payments

Declined by the issuing bank

Most payments failures are because they were not approved by your bank. If you receive a payment failed message, please contact your credit card provider for more information.

Mismatched address or verification number

If you notice the payment pending on your credit or debit card account, then it may have been rejected by the payment processor due to the anti-fraud billing address or card verification checks. This indicates that either the billing address that you entered on the form did not match the billing address you have set with your card company or that the short 3 or 4 digit card verification number (CVV or CVN) that you entered on the form from the back of your card does not match what the credit card company has for your active card.

In these cases, the payments are just in a pending state and have not been received by edX. Since the processor has not completed the transaction the funds will automatically return to your account within a few days. You are welcome to verify your information try to pay again.

Paypal

If you are receiving a receipt error for a PayPal order then likely there is an issue with your PayPal funding source. Please contact PayPal support for more information.

Will I receive a tax form?

Since edX is not considered an “accredited body”​ or an ​​“​eligible educational institution​”​ under US IRS guidelines, and courses are not part of a matriculated degree program, we are unable to issue US 1098-T forms or other tax forms for the verified track enrollment fees.

Proctortrack

Allowing Proctortrack on MacOS Catalina 10.15 and Above

If your machine is running on MacOS Catalina 10.15 and above, you will see the following message pop up after downloading the proctoring software for the first time:

Please click ‘OK’ to close the message, and then follow the below steps to allow Proctortrack on your device.

Solution 1:

  • Go to your ‘Downloads’ folder and locate the Proctortrack application.

  • Make sure you have dragged the Proctortrack to/Applications to complete the installation. Then right-click on the Proctortrack app and click ‘Open’. Solution 2:

  • Go to your ‘Downloads’ folder, locate the Proctortrack application.

  • Right click on the Proctortrack app and select ‘Open’. This will show you the same dialogue box, but with an option to ‘Open the file anyway’. Please click on this option. Solution 3:

You can also follow the below steps to run the Proctortrack app on Mac Catalina OS if you get an error “malicious software” error after launching it.

  • Go to System Preferences and then open Security and Privacy, then click on the ‘General’ tab.
  • Then click on “Open Anyway”. In order for Proctortrack monitor and record your test session without any issues, please make sure that the below settings are enabled.

Go to System Preference Security and Privacy -> Privacy tab Select the Proctortrack check box for following options:

  1. Screen Recording

  2. Camera

  3. Microphone

  4. Full Disk Access

  5. Accessibility

  6. Input Monitoring(Not mandatory)

Checking Your Onboarding Status and Resetting Your Onboarding Exam

Once your onboarding exam is reviewed, you will receive an email alerting you of your onboarding status. You can also look at your course’s main page to see your onboarding status in that course.

Please note that if you were approved for onboarding in another edX.org course within the past 12 months, you are approved for onboarding in all courses on edX.

If you did not pass your onboarding attempt, you can reset your onboarding attempt yourself by going back to the Onboarding section of the course and clicking on ‘restart my exam’. This will allow you to be immediately reset and try again. Please make sure to restart your computer before re-attempting onboarding. You will usually receive your new onboarding results within 2 days.

Completing a Proctortrack Room Scan

Most Proctortrack exams require a room scan. During the ‘Room Scan’ step of your exam, you will be asked to take a video scan of the room in which you are taking the proctored exam. Please make sure to show your entire desk area.

  1. Click the ‘Scan’ button to begin the room scan:

  2. After clicking ‘Scan’ you will see a timer in the center of that bottom of the page, as well as a ‘scanning’ box on the right that signifies that you are currently performing the room scan. Please note that the room scan video has a time limit of 4 minutes. If you make multiple room scan attempts they will all be counted towards the total time limit. After 4 cumulative minutes, your room scan will be automatically submitted. Complete this scan by panning the webcam or laptop around your desk. Be sure to move the webcam or laptop slowly to clearly capture the entirety of your desk area.

  3. After completing your room scan, please click the blue ‘Stop’ button on the bottom left:

  4. You will now see the recording of your room scan. Please click ‘Yes’ to confirm that you are satisfied with your room scan:

  5. You are now ready to take the exam. You will be automatically directed to the ‘Set Up and start your proctored exam’ page. You will see a box in the bottom right corner signifying that you are being recorded. Please click ‘Start Exam’:

You will now see a page reminding you of proctoring rules and tips. Please click ‘Start My Exam’ to proceed to your proctored exam:

You can review three different room scan techniques here.

Proctortrack ‘Waiting for the system check to finish’ Error Message

If you experience a persistent ‘Waiting for the system check to finish’ message when attempting a Proctortrack onboarding or graded exam, please delete the text in the URL that occurs after this pattern, i.e ” session/sdf2309u059uwednksjii78y/“. The value after ’‘/session/’ is alway unique. The one mentioned here is just an example. Next, add #/auto-connect to the URL of the page, and click to refresh the browser.

If you are still stuck, please reach out to edX Support at info@edX.org.

Proctortrack Error Message

System Error

The following error message indicates that you have been removed from your proctored exam session.

A system error has occurred with your proctored exam. Please reach out to your course team for assistance, and return to the exam once you receive further instructions.

If you encounter the following proctored exam error message while taking your Proctortrack exam, please reach out to you course team via email. In your email please state the name of the course and exam, as well as any relevant details about your proctoring experience (your computer froze, the error message occurred when you attempted to end your exam, etc).

Sometimes when the error occurs, you grade has still been saved properly. The course team will evaluate your case and determine if your attempt is valid and acceptable, or if you will need a reset to re-take your exam.

Something has gone wrong…

The following error message indicates that your exam attempt wasn’t able to connect with Proctortrack.

Something has gone wrong starting your exam. Please double-check that the application is running. If the issue persists, please reach out to edX Support in a new tab for assistance, and return to the exam once you receive further instructions

To prevent this error message from occurring, we strongly recommend the following before trying again. Adjusting DNS settings seems to resolve the error in most cases.

Adjust your DNS Settings

FOR MAC:-

-Open Network Preferences

-You can see the option for DNS

-Please select and click on the ‘plus’ symbol

-And add the 8.8.8.8

-Again, click on “” + “” and add 8.8.4.4

-Click Ok and apply the changes

-Now please go back and reload the app

OR WINDOWS:-

-Open the network and internet settings

-Click on the change adaptor option

-If you’re using a wired connection, right-click on ethernet and go to preferences. Otherwise select wifi and preferences.

-Select internet protocol version 4 (IPV4) and click on properties

-Select DNS server addresses radio button and enters the below DNS address

-Preferred DNS server 8.8.8.8

-Alternate DNS server 8.8.4.4

Here are some other steps we recommend trying:

-Restart your computer before beginning the exam

-Use a wired connection instead of a wireless connection, if possible

-Use Google Chrome or Firefox as your internet browser

-Follow these instructions for properly submitting your exam

If you continue to see the error message, please reach out to info@edx.org and use Proctoring in the subject line.

Proctortrack System Requirements

Proctortrack has the following system requirements:

1: Operating System-> MAC: macOS 10.13 or higher, PC: Windows 7, 8 or higher, Chromebook: version 80 or higher.

2: Processor/RAM-> MAC: Intel / AMD Processor, 4 GB RAM, PC: Dual-core 2.4 Ghz CPU, 4 GB RAM or better

3: Browser-> Mozilla Firefox v20.0 or Higher Google Chrome v25.0 or higher edX recommends Google Chrome for taking exams with Proctortrack

4: Plug Ins-> Javascript Enabled & Third Party Cookies Enabled

5: Web-Cam Resolution-> 800x600 or better (and a working Microphone)

6: Internet Connection-> Cable Modem, DSL or better (300 kbps download, 250 kbps upload)

Note: Proctortrack does not support Linux, Virtual Machines, tablets (including iPad, Surface, and others), and computers or networks with firewall restrictions.

Taking a Graded Proctortrack Exam

  • Before you take the exam
  • Using Proctortrack for the first time?
  • Have you used Proctortrack version 1 before? Follow these instructions as there are updates to the Proctortrack software.

Before you take the exam

Taking a Proctortrack Exam in Mainland China

Some of our learners located in mainland China have reported issues accessing Proctortrack with our site. They have found that following these instructions can help:

If you are getting the “Connection unsuccessful due to DNS issue” message in Proctortrack, then follow the instructions below related to resolving DNS:

  • Delete the already downloaded version and download and run the latest version
  • Try changing the DNS to 8.8.8.8 and 8.8.4.4
  • Try changing the internet connection, e.g. use the mobile hotspot if possible If you are still having trouble, please try changing the hosts file (for Windows, add an entry for “app.verificient.com 127.0.0.1”)

If you are not getting the DNS message error and still Proctortrack is unable to connect to the browser, then follow the steps below related to allowing app.verificient.com / local traffic in your proxy settings:

Make sure you are using the latest version of Proctortrack app (since the app is not able to connect and auto-update, best is to delete the current version and download afresh)

  • Check your proxy settings and exclude the local traffic and traffic related to app.verificient.com from proxy
    • Windows and macOS screenshots attached below in case of manual proxy in use
    • Please revert the network settings changes after your exam

Taking a Proctortrack Onboarding Exam

The Proctortrack Onboarding exam establishes your identity with Proctortrack and helps you make sure the Proctortrack exam software works on your device. Once you have approved onboarding status in any edX course, it’s valid towards all edX courses that use Proctortrack exams.

Please note that if you were approved for onboarding in another edX.org course within the past 12 months, you are approved for onboarding in all courses on edX.

Once you click into the onboarding section of the course you will see the following. Please click ‘continue to onboarding’:

​​

Next, you will see the following page. Please click ‘Start System Check’:

​​

You will now see a new, Proctortrack window:

​​

Please read this message, and then click the blue ‘X’ to close the pop up window. You will then be led through downloading the Proctortrack software, completing a system check, and taking photos of your face and ID photo.

During the face scan please make sure your face is filling the frame before clicking ‘Scan’. After that, follow the on-screen prompts.

If dissatisfied with the scans, click “No” to take the scan again. Otherwise, click “Yes” to proceed.

Next please read through the instructions for a valid photo ID scan and click “Next”.

Hold a photo ID card (such as a license, passport, or school ID) up to the camera and click “Scan”. Please make sure that your ID is clear and legible. Proctortrack must be able to clearly read the name on your ID in order for your onboarding exam to pass.

Click “No” if you are not satisfied with the scan. You can rescan your ID card as many times as needed or click “Yes” to proceed.

You will then be directed from the PT app back to edX. You will see a blue frame around your screen with a ‘You are being monitored’ message. At this point you will see the following edX page again. Please click ‘Start Exam’:

Next, click ‘Start my exam’:

You can then answer the questions in the onboarding exam.

It may take up to two days for Proctortrack to review your onboarding exam, so we recommend taking onboarding as early as possible.

Once your onboarding exam is reviewed, you will receive an email alerting you of your onboarding status. You can also look at your course’s main page to see your onboarding status in that course.

Purchasing Academic Credit

Completing the credit process For ASU or Charter Oak courses

After you have purchased academic credit on edx.org, you must complete the credit process at the credit-issuing organization. For example, you must contact the issuing college or university to receive its official notice of academic credit, such as a transcript.

To complete the credit process

  • Immediately after you purchase credit on edx.org, select Complete Credit on the payment confirmation page to go to the organization’s website.
  • At any time after purchasing credit on edx.org, select Get Credit from the course card on your edX dashboard to go to the organization’s website. Note: After the organization has evaluated your information, a message on your dashboard indicates whether the organization has granted you academic credit for the course and provides a link to the organization. You also receive an email that indicates whether you have received academic credit for the course.

For answers to questions about receiving academic credit for the course, you can directly contact the organization that issues the credit.

How do I transfer the credits that I earn through ASU?

After you pass a credit eligible ASU course and meet the requirements for credit, you have up to one year to purchase academic credit on edx.org.

  • On your dashboard, select Purchase Credit next to the course that you want.
  • On the Checkout page, verify that the organization that you want to purchase credit from appears, and enter your payment information.
  • Follow the instructions to complete the payment process. When the payment process is complete, a confirmation page opens, and you receive an email message.
  • On the payment confirmation page, select Complete Credit to go to the credit-issuing organization’s website to complete the credit process. Note: After you purchase academic credit, you must contact the issuing college or university to complete the credit process. This process usually includes receiving the official notice of academic credit, such as a transcript.

How long after a course can I pay for the credit through ASU?

In ASU credit eligible courses you will be able to purchase credit for one year after you become eligible for credit in the course. The date of eligibility will be earlier than the course end date or the date your certificate was issued.

You can find the eligibility requirements in a course offering credit under the progress graph on the progress tab.

Purchasing academic credit through ASU or Charter Oak

After you pass an ASU or Charter Oak “for credit” course and meet the requirements for credit, you have up to one year to purchase academic credit on edx.org.

After you purchase academic credit, you must complete the credit process at the credit-issuing college or university.

To purchase academic credit

  • On your dashboard, select Purchase Credit next to the course that you want.
  • On the Checkout page, verify that the organization that you want to purchase credit from appears, and follow the instructions to complete the payment process. When the payment process is complete, a confirmation page opens, and you receive an email message.
  • On the payment confirmation page, select Complete Credit to go to the credit-issuing organization’s website to complete the credit process. Note: After you purchase academic credit, you must contact the issuing college or university to complete the credit process. This process usually includes receiving the official notice of academic credit, such as a transcript.

Will my university accept my edX coursework for credit?

Most edX open courses do not directly award academic credit. Each educational institution makes its own decision regarding credit. Check with your university for its policy.

Please note: edX open courses don’t currently offer transcripts or proof of registration for the purpose of obtaining credit.

We have select credit-eligible courses and now MicroBachelors programs which award credit on completion. You can read more about MicroBachelors credit here.

Here are our current standalone open credit-eligible course:

  • Introduction to Computer Science and Programming Using Python (Charter Oak State College Credit, up to 3 credit hours) You can read more about the process to purchase credit for these courses here: Completing the credit process For ASU or Charter Oak courses

We also offer full Online Masters programs.

Receive and Share edX Certificates

How are edX certificates delivered?

edX certificates are only provided in a soft copy form electronically and we do not provide any physical certificate in a hard copy form.

Once your certificate becomes available, you can share and print it as well according to your choice.

If you would like to download an electronic copy of certificate in your computer in a PDF form, please visit: How do I download my certificate?

How can I add my certificate to my LinkedIn profile?

To add your edX certificate directly to your LinkedIn profile

  • Log in to your LinkedIn account, then go to your profile.
  • Scroll down to Licenses & Certifications.
  • Click the plus sign (Add a new certification button).
  • In Name, enter the name of the course or program.
  • In Issuing Organization, enter “edX”.
  • (Optional) In Issue Date, enter the time period during which you earned the certification.
  • (Optional) Select the checkbox This certification does not expire.
  • (Optional) Add the Credential ID from the bottom of your certificate.
  • In Certification URL, enter the URL for your certificate.

How do I download my certificate?

You can save your certificate on your computer by downloading it as a PDF through your browser. If you’ve earned a certificate, you can find it on your dashboard, your course’s Progress page and your edX profile.

Saving your certificate to PDF in Chrome or Firefox

  1. Choose File > Print.

  2. Choose Save to PDF as the Destination.

  3. Click Save and choose a name and location to save the PDF.

Saving your certificate to PDF in Edge Chromium

  1. Click the Settings and more (three-dotted) button from the top-right.

  2. Select the Print option (or CTRL + P shortcut)

  3. Choose Save as PDF from the Printer option.

  4. Click Save and choose a name and location to save the PDF.

Saving your certificate to PDF in Safari

  1. Choose File > Export to PDF

  2. Type the name for your file and optionally select the location of the file under Where.

  3. Click Save.

Where can I find my certificate?

Important: To receive the certificate, you must be enrolled in the verified track of the course and achieve a passing score but it is necessary to complete it before the course ends. Your certificate availability depends on whether the course is self-paced or instructor-paced.

Certificates in Self-Paced Courses:

If your course is self-paced then your certificate will be available on your course homepage and dashboard as soon as you reach the required passing grade in your course.

Certificates in Instructor-Paced Courses:

If your course is instructor-paced then your certificate will be available on your course homepage and your dashboard after the course ends. The date of certificate availability in an instructor-paced course is mentioned at the right side of your course homepage under the Important Dates section.

Solve a Sign in Problem

What if I am having login problems or forgot my password?

If you are unable to login or see an Email or password is incorrect message, make sure that the email address you entered is the one that you registered for your edX account.

If you continue to see this message, you can select Forgot password and enter the email address you used to sign up with edX to receive a password reset email. You’ll receive a password reset email from no-reply@registration.edx.org

Common Login Problems

If you entered your email address under Password assistance on the ‘Need Help Logging in?’ form but have not received an email message with a link to reset your password, check for these common problems:

  • Did you enter the same email address that you used to register your edX account? Reset messages are sent only to registered email addresses.
  • If you received a license activation email you must also have a matching edX account with the same email address. You can change the email address on an existing edX account.
  • Is your email address entered correctly? Check for errors and try again.
  • Did you check your spam message folders? Check your spam folder for a message from no-reply@registration.edx.org
  • If the message is not there, try adding no-reply@registration.edx.org
  • to your contacts or your approved senders list, or contact your and then reset your password again
  • Did you delete your account? It will not be possible to regain access to a deleted account.
  • Did you complete the account registration process for edx.org? To check, try registering again with the same email address. If the account does exist, a message will indicate that you entered a duplicate email address.

Other Error Messages

If you see a different error when trying to sign in, check for these common problems:

  • Make sure that you are using a current version of Firefox or Chrome.
  • Make sure that you have cookies and JavaScript enabled.
  • Try clearing your browser’s cache.
  • 404 error - attempting to activate a license assigned to an edX account associated with another email address. If you continue to have difficulty you may reach edX support by sending an email to info@edx.org.

What if I did not receive a password reset message?

If you entered your email under password assistance but have not received an email message with a link to reset your password, check for these common problems:

  • Did you enter the same email address that you used to register your edX account? Reset messages are sent only to registered email addresses.
  • If you are trying to login (after receiving a license activation email), be aware that creating or having an edX account with matching email address is necessary. You can change your email on your edX account if needed.
  • Did you check your spam message folders? Check your spam folder for a message from no-reply@registration.edx.org
  • If the message is not there, try adding no-reply@registration.edx.org to your contacts or your approved senders list, and then reset your password again.
  • Did you previously delete your account connected to this email? Deleted accounts cannot be accessed and you will not receive the reset message.
  • Did you complete the account registration process for edx.org? To check, try registering again with the same email address. If the account does exist (or was previously deleted), a message will appear “This email is already associated with an existing or previous edX account” If you still have difficulty, please contact edX support at info@edx.org.

Why am I getting a message ‘Too Many Failed Login Attempts’ in Firefox?

If you’re trying to log into edX.org but you encounter a message, “We couldn’t sign you in. Too many failed login attempts. Try again later,” this may be due to a cookie issue.

If you’re using the Firefox browser, you can take these steps to address the issue:

  • Try logging in using a different browser such as Chrome. You can review all browsers we support here.
  • (Advanced) Adjust your Firefox cookie preferences.
    • Open the Configuration Editor in Firefox.
    • Select Show All.
    • Type network.cookie.cookieBehavior in the search.
    • Modify your preference for network.cookie.cookieBehavior to 0 or 5. (Setting to 0 accepts all cookies. Setting to 5 rejects (known) trackers and partitions third-party storage.)

Why haven’t I received my confirmation email?

You may not receive the confirmation email for the following reasons:

  1. There was a typo in the email address when you registered your edX account. (For example, maybe you typed email@gmail.co instead of email@gmail.com)

  2. You did not successfully register on edX.org, or you registered on a different website.

  3. The confirmation email is in your spam folder.

  4. Your email provider has blocked automated messages.

What can be done to confirm your email:

  • Check your spam or junk mail folder for an email from no-reply@registration.edx.org
  • Add no-reply@registration.edx.org to your email contacts or approved senders list.
  • From the edx.org log in page, click Forgot my password. Clicking the link in the password reset message will confirm your email. (You don’t have to reset your password.)

Why is my account locked?

For account security, your edX account is temporarily locked if someone enters the wrong password several times. You’ll encounter a message ‘To protect your account, it’s been temporarily locked. Try again in 30 minutes.’

To regain access after a lockout:

  1. Wait 1 hour

  2. Reset your password using the Forgot password link.

  3. Enter your registered email address. An email will then be sent with a link to create a new password.

  4. Click the password reset link in the password reset message and type, confirm, and submit the new password.

If you are repeatedly locked out, we recommend:

  • Double check that you have submitted the password change
  • Clear your browser of any saved passwords, or use an incognito browser, before trying again If you continue to have difficulty you may reach edX support by sending an email to info@edx.org.

Taking Timed and Proctored Exams

Guide: Get Ready for a Proctored Exam

Make sure to prepare for proctored exams multiple days before the exam due date. Choose a computer that meets the system requirements. Please adhere to the rules below when you choose your testing environment.

Rules about your test environment

  • No other person is allowed to enter the room while you are taking the proctored exam.
  • The lighting in the room must be bright enough to be considered “daylight” quality. Overhead lighting is preferred. If overhead lighting is not available, the source of light must not be behind you.
  • You must sit at a clean desk or table.
  • The desk or walls around you must not have any writing.
  • The room must be as quiet as possible. Sounds such as music or television are not permitted.
  • The following items must not be on your desk or used during your proctored exam, unless posted rules for the exam specifically permit these materials:
    • Books
    • Paper
    • Pens
    • Calculators
    • Textbooks
    • Notebooks
    • Phones More details about your test-taking environment will be provided within the course materials. See the video below for how to best prepare.

How do I take a timed exam?

A timed exam is a special assessment that you must complete within a limited amount of time. The time limit is fixed by the course team, so it can be different in two different courses.

When you start taking a timed exam, a timer on the course page counts down the amount of time that you have remaining to complete the exam. You cannot pause or reset the timer. Navigating away from or closing the exam will not stop the timer. When time runs out, your exam is automatically submitted.

The time limit for an exam applies to all of the tabs (units) within the exam. You should be sure to review each tab of the exam before you submit, or before the timer runs out.

When you review the outline on the course homepage, a timed exam is indicated with the text ‘Timed Exam’ below the subsection that contains the timed exam.

When you navigate to a subsection that features a timed exam, a message appears “Subsection is a Timed Exam” with an indication of the length of time.

Taking a timed exam

  • Open the timed exam page in the course.
  • Find the length of the time limit on the exam page. Make sure that you have enough time available to complete the exam.
  • Note: You will not be able to pause or restart the exam after you begin.
  • Select I am ready to start this timed exam.
  • Once you click I am ready to start this timed exam, the exam timer cannot be stopped. Note: Taking multiple timed exams at once is not recommended. Only the most-recently-started exam’s timer will display, and you must end that exam in order to see other timers.
  • Complete the problems in each unit of the exam. Select Submit to score the problems in the unit. If you do not select Submit, your responses will not be scored.
  • After you have completed and checked all the units in the exam, select End My Exam at the top of the course page. If you run out of time, the exam will end automatically.
  • After you click End my Exam, you will be asked to confirm that you want to submit. If you are certain you wish to end the exam session, click Yes, submit my timed exam. This will end your exam. If you need more time or clicked End My Exam accidentally, click No, I’d like to continue working. Note: You must select Submit for each of the problems within the exam. If you submit an exam without selecting Submit on any problem, it won’t be graded.

Hiding the exam timer

By default, in timed exams you see a timer that shows the amount of time remaining. You can hide or show the timer.

  • To hide the timer, select the eye icon that is located next to the timer.
  • To show the timer, select the eye icon again.

Requesting additional time

To accommodate specific needs, course teams and instructors can increase the time allowed to complete an exam. Contact your course team to get information on whether extra time may be available for your exam.

Note: You must request additional time before you begin a timed exam.

How do proctored exams work?

Proctored exams are timed exams that you take while proctoring software monitors your computer’s desktop, webcam video and audio. The data recorded by the proctoring software is transferred to a proctoring service for review. Proctored exams may or may not be required for your course and enrollment track.

If you’re required to take a proctored exam for your course:

  • You cannot take proctored exams using the edX mobile app.
  • You’ll need to install the proctoring software on your computer when you take the proctored exam.
  • At the start of the proctored exam, you must verify your identity by taking a webcam photo of your face, your photo ID, and anything else the software needs to verify the exam environment.
  • While you take the exam, you must follow edX’s proctoring rules and requirements. If your course exam has different rules, instructions will be posted in your course.
  • While you take the exam, the proctoring software monitors your computer, including any software that is running, and streams the exam data to the proctoring software via the cloud. The software also records video and audio from your webcam. After you end your exam, the exam session is reviewed by the proctoring service. You’ll usually receive results within 5 days of submitting the exam, however reviews may take longer if there is heavier than usual volume or if your exam is marked for the course team to review.

To be eligible for course credit (where applicable), you must pass the exam and also receive a Satisfactory result for the proctoring review.

Proctoring Software:

Proctoring services on edX are supplied by either of the two 3rd party vendors: Proctortrack or Software Secure. Please read your course material carefully to identify which proctoring software the course uses. Each course can only use one proctoring software for all of its proctored exams. If you need help identifying proctoring software, please contact your course administrators or edX Support.

Computer System Requirements

You must have a working webcam, and we recommend 1GB of free space on your machine.

View the detailed System Requirements for Software Secure proctoring software.

View the detailed System Requirements for Proctortrack proctoring software.

Getting Ready

It is strongly recommended to follow the instructions on Guide: Get Ready for a Proctored Exam to start your proctoring preparation today.

Proctored exam rules and requirements

Computer and browser requirements

Note: You are responsible for making sure that the proctoring software works with your system before you take a proctored exam.

  • View the detailed System Requirements for Software Secure proctoring software.
  • View the detailed System Requirements for Proctortrack proctoring software.

Learner rules

If you violate the online proctoring rules and receive an Unsatisfactory status, you automatically receive a score of 0 for the exam and may lose any certificate you earned.

The following proctored exam rules are the basic rules for “closed book” exams. Please check your course for any exceptional rules, such as allowing a calculator or note paper. Any additional rules will be visible along with the exam instructions before you start the exam.

Test environment rules

Please review Guide: Get Ready for a Proctored Exam for rules about your testing environment.

The computer you use to take the test

  • The computer you are using to take the exam must not have more than one display or monitor. For example, if you usually use a laptop with a monitor connected, disconnect your monitor and use only the laptop screen.
  • You must close all other programs or windows on your testing computer before you begin the exam.
  • You must not use the following tools:
    • Programs such as Excel, Word, or PowerPoint
    • Communication programs such as Skype
    • Any website page other than the exam window in your course, including other content on edX.org

Your behavior

  • You must verify your identity using a photo ID that has your name and photo on the same side. The name must match the full name you use in your edx.org account. You can change your name in your edX account.
  • You must dress as though you were in a public setting.
  • You must not communicate with any other person by any means.
  • You must not use a phone for any reason.
  • You must not leave the room during the exam for any reason.

Additional time or special allowances

If you have a disability and need additional time or specific accommodations to take the exam, consult your organization’s disability resources or the course team before you make any choices on the exam page. The course team must approve your request and make any adjustments before you start your exam.

Consequences of Rule Violations

If you have questions about your proctoring exam status, click the Help button at the bottom right of this page to contact edX Support, or consult your course team.

Upgrade to the Verified Track

Can I upgrade to the verified track after the course ends?

After a course ends and becomes archived, it’s no longer possible to upgrade to the verified track. You can find the end date posted under Important Dates on the right side of the course homepage.

How do I know if I’m enrolled in the verified track?

If you’ve made the payment to upgrade to the verified track, there are a few ways to confirm your enrollment track in your course.

Verified track courses on the dashboard

If you’re enrolled in the course in the verified track, the course’s image thumbnail will feature a green checkmark banner with the word Verified.

For screen readers, below the course title it will read ‘You’re enrolled as a verified student.’

Check your Order History

When you upgrade to the verified track, an entry is made in your Order History. You can view thepayment confirmation in your account settings.

To View Your Order Details

  • Select the arrow next to your username in the upper right of edx.org.
  • Select Order History.
  • Select View Order Details to view the confirmation page of an order.

Check the Progress page

On the new Progress page, in the free audit track graded assignments may show as locked with the message ‘You have limited access to graded assignments as part of the audit track in this course’.

If you’re enrolled in the paid verified track, all assignments will be unlocked.

Audit track courses on the dashboard

For audit track courses, there will be no green banner over the course’s thumbnail image, and below the course will be a prompt encouraging you to Upgrade if the upgrade deadline hasn’t passed.

If the upgrade deadline has passed, there will be no button to Upgrade.

How do I pay and upgrade to access assignments?

Click the Upgrade button under the course on your edX dashboard.

You can also find the Upgrade button on the right side of the course homepage inside of the course to access the checkout page.

Note: If these buttons aren’t available, then the upgrade deadline of your course has already passed or this course does not offer certificates.

Unless otherwise provided for in these Terms, all fees will be charged in the available currency selected at checkout. Fees are non-refundable, except as provided by our Refund Policy or as required by law.

Can I start for free and pay later?

What is the deadline to upgrade to a verified certificate?

If the upgrade deadline has not passed, you can find the upgrade deadline on the right side of the course homepage under Important Dates.

If this date is not posted on the course homepage, the deadline has passed and it is no longer possible to upgrade.

Most courses allow upgrading until 10 days before the end of the course, but some instructor-paced courses may set this deadline sooner.

To upgrade to a verified certificate

Click on the Upgrade button under the course on your dashboard or on the right side of the course tab.

Where’s the course homepage?

To navigate to the course homepage from your dashboard, click the title of the course.

To navigate to the course homepage from any page within the course, click the Course tab in the course’s navigation menu.

Why is there no option to upgrade?

The option to upgrade will not appear if the deadline has passed or if the course has ended and been archived. A select few edX open courses are audit-only.

To be eligible to receive a certificate, a learner must pay and upgrade to the verified track before the course’s upgrade deadline. The upgrade deadline is fixed by the course team offering the course and it cannot be extended.

To try for a certificate you may instead enroll again in a future session. Most edX courses repeat in new sessions, so we encourage you to check back in the course catalog.

Using the edX Mobile App

Downloading videos in the edX App

To download videos in the edX app, you must be connected to the internet. After you download a video, you can watch it at any time.

In your course, navigate to the video or to the section of the course that contains the video you want to download. You can find videos within the course content under the outline icon in the upper left, or see just the videos under the video icon:

Click the download icon for an individual video to download a single video, or the download icon for a subsection to download all the videos in that subsection. In a subsection, the number to the right of a subsection name indicates the number of videos in that subsection.

You may also download all videos in a course in one click. Just slide this toggle at the top of the videos tab:

When a download completes the icon changes to a check mark.

Change Your Download Settings

By default, the edX app is set so that it only downloads content, including videos, if you are on a Wi-Fi network. If you change this setting, you might exceed the data allowance for your cellular plan.

  • From the main menu, select Account.
  • Select Settings.
  • Change the Wi-Fi only download setting.

Delete Downloaded Videos

You can delete downloaded videos one by one or, if you have downloaded all the videos in a course subsection, you can delete all of a subsection’s videos at once.

On Android:

  • In your course, navigate to the downloaded video or to the subsection containing the videos you want to delete.
  • You can only delete all the videos in a subsection if a checkmark icon appears for the subsection, indicating that you have downloaded all of the videos in that subsection.
  • Long press a downloaded video or a subsection of downloaded videos.
  • The screen title changes to Delete Videos, and a trash can icon appears in the header bar.
  • Select the trash can icon to delete the selected video or videos. On iOS:

For Apple iPhones and iPads to delete videos go to the videos section and swipe left on the video you would like to delete. The trash can delete icon will then appear on the right. Click this to delete.

Finding and enrolling in courses in the edX app

Finding Courses

To browse courses, select Discovery in the edX app.

When you find a course that interests you, select the course to view its About page. Under About this course you can find course details such as the course start date, the length of the course and effort required, whether a certificate is offered, the language that the course is taught in, and so on. Many About pages include an informational video about the course.

Please note that depending on the type of problems used in the course, you might not be able to complete all the course assignments or exams using the app.

Enroll in Courses

To enroll in a course, select Enroll from the course About page.

Note: For some courses, such as Professional Education courses where there is no audit track and you must pay the certificate fee upon enrolling, you can only enroll on the website.

When you initially enroll in a course, you join the audit track for a course. If the course includes a verified track and the upgrade deadline for the course has not passed, you can upgrade to the verified track via mobile browser to earn a certificate.

For more information about enrolling and about course tracks, please review What are the differences between audit (free) and verified (paid) courses?

Upgrade Courses

At this time, payment is not available through the edX mobile app. To upgrade your course, log into your edX.org account in a mobile browser.

Unenroll From a Course

To unenroll from a course, use a web browser to go to your dashboard on the edx.org website. Follow the directions in How do I unenroll from a course?

How do I post questions on the discussion board in the app?

You can read and contribute to course discussions using the edX app whenever you have an internet connection. In your course, select Discussion.

At the top level of course discussions, you find categories of posts or Topics, usually organized based on course structure. Within each category, you see lists of posts by learners. Each post has indicators to show the number of comments and responses the post has received. The number of unread comments for a post is shown until you have viewed a post.

You can select a topic to view the posts made within that topic or select All Posts to view all posts made.

Click Create a new post to create a new post while viewing all the posts within a Topic. Click Add a response to reply to a post you’re viewing.

When you view individual posts, you can add a comment or a response, or upvote a post. Posts by community TAs or course staff have a role indicator next to their username.

For more information about course discussions, see Participating in Course Discussions.

What can I do on the edX app?

The edX app is a companion to the edx.org website. The edX app is free and available for Android on the Google Play Store, and for the iPhone in the Apple App Store.

You can use the edX app to do the following things:

  • Register and create an account
  • Find and enroll in courses
  • Complete most course assignments
  • Watch course videos
  • Read course handouts and announcements
  • Participate in course discussions
  • Download course videos for watching when you do not have an internet connection Note: If your course includes timed exams or proctored exams, you cannot take them using the app.

What if I’m experiencing a Calendar Sync problem in the mobile app?

This is an app only feature. If you want to use the feature, you may download the app.

List of Scenarios

  • On Android, it’s expected that you will only see the events on the phone’s calendar. It is not associated with any accounts (like gmail, etc). You will receive notifications only in the device where the sync happened.
  • If you have 2 different Operating Systems (for example an Android phone and an iPad) where you use the edX app, you would sync calendars in both devices separately if you want to be notified of deadlines on both devices.
  • When you uninstall and reinstall the app, you will remove the old calendar manually.
  • On reinstall, you would grant the calendar permission and the calendars would automatically re-sync.
  • If you shift dates on the web, the calendar will re-sync when you navigate to the course dashboard page of a course.
  • After the first app install/update, if you do not see the calendar, close and reopen the app to refresh.
  • If a device is shared between multiple learners that are enrolled in the same course there may be date discrepancies. The learners may be presented with a resync alert (“Your course dates have been shifted and your course calendar is no longer up to date with your new schedule”) on navigating to the course dashboard.
  • If you enrolled in multiple runs of a course then on navigating to different runs of the course, you will be presented with a resync calendar alert (“Your course dates have been shifted and your course calendar is no longer up to date with your new schedule”) on navigating to the course dashboard.
  • please note that for the moment, you will see the calendar sync button only for self-paced courses

What if I’m experiencing a problem in the mobile app?

Most components of your courses will function in the app or a mobile browser, but some might not, depending on the content of the particular course. At times you might need to use a computer with an up-to-date version of Firefox or Chrome to complete tasks.

If you are not able to view videos, the course list or see an error message, here are some things which often help:

  • Log out from your account in the app from the menu, then log back in.
  • Restart your device.
  • Uninstall then reinstall the app. If you’re trying to upgrade your course, log into your edX account in a mobile browser. At this time payments aren’t available through the app.

What types of assignments can be done in the edX app?

You can use the edX app to do most of your assignments.

edX courses can use a variety of problem types, and some courses might include problem types that cannot be completed in the app. To complete problems that require complex actions (such as a drag-and-drop assignment), you must use a web browser.

You can also watch course videos on the mobile app, and can also download videos to your mobile device for watching when you do not have an internet connection.

Note: If your course includes timed exams or proctored exams, you cannot take them using the edX app.

Step by Step Guide: How to Redeem your MicroBachelors Certificate for credit

  • To redeem your MicroBachelors program certificate for credit please visit Thomas Edison State University.
  • On the landing page click “Start Your Application Now”

  1. On the following screen select “Create a New Student Profile”

  2. On the next screen fill out the required fields. *** Please be sure that your First Name, Last Name on your TESU application is identical and in the correct order as it appears on your edX account. This is necessary for TESU to confirm your identity and award your credits.

  3. At the button of the page be sure to create a password and click “Create a Student Account Profile”.

  • Once you’ve created your account go back to the sign in screen. You can get back there by using this link and clicking “start your application now”. Once here sign into your account.
  • On your accounts page click “Create a New Application” on the right side of the screen.
  • On the next screen select “Start a New Individual Learning Application”
  • On the following screen select “ I am using TESU’s individualized Learning Account program (edX or other course to be transcribed) Then click submit.
  1. Next you will need to sign into your edX account to send Thomas Edison your learner record, which demonstrates that you’ve successfully completed the program.

You must complete the MicroBachelors program before sending the learner record.

  • At the top of any page in the upper right, select Profile from the dropdown menu icon next to your username.
  • On the Profile tab, select View My Records.
  • On the My Learner Records page, find the completed MicroBachelors program record that you want to share and select View Program Record.
  • On the Record page for the program, select send Learner Record
  • In the Send to Credit Partner dialog box, select the credit-offering institution (Thomas Edison State University) to which you want to send your record, then select Send.
  1. After you select Send, the institution you shared your program record with can view the record for that program. Once Thomas Edison State University receives and verifies your learner record, your credit will be applied to a transcript, and a TESU transcript will be issued to you via mail free of charge for your first mailed transcript. Please allow for 5 business days for your credits to be processed.

Electronic transcripts can be accessed via the National Student Clearinghouse for a $15 fee. Additional mailed transcripts may require a fee payable to TESU.

For questions regarding your application status or transcript, please email registrar@tesu.edu.

What is a Professional Certificate program?

Professional Certificate programs are a series of career-oriented courses that teach critical skills in specific professional fields. Courses in a Professional Certificate program offer verified certificates. To earn a certificate for a Professional Certificate program, you must earn a verified certificate in each of the courses in the program.

Professional certificate programs have a “Professional Certificate program” indication in the course catalog on edx.org.

For more information, see Professional Certificate Programs on edx.org.

What is an XSeries program?

In addition to taking individual edX courses, you can enroll in a series of courses called a program. XSeries programs are created by world-renowned experts and top universities, and consist of groups of courses that explore a specific subject in depth.

You earn an XSeries certificate by passing each course in the series in the verified track. The XSeries certificate has the logo of the institution that provides the program.

You can read more about our XSeries programs on the XSeries page.
